- **Must NOT:** edit other crates, the root Cargo.toml, CI or docs outside `crates/sm-boolean/`; register layers; depend on sm-model in production code, or on sm-mesher or any L3+ crate, or on a W3a crate still in progress; iterate a `HashMap`/`HashSet` into output; use time or unseeded randomness; reduce floats across threads in non-fixed order; use `unwrap` on input paths; silently move geometry beyond tolerance; call an external tool (checkMesh) to judge validity; reconstruct faces/edges with `make_face`/`make_edge` to emulate `join_faces`/`join_edges` (use the stage-0 methods; if one returns `Unsupported`, stop with `blocked_on_api`). Write `DESIGN.md` (kernel stages, classification rules, tolerance policy, creation order) and a `W3_REPORT.md`.

#### registers
ops: model.unite, model.subtract, model.intersect, model.imprint, model.merge, model.webcut_plane, model.webcut_surface, model.webcut_cylinder (stretch: model.webcut_body, model.webcut_sheet, model.unmerge)
queries: model.merge_check
plugin: BooleanPlugin (id "boolean"), no layers
public library fns: boolean, imprint_bodies, merge, merge_check, webcut, locate_point (reusable for ray tracing / transport); stretch: imprint_curves

#### ACRs
None. Stage 0 supplies `TopologyEdit::join_edges` / `join_faces`, the topology fixtures and the sm-testkit boolean cases (decision 12). Anything else missing is returned as `blocked_on_api`.

#### depends
[]

#### baseline
Conceptually the same decomposition as OpenCascade's General Fuse (BOPAlgo: face/face intersection, edge splitting, face splitting, classification, solid building) and the ACIS/Cubit boolean + imprint + merge + webcut stack, with one shared fragment kernel and the smart-mesh cell complex as the result representation. It differs in three ways.
1. Results are non-manifold cell complexes with two-sided shared faces and Coincidence records (Cubit-style merge), not manifold B-reps. Merge/imprint/webcut are first-class, and conformal meshes need no post-pass.
2. Every result carries lineage (split/merged/generated), so colors, blocks, groups, decks and mesh intent follow the geometry. Every failure is a typed error naming entities with a hint.
3. Validity is judged by our own validators (`sm_topo_api::validate`, `validate_geometry`, `sm_model_api::validate`), not by a downstream tool.

The advantage over Cubit/ACIS is determinism (bit-identical 1 vs N threads, replayable ids), exact quadric handling through sm-geom closed forms, and `locate_point`/ray machinery reusable by transport and ray tracing.

#### risks
- Scope is the largest single crate in W3a. Mitigation: M0-M2 only are mandatory; M3 and M4 are an explicit stretch list; partial delivery is still useful because merge/imprint/planar webcut stand alone. Planar unite with regularize is the one M2 piece the milestone needs, so it is built first within M2.
- Robust classification: coincident (OnSame/OnOpposite) faces, rays through edges or vertices, tangent contacts. Mitigation: distance-to-boundary On test first, eight fixed ray directions, typed `Ambiguous` error with location instead of a wrong answer, final per-volume validity check with rollback (acceptance 15).
- Pole/seam handling on spheres, cones and cylinders (ChordAt, split_degenerate_edge, periodic pcurve unwrapping) is where split_face usually fails. Acceptance 5 and 6 target it; the axial sphere cut is stretch.
- Marched SSI (sm-geom) can miss components smaller than the seed grid and cannot continue through tangent contacts (documented in SsiOptions). Acceptance 9 is mandatory on `crossing_cylinders`, so keep the seed grid dense enough there.
- The private `cc.rs` duplicates what should live in sm-geom (promotion is W3b).
- Copy-on-write clone of the whole TopoStore per `topo_txn` makes a boolean O(model) per op. Acceptable for W3a.
- `keep=true` deep copies share curve/surface handles. Harmless for an append-only store but must be documented.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
1. Body structure after webcut/subtract: one body per resulting volume (Cubit; proposed, and the milestone's `positive`/`negative` roles assume it) versus pieces staying in the original body (as `sm_topo_api::fixtures::webcut_brick` does). Decision 12 does not rule on it. The proposal makes the canonical hash differ from the webcut_cube fixture, so acceptance compares structure, not hash.
2. Unite of bodies that do not touch: proposed `Invalid("bodies do not touch")`; the Cubit-like alternative is one compound body.
3. Role disjointness: `commit_topology` allows an id in at most one role, while decision 12 asks for `positive`/`negative` in addition to `bodies`/`volumes`/`cut_faces`. This entry makes `positive`/`negative` hold bodies and `bodies` hold the unsided remainder (empty for plane/surface/cylinder tools). Orchestrator to confirm, or to rule that `positive`/`negative` carry volumes and `volumes` is the remainder.
4. Webcut tool body default: retained and unchanged (proposed, only relevant to the stretch `webcut_body`).

#### escalation
Nothing is blocking. Items 1-3 of Open change op OUTPUTS and fixture comparisons, so they need a ruling before workers start. Integration owns installing BooleanPlugin in the standard distribution (sm-cli/sm-engine assembly), with the script templates taken from the op shapes above (`positive`/`negative` roles, `unite {bodies}`, `imprint {bodies}`, `merge {entities}`).

### W3-CAD. `sm-cadio` (L2, new): STEP read (AP203/214/242 B-rep) into sm-topo, content-addressed import op
- **Layer and deps (decision 12: sm-cadio is L2; decision 9/10 and the W2 dependency ruling: Deps lines bound internal crates only).** Internal: sm-core, sm-geom-api (features `serde`, `schemars`), sm-topo-api, sm-op-api, sm-layer-api. External: serde, schemars, serde_json, thiserror, sha2, base64, rayon, smallvec, exactly the set pinned in the stage-0 skeleton `Cargo.toml` (the orchestrator owns `Cargo.lock`; do not add external crates beyond the skeleton). Dev: sm-topo (production `TopoStore`; L1, on main), sm-testkit, proptest, criterion. No sm-model, sm-geom, any other W3a crate (built or being built), or OCCT/C dependency. Part 21 is tokenized and parsed by us. Not `ruststep`: we need byte offsets in errors, a depth cap and no EXPRESS code generation.
- **Stage-0 items this task consumes (by name, not ACRs; all exist before the worker starts).** `sm_topo_api::fixtures::{torus, cone, brick_with_void}` (and the testkit mirrors `sm_testkit::topo::{torus, cone, brick_with_void}`), used as the reference models for torus/apex/void tests. `sm_testkit::w3a` (the milestone part: `VOLUMES = [1200, 200, 600]`, `TOTAL_VOLUME = 2000`, and its STEP bytes). The OCC-written fixtures committed at 7910d84 in `crates/sm-testkit/data/step/`: `box_fillet1.step`, `cone_r2_h3.step`, `cylinder_r2_h5.step`, `loft_bspline.step`, `sphere_r3.step`, `torus_R5_r1.step`, `w3a_part.step` (all AP214 `AUTOMOTIVE_DESIGN`, `SI_UNIT(.MILLI.,.METRE.)`). The `sm-cadio` skeleton itself. If a named item is missing on main when the worker starts, stop with `blocked_on_api`; do not reimplement it.
- **Decision: how a pure op imports a file (binding; decision 12 "STEP import is content-addressed").** `apply` must be a pure function of `(params, state, services)`, and a path is neither replayable nor forkable. So the op never takes a path. Its param is `source: {"sha256": "<64 hex>"}` or `source: {"inline": "<base64>"}`. There is no `ApplyCtx::blob` and no contract change.
  - `inline` is for small files and agents, capped at `MAX_INLINE_BYTES = 8 MiB`. The bytes live in the op, so replay, fork and git are self-contained.
  - `sha256` is for real CAD files. Bytes come from the injected service `StepInputs(Arc<dyn InputStore>)` (read through `ApplyCtx::service`/`QueryCtx::service`, which exist). The bytes are re-hashed on read: a mismatch is `Invalid`, a missing blob is `Invalid("step.input_missing: input blob <sha> not found; stage it first")`, and both are deterministic.
  - Providing the service is NOT this task. sm-server injects `StepInputs(DirInputStore)` (and the memory store for `--memory` projects), and sm-cli/sm-lang/sm-mcp stage a path into `<project>/inputs/<sha256>.stp` and rewrite it to `{sha256}`; both are integration wiring (item D6) outside this crate. This task ships `DirInputStore`, `MemoryInputStore` and `stage_file(&DirInputStore, &Path) -> io::Result<InputRef>` as library pieces and tests them directly.
  - Inline and sha256 forms of the same file give the **same state hash**. The reader is versioned through `IMPL_VERSION`; the `StepInputs` service is unversioned.
- **Scope, mandatory (module layout, in priority order):**
  1. `part21/` tokenizer and parser. `pub fn parse(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<StepFile, StepError>`; `StepFile { header: Header /* FILE_DESCRIPTION, FILE_NAME, FILE_SCHEMA */, entities: BTreeMap<u64, Entity> }`; `enum Entity { Simple(Rec), Complex(Vec<Rec>) }`; `Rec { name: String /* upper-cased */, params: Vec<Param>, offset: usize }`; `enum Param { Null, Derived, Int(i64), Real(f64), Str(String), Enum(String), Ref(u64), List(Vec<Param>), Typed(String, Box<Param>) }`.
     - Handles `/* */` comments, multi-line records, forward references, complex entities `#5=(A() B(..));` (including OCC's spaced `#426 = ( LENGTH_UNIT() NAMED_UNIT(*) SI_UNIT(...) );`), `''` and `\X2\..\X0\` / `\X\` string escapes, reals such as `1.`, `-.5`, `1.E-3`; `\S\` is left raw. Real literals parse through `str::parse::<f64>` (correctly rounded).
     - Limits: list nesting at most 256 (so 100k nested parens return `TooDeep`, no stack overflow), input at most 2 GiB, every error carries `line:col`. Single DATA section only. Cost target: at least 40 MB/s on one thread, no per-token heap allocation beyond the `Param` tree (recorded, see acceptance 13).
  2. `decode`: `pub fn read_step(bytes: &[u8], opts: &ReadOptions) -> Result<BrepModel, StepError>`, pure and independent of any topology store. `BrepModel` is plain, ordered data (`Vec<Solid>`, each with shells, faces, loops and oriented edges, and geometry as `CurveDef`/`SurfaceDef`), every list in ascending STEP id order. Per-face geometry decode may run under rayon, collected by index.
     - Solids: `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP`, `BREP_WITH_VOIDS` (`ORIENTED_CLOSED_SHELL` allowed). `SHELL_BASED_SURFACE_MODEL` / `OPEN_SHELL` are skipped with info `step.sheets_skipped` (the `sheets: true` import is Stretch).
     - Topology: `VERTEX_POINT`, `EDGE_CURVE`, `ORIENTED_EDGE`, `EDGE_LOOP`, `FACE_BOUND`, `FACE_OUTER_BOUND`, `ADVANCED_FACE`, `FACE_SURFACE`, `CLOSED_SHELL`. (`VERTEX_LOOP` is Stretch S1.)
     - Curves: `LINE` (VECTOR magnitude ignored, our `Line` is unit speed), `CIRCLE`, `ELLIPSE`, `B_SPLINE_CURVE_WITH_KNOTS`, and the rational complex form (`RATIONAL_B_SPLINE_CURVE`). `SURFACE_CURVE` / `SEAM_CURVE` use `curve_3d` and ignore their pcurves (OCC files carry them on every edge, so this path is exercised by the OCC fixtures).
     - Surfaces: `PLANE`, `CYLINDRICAL_SURFACE`, `CONICAL_SURFACE` (semi-angle converted from the context's plane-angle unit), `SPHERICAL_SURFACE`, `TOROIDAL_SURFACE` (`major > minor` required), `B_SPLINE_SURFACE_WITH_KNOTS`, rational complex form. Multiplicity-plus-distinct-knot lists are expanded to our full knot vectors; the control net is `u`-major (`iu * nv + iv`), as in `NurbsSurface`.
     - Units: `SI_UNIT` with a prefix, `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT` (inch and others via `LENGTH_MEASURE_WITH_UNIT`), and `UNCERTAINTY_MEASURE_WITH_UNIT`. Units are per shape-representation context, so each solid gets its own scale.
     - Anything else geometric (`OFFSET_SURFACE`, `SURFACE_OF_REVOLUTION`, `DEGENERATE_TOROIDAL_SURFACE`, ...) gives `Unsupported { id, entity }`. PMI, tessellation, colors and names are counted and reported as info only.
  3. `edge_range(def: &CurveDef, p_start: Point3, p_end: Point3, same_sense: bool, closed: bool, tol: f64) -> Result<(Interval, Sense), StepError>`. Parameters come from `ParametricCurve::project`. A closed circle/ellipse edge gets `[t0, t0 + 2π]`. A closed B-spline edge needs the vertex at the curve start, otherwise `Unsupported`. If the vertex is farther than `max(weld_tol, 10 × file uncertainty)` from the curve end, the error is `GeometryMismatch { id, msg }`; a smaller gap is a warning.
  4. `weld`: vertices within `weld_tol` are merged (lowest STEP id wins, via a sorted-grid pass that never depends on hash order, O(n log n)). Duplicate `EDGE_CURVE`s with the same welded ends and 5 sampled curve points within tol are merged, recording the relative direction. Counts are reported as info `step.welded`. This is reader-side stitching only; cross-solid face merging is `model.merge`'s job (sm-boolean).
  5. `realize<E: TopologyEdit + ?Sized>(m: &BrepModel, topo: &mut E, o: &RealizeOptions) -> Result<Realized, StepError>`, called inside an open operation, generic so it runs on any host.
     - Creation order is fixed: vertices ascending by STEP id, then edges, then faces, then volumes and bodies (one body per solid). Each distinct STEP curve/surface entity gets one handle, added when its first user is created.
     - Mapping: `ADVANCED_FACE.same_sense` becomes `SurfaceCarrier.sense`; `ORIENTED_EDGE.orientation` XOR `FACE_BOUND.orientation` becomes the `CoedgeSpec` sense; `EDGE_CURVE.same_sense` becomes `CurveCarrier.sense`. Face uses are `Side::Left` (normals out of the material); void shells are `ShellKind::Void`.
     - Pole rule (seam encoding, as in hand-written fixtures and sphere `sphere_r3` variants with an explicit seam): in a loop, where consecutive coedges are the same edge traversed oppositely (a seam pair) and the vertex lies at a `Domain2.singularities` point of the face's surface (sphere pole, cone apex), insert a `DEGENERATE` closed edge with a `PCurve::Line2` pole pcurve. Its u-sweep is ±2π; the sign is the one that gives positive face area. Pcurves are set **only** for pole coedges (geometry defaults unwrap seams by projection).
     - Orientation self-check: realize each solid first into a scratch `sm_topo_api::CellComplex`. If the signed volume (`volume_moments`) is negative, flip that solid's loops and carrier senses before the real run and emit warning `step.inverted_solid`. Hard failures never leave partial state (`topo_txn` is atomic).
  6. `op.rs`: `ImportStep` (`model.import_step`) registered by `CadioPlugin` (`Plugin::id() == "cadio"`; ops and queries only, no layers; `CoreLayers` registers layers). The body is one `topo_txn` plus `commit_topology`; diagnostics are `ctx.warn/info` with codes `step.units`, `step.inverted_solid`, `step.welded`, `step.sheets_skipped`, `step.unsupported_entity`, `step.validate` (one per `sm_topo_api::validate` / `validate_geometry` issue, with the located cell id). Our validator decides validity: a `Severity::Error` issue found by `check: true` fails the op with `OpError::Invalid` listing the first 20 issues. sm-model does not register this op.
  7. `query.rs`: `cad.step_info {source, name?}` (pure): schema (AP203/214/242), sha256, detected length and angle unit, uncertainty, and per solid `{step_id, name, kind, shells, faces, edges, vertices, surface_kinds}`. It also lists unsupported entities and `assemblies: bool` (`MAPPED_ITEM`, `NEXT_ASSEMBLY_USAGE_OCCURRENCE` present).
  8. `inputs.rs`: `trait InputStore { fn get(&self, sha256: &str) -> Option<Arc<[u8]>>; }`, `MemoryInputStore` (`put(&self, &[u8]) -> InputRef`, `Mutex<BTreeMap>`), `DirInputStore`, `struct StepInputs(pub Arc<dyn InputStore>)`, `pub fn sha256_hex(&[u8]) -> String`, `stage_file`. This is the only file I/O in the crate.
  9. `StepError` (thiserror): `Syntax{line,col,msg}`, `Truncated`, `TooDeep`, `TooLarge`, `MissingEntity{id,referenced_by}`, `WrongType{id,expected,found}`, `BadParam{id,entity,index,msg}`, `Cycle{id}`, `Unsupported{id,entity}`, `Units(String)`, `GeometryMismatch{id,msg}`, `OpenShell{solid,issues}`, `Topology(TopoError)`, `Inline`. Each maps to `OpError::Invalid("step.<code>: ...")`.
- **Op args (exact):** `model.import_step { source: {sha256}|{inline}, name?: String, to_unit?: "um"|"mm"|"cm"|"m"|"in"|"ft", assume_unit?: LengthUnit /* only when the file declares none; default mm + warning */, weld_tol?: f64 /* target units; default model linear tol, must be <= linear_max */, solids?: [u32] /* 0-based ordinals in ascending STEP id; default all */, sheets?: bool = false /* accepted; has effect only once Stretch S4 lands, until then true gives Unsupported("step.sheets") */, check?: bool = true }`, `deny_unknown_fields`. `to_unit` absent means the file's unit is kept and reported (front ends fill it from `project.toml` units). The op never changes the model tolerance. Outputs (all `Many`): `bodies` (TopoBody), `volumes` (TopoVolume), `faces` (TopoFace), `edges` (TopoEdge), `vertices` (TopoVertex).
- **Fixtures.**
  - Hand-written ASCII, checked in at `crates/sm-cadio/tests/data/`: `brick_2x3x4.stp` (AP214, min corner (1,2,3)), `brick_inch.stp` (1x2x3 in, `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT`), `brick_ap203.stp` (`CONFIG_CONTROL_DESIGN`), `two_bricks.stp`, `brick_with_void.stp` (`BREP_WITH_VOIDS`, 10-cube minus centred 2-cube), `frustum_r2_r1_h3.stp`, `sphere_seam_r3.stp` (seam + two explicit pole vertices, no VERTEX_LOOP), `block_fillet_nurbs.stp` (10-cube, one vertical edge filleted r = 2; the fillet is a rational degree-2x1 `B_SPLINE_SURFACE_WITH_KNOTS` with weights 1, sqrt(2)/2, 1; its arc boundaries are rational B-spline curves).
  - Malformed, in `tests/data/bad/`: `not_step.txt`, `truncated_no_endsec.stp`, `unterminated_string.stp`, `dangling_ref.stp`, `cyclic_ref.stp`, `wrong_param_type.stp`, `unsupported_offset_surface.stp`, `zero_radius_cylinder.stp`, `no_units.stp`, `open_shell.stp` (one face removed), `deep_nesting.stp` (generated at test time).
  - Real-writer inputs are read from `sm_testkit` (`data/step/`, OCC output), not copied into this crate. Their expected values: `cylinder_r2_h5` r 2, h 5; `torus_R5_r1`; `cone_r2_h3` r 2, h 3 (apex, see S1); `sphere_r3` (OCC `VERTEX_LOOP` encoding, see S1); `box_fillet1` 10-cube with a vertical edge filleted r = 1 at corner (0,0) (cylindrical fillet); `loft_bspline` (circle r 2 at z = 0 lofted to ellipse 3 x 1.5 at z = 4 over a B-spline surface, 2 planes, 1 seam); `w3a_part` (3 solids, 19 planar faces).
- **Acceptance** (volume = `volume_moments(v).volume`, area = sum of `face_moments(f).area`, relative error <= 1e-9 unless noted; every imported state passes `sm_topo_api::validate` and `validate_geometry` with zero errors; every `op` case replays through `sm_op_api::Session` with `CoreLayers` + `CadioPlugin` + `StepInputs`):
  1. Parser: a unit test per lexical rule (comments, `''`, `\X2\00E9\X0\`, `1.`, `-.5`, `1.E-3` bit-equal to `str::parse`, complex entity incl. the OCC spaced form, forward ref, nested lists). `Syntax` errors carry exact line and col on every `bad/` file (assert both).
  2. Brick 2x3x4: V/E/F = 8/12/6, chi = 2, volume 24, area 52, bbox (1,2,3)-(3,5,7) bit-exact. `brick_ap203` and `brick_2x3x4` give the same topology hash modulo the schema string. For the brick, `canonical_hash(imported) == canonical_hash(sm_topo_api::fixtures::brick)` built in a fresh store at the same `OpId`; `CellComplex` and `TopoStore` give equal hashes.
  3. `brick_inch.stp` with `to_unit: "mm"`: volume 6 x 25.4^3 = 98322.384; without `to_unit`: volume 6 and the `step.units` info says inch.
  4. OCC cylinder r=2, h=5 (`cylinder_r2_h5.step`): V/E/F = 2/3/3, chi = 2, volume 20pi, area 28pi. Frustum (hand-written; r 2 -> 1, h 3): volume 7pi, lateral area 3pi sqrt(10), total 3pi sqrt(10) + 5pi.
  5. Hand-written seam sphere r=3: one face, 2 vertices, 1 seam plus 2 `DEGENERATE` edges, chi = 2, volume 36pi, area 36pi. This proves the pole rule.
  6. OCC torus R=5, r=1 (`torus_R5_r1.step`): V/E/F = 1/2/1, chi = 0, volume 10pi^2, area 20pi^2; canonical hash equals `sm_topo_api::fixtures::torus(5, 1)` at the same `OpId` up to the id/handle-order differences the test documents (fall back to numeric equality if handle order legitimately differs).
  7. NURBS: hand-written fillet block: 7 faces (6 planes... namely 5 planes plus 1 on `SurfaceDef::Nurbs`, plus the second end plane makes 6 planes + 1 NURBS = 7), volume 960 + 10pi = 991.4159265358979, area 552 + 12pi = 589.6991118430775, NURBS quadrature tolerance 1e-8 relative. OCC `box_fillet1`: 7 faces, volume 990 + 2.5pi = 997.853981633974, area 578 + 5.5pi = 595.27878..., 1e-9. OCC `loft_bspline`: volume converged (Gauss orders 20 vs 40 agree to 1e-10) and inside [16pi, 18pi] (section area runs from 4pi to 4.5pi); the converged value is pinned in the test at 1e-8.
  8. `brick_with_void`: one volume with 2 shells (`Outer`, `Void`), 12 faces, chi = 4, volume 992; hash/volume equal to `sm_topo_api::fixtures::brick_with_void`. `two_bricks`: 2 bodies, ids ascending in file order.
  9. **Real-writer import (the milestone part):** `sm_testkit::w3a` STEP (`w3a_part.step`): 3 bodies, 19 planar faces, volumes equal `w3a::VOLUMES` = [1200, 200, 600] to 1e-12 relative, total 2000, zero validator errors, no `step.unsupported_entity`; imported through the op in both `inline` and `sha256` forms with identical state hash.
  10. Op level (replay through `sm_op_api::Session`): `Delta.created` is the topology ids in ascending order, `outputs` match the declared roles. Inline and sha256 forms of one file give the same state hash. Undo/redo and `state_at_from_root` give no `Diverged`. A missing blob, a hash mismatch and an oversize inline each give `Invalid` and leave the state hash unchanged (atomicity). `solids: [1]` imports only the second solid of `two_bricks`.
  11. Every `bad/` fixture gives its designated `StepError` variant (table in the test), with the offending STEP id in the message; `open_shell.stp` gives `OpenShell` naming the open edge; none panics.
  12. Robustness: a proptest (>= 2000 cases, seeded from the case index) of byte mutations, truncations and line shuffles of every fixture (hand-written and OCC), plus random bytes, never panics and never allocates more than 20x the input size.
  13. Determinism (CLAUDE.md): two runs, and `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` against default, give identical `BrepModel`, identical state hash, via `sm_testkit::determinism::check`.
  14. `cad.step_info` on each fixture reports the exact solid, face and surface-kind counts above. Rustdoc on every public item; `DESIGN.md` lists supported entities, every `step.*` diagnostic code, the pole rule and the `IMPL_VERSION` policy.
  15. Performance (criterion, release, **record-only**, numbers written to `DESIGN.md`, not gated; import numbers depend on the deferred bulk commit): parse a generated 50 MB file (target <= 2 s); import 1000 disjoint bricks (target <= 3 s); weld 1e5 vertices (target <= 1 s).
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-15 are green; each behind its own test; unfinished items are reported, not half-landed):**
  - **S1. Pole encodings used by real writers.** OCC `sphere_r3.step` (a single face bounded by a `VERTEX_LOOP`) and `cone_r2_h3.step` (apex vertex at the end of the seam line, no degenerate edge): `VERTEX_LOOP` bounds, apex pole rule. Targets: sphere V=2, 1 seam + 2 `DEGENERATE`, chi 2, volume = area = 36pi; cone V = 4pi (r 2, h 3), lateral area = pi r slant = 2pi sqrt(13), validator clean, canonical hash vs `sm_topo_api::fixtures::cone(2, 3)` documented as for the torus. Highest-priority stretch item because OCC emits these encodings.
  - **S2. STEP writer and round trip** (decision 12: the writer is stretch; if it slips it is W3b). `pub fn write_step(view: &dyn TopologyView, bodies: Option<&[EntityId]>, o: &WriteOptions, w: &mut dyn Write) -> Result<WriteReport, StepError>` and `pub fn export_step(state: &State, ...)`. `WriteOptions { schema: Ap214 | Ap242, unit: LengthUnit /* default mm, no scaling */, timestamp: Option<String> /* default fixed 1970-01-01T00:00:00 */ }`. Output: header, minimal product structure, one `ADVANCED_BREP_SHAPE_REPRESENTATION`, one `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP`/`BREP_WITH_VOIDS` per volume; entity numbering ascending by model id; shortest round-trip reals with a mandatory `.`; pole coedges dropped (seam pair closes the loop); faces shared by two volumes written once per volume (`WriteReport.losses`); carriers Line, Circle, Ellipse, Nurbs (rational complex form) and Plane, Cylinder, Cone, Sphere, Torus, Nurbs, others give `Unsupported` naming the cell. Acceptance: for each hand-written and OCC fixture X: `T1 = write_step(X)`, `Y = read(T1)`, `T2 = write_step(Y)`; `T1 == T2` byte-identical; `canonical_hash(Y) == canonical_hash(read(T2))`; counts, volume and area within 1e-12 of X; the NURBS block is a fixed point. Not registered as an op; surfacing as a `session.export` format is later work.
  - **S3. Assemblies.** `MAPPED_ITEM` / `REPRESENTATION_MAP` and `ITEM_DEFINED_TRANSFORMATION` flattened with `Transform` applied through `CurveDef/SurfaceDef::transformed`. Until then a file with `assemblies: true` imports solids in local coordinates and emits warning `step.assembly_transforms_ignored` (this warning is mandatory).
  - **S4. Other.** `sheets: true` (`SHELL_BASED_SURFACE_MODEL` / `OPEN_SHELL`), `FACETED_BREP` with `POLY_LOOP`, `TRIMMED_CURVE`.
- **Deferred to W3b / later (not this task):** IGES, STL/OBJ (the ARCHITECTURE crate map groups them in sm-cadio; they are separate later tasks); colors, names, layers, PMI and GD&T to Model colors/groups (follow-up op `model.import_step_attributes`); `PCURVE` / `DEFINITIONAL_REPRESENTATION` import; surfaces of revolution, extrusion and offset; periodic B-splines; Part 21 edition 3 (multi-DATA, ANCHOR, signatures); `.stpz`; writing assemblies, colors and tessellated geometry; `session.export` / `smesh import` wiring; the remaining primitives; bulk-commit-dependent performance gating.
- **Must NOT:** read files, the clock or the environment inside `apply` or in any `read_*`/`realize` path (only `DirInputStore`/`stage_file` touch the filesystem); take a path in any op or query; change the model tolerance; merge faces across solids; iterate a `HashMap` into output; use `unsafe`; `unwrap` or index unchecked on parsed data; edit another crate or `Cargo.lock`; register layers (`CoreLayers` does); register an export op; depend on another W3a crate; copy testkit STEP files into this crate; add an `ApplyCtx::blob`-style contract workaround.

#### registers
op: model.import_step {source: {sha256}|{inline: base64}, name?, to_unit?, assume_unit?, weld_tol?, solids?, sheets?, check?}
query: cad.step_info {source, name?}
plugin: CadioPlugin (id "cadio", ops and queries only, no layers)
service type: StepInputs(Arc<dyn InputStore>) (injected by sm-server, unversioned; wiring is integration item D6)
stretch export function (not an op): write_step / export_step (STEP AP214 and AP242), to be exposed later as a session.export format

#### ACRs
[]  (none: the torus/cone/brick_with_void fixtures, `sm_testkit::w3a` and the OCC STEP files are stage-0 items by name; `ApplyCtx::blob` is rejected by decision 12)

#### fixtures
Consumed from stage 0, not produced here: `sm_topo_api::fixtures::{torus, cone, brick_with_void}` (+ testkit mirrors), `sm_testkit::w3a`, `crates/sm-testkit/data/step/*.step`. Crate-local hand-written STEP text fixtures live in `crates/sm-cadio/tests/data` and are not testkit items.

#### depends
[]  (no W3a crate dependency; sm-boolean consumes cadio output only through integration)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to OCCT's STEPControl_Reader / STEPCAFControl_Writer (and the FreeCAD and Gmsh importers built on it): an AP203/214/242 B-rep reader that keeps analytic surfaces and NURBS with trimming loops. It differs in being pure Rust with a hand-written Part 21 parser (no C++ dependency); in reading straight into our non-manifold cell complex with deterministic ids and creation order; in referencing the file by content hash so the import is a replayable, forkable timeline op; and in reporting located, classified diagnostics from our own validator. The advantage for neutronics is that exact planes, quadrics and tori survive to the tracking structure (ray tracing) instead of being faceted as in DAGMC.

#### risks
Real-world STEP variety (sphere/cone pole encodings, closed B-spline edges that do not start at the curve start, vertex-to-curve gaps, non-shared duplicate edges) will exceed the fixtures. Mitigation: the OCC fixtures from stage 0 are in the mandatory acceptance (planar milestone part, cylinder, torus, fillet, loft); located errors with STEP ids; welding; `cad.step_info` to see what a file contains before importing.
The OCC sphere (`VERTEX_LOOP`) and cone apex encodings are Stretch S1, not mandatory; the pole rule is proven in the mandatory set only on the hand-written seam sphere.
Reader-created faces on periodic surfaces carry no pcurves except at poles. Geometry defaults unwrap seams by projection, but downstream schemes (map/TFI) fall back to snap_to_face; untested with sm-scheme-map.
Exact canonical_hash equality with an original model is only claimed for the brick (and torus/void where the fixture builders match); other shapes use numeric equivalence (and the byte-identical write-read-write fixed point if S2 lands).
Inline mode puts up to 8 MiB into one op record, which bloats `timeline/ops/<hash>.toml`; the sha256 route depends on the front end staging the file (integration D6).
A negative-volume or inward-oriented solid is repaired heuristically (scratch realize, flip); mixed-orientation shells are reported as OpenShell rather than repaired.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
Should `to_unit` default from project.toml `units`, filled by the front end before the op is recorded (our proposal), or via a service?
Does the op layer impose a maximum op-record size that makes the 8 MiB inline cap too large?
Which names/colors become Model groups/colors on import (follow-up op `model.import_step_attributes`, W3b)?

#### escalation
Orchestrator items outside this crate: (1) integration D6: sm-server `standard()` injects `StepInputs(DirInputStore)` (memory variant for `--memory`), and sm-cli/sm-mcp/sm-lang stage a path into `inputs/<sha256>.stp` and rewrite it to `{sha256}`; (2) who fills `to_unit` from project.toml units; (3) where STEP export is exposed (only if S2 lands); (4) the op name `model.import_step` lives in the model namespace but is registered by sm-cadio, and sm-model must not also register it.

### W3-SUBMAP. `sm-scheme-submap` (L3, skeleton exists from stage 0): Cubit-style surface submap
- **Deps:** sm-core, sm-geom-api, sm-topo-api, sm-scheme-api, thiserror. Dev (allowed by decision 12: acyclic, peer already on main): sm-testkit (`loops2d::{l_shape, five_sided_submap, rectangle}`, `topo::planar_face`, `check`, `determinism`), sm-scheme-api[stubs] (stub `Equal` curve scheme, stub `Pave` as fallback), sm-mesh, sm-mesh-api, sm-model-api, sm-quality (L2), sm-sizing (`AnglePicker`, `IiaSolver`; L2), sm-scheme-map (dev only, on main; the tests need the same face builder and VertexRule/ReferenceCornerPicker setup it already uses), proptest. NOT sm-scheme-pave, sm-scheme-tri or any other W3a crate still being built, normal or dev. The small Coons kernel is private to this crate (no normal dependency on sm-scheme-map).
- **Positioning (first in the crate `DESIGN.md`):** conceptually Cubit's `submap` (and Gmsh's transfinite surface on a decomposed face). It meshes a single-loop face with more than four logical sides (L, U, staircase) as a union of mapped rectangular blocks. It differs from `sm-scheme-map` in the vertex rule (`VertexRule::Submap`, corner sum 4) and the interval rows (closure per direction). It differs from the `stubs.rs` `Submap` in that the stub only works for four sides and has no geometry. Result: conforming, all-quad, valence-4-interior mesh whose layout is a pure function of the solved plan; node keys are canonical, so output is bit-identical for 1 and N threads.
- **Stage-0 items used (exist per decision 12; reference, do not re-request):** `sm_testkit::topo::planar_face(&Region2, normal)` (face on the left of the loop, one Line edge per segment, edge i from vertex i to i+1, area = `Region2::area`); `GenerateFailure::with_nodes` (optional: attach the offending boundary nodes to a lattice failure); the sm-quality L2 re-ladder. `loops2d::staircase` is NOT a decision-12 item: define it test-local (fallback is the default). Not used in W3a: `GenCtx::loop_uvs` (pcurve path is W3b).
- **Scope (mandatory):**
  - `pub const NAME: &str = "submap"`; `pub fn register(reg: &mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>`; `pub struct SurfaceSubmap` (unit struct, `Scheme`).
  - `Scheme` impl: `dim = Surface`, `caps = QUAD | BLOCK_STRUCTURED` (no `STRUCTURED`, so a volume `map` will not accept it and a sweep reads it through `strip_from`). No params (non-empty gives `NotApplicable`). `generate` leaves `Patch::grid = None`.
  - `applicability`: `no` for a non-surface target, params, more than one boundary loop (`geom::face_loops`), embedded edges or vertices, or fewer than 4 corners. Otherwise compute `geom::corners`, `VertexType::nearest` and the sum S of `submap_weight()`. A `Reversal` gives `no("reversal corners deferred")`. S = 4 gives `yes(80)` (decision 12 score table: map 100, submap 80, pave 40, trimesh 30; a rectangle therefore still loses to map). Any other S gives `yes(20, "corner types need picking")`. The auto list is unchanged in W3a, so no W2 hash changes; auto selection is W3b.
  - `requirements`: `out.vertex_rule(f, VertexRule::Submap)`.
  - `interval_constraints`: from `cx.layout(f)`, `sum_equal(edges of dir PlusI, edges of MinusI)` and the same for PlusJ/MinusJ (as `stubs::Submap` does; read `LogicalSide::dir`, never recompute). An empty row after cancellation (a seam edge on both sides) is skipped. No layout, or fewer than 4 sides, gives `SchemeError::Input`. These two rows are the whole interval contribution (see `lattice` for what they do not guarantee).
  - `pub mod lattice` (pure, no topology types):
    - `pub struct LatticePolygon { pub verts: Vec<(i64, i64)>, pub dirs: Vec<SideDir>, pub lens: Vec<u32> }`
    - `pub fn lattice_polygon(sides: &[(SideDir, u32)]) -> Result<LatticePolygon, SubmapError>`: start at (0,0), accumulate `lens` along `dirs`; require closure, positive signed area (face on the left) and simplicity (exact integer test: no two non-adjacent edges intersect or touch, no vertex on another edge).
    - `pub fn pick_area(&self) -> (u64 /*A*/, u64 /*B*/, u64 /*I*/)`, I = A - B/2 + 1.
    - `pub fn inside(&self, a: i64, b: i64) -> Location` with `Location::{Outside, Boundary, Inside}`, exact integers.
  - `pub mod blocks`: `pub struct Block { pub i0: i64, pub i1: i64, pub j0: i64, pub j1: i64 }` and `pub fn decompose(p: &LatticePolygon) -> Vec<Block>`. From every reflex vertex (right turn) extend the incident edge that runs along j into the interior to the first boundary contact (vertex or edge). Cuts are parallel, never cross, every piece is a rectangle. Blocks sorted by `(j0, i0)`.
  - `generate` (pure):
    1. `side_nodes(f, k)` for every side gives the boundary lattice points (walk sides along `dirs`; boundary node m of side k at its lattice point). Lattice-point to `NodeKey` map is a `BTreeMap`.
    2. Cut nodes: a cut P to Q with m interior points gets 3-D positions `P + (Q-P)·s/(m+1)`, s = 1..m, snapped by `geom::snap_to_face`.
    3. Block interiors: Coons (TFI) with 3-D chord-length fractions averaged over opposite lines as in `sm-scheme-map/DESIGN.md`, then `geom::snap_to_face`. Planar faces are exact.
    4. Interior-node key = `NodeKey::new(f, r)`, r = rank among strictly interior lattice points in row-major `(b, a)` order (cut nodes included). Boundary nodes use `PatchBuilder::refer`.
    5. One `ElemShape::Quad` per lattice cell inside the polygon, row-major, corners `[(a,b),(a+1,b),(a+1,b+1),(a,b+1)]` (face on the left, so quads follow the face normal).
    6. Orientation check as in `sm-scheme-map`: the cross product of each quad's diagonals agrees with `geom::face_normal` at its centroid; a fold gives `FailureKind::Quality`.
  - Failure mapping (`GenerateFailure`): lattice that fails closure, positivity or simplicity gives `FailureKind::Constraint` (message starts "lattice:", boundary nodes attached via `with_nodes` when known); hard Reversal in the layout gives `NotApplicable`; `snap_to_face` failure gives `Geometry`; missing node or layout gives `Internal`. Never `unwrap`. `thiserror` `SubmapError` converted at the boundary.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-12):** graded cut-line spacing (chord-length-averaged between neighbouring parallel boundary lines) if the measured L-case Jacobians justify it; triangle-fan or smoothing fixups.
- **Deferred to W3b:** parameter-space blending through pcurves via `GenCtx::loop_uvs` (periodic/seam faces); Reversal (slit) corners; inner loops (holes); auto selection by score. One-line note each in `DESIGN.md`.
- **Acceptance (analytic; faces built with `topo::planar_face` and the `sm-scheme-map/tests/common` setup, using stub `Equal`, `ReferenceIntervalSolver`, `ReferenceCornerPicker`; every case also passes with `sm_sizing::AnglePicker` and gives the same layout):**
  1. `loops2d::l_shape()` (outer types End, End, End, Corner, End, End), all edges size 0.25 (counts e0..e5 = 8,4,4,4,4,8): layout has 6 sides, dirs [PlusI, PlusJ, PlusI, PlusJ, MinusI, MinusJ] (first side is geometric edge 1, since sides start after the first End); rows {e1,e3}={e5} and {e2,e4}={e0} hold; 48 quads, 65 nodes (A = 48, B = 32, I = 33), area 3 within 1e-12, every quad a 0.25 square (min and max scaled Jacobian = 1 within 1e-12), `sm_mesh_api::validate` empty, Euler V - E + F = 1, 2 blocks.
  2. Same L with hard counts (e0..e5) = (5,3,3,2,2,5): rows hold (3+2 = 5 and 3+2 = 5). Lattice polygon (0,0)->(3,0)->(3,3)->(5,3)->(5,5)->(0,5): A = 19, B = 20, I = 10; so 19 quads, 30 nodes, 2 blocks. Area equals the geometric area within 1e-12, `validate` empty, every scaled Jacobian > 0 and min >= 0.5 (worker records the measured min in DESIGN.md and pins the test at 90% of it, never below 0.5).
  3. `loops2d::five_sided_submap()` (End, Side, End, End, End), size 0.5: layout is 4 sides, one with two edges; 32 quads, 45 nodes, area 8 within 1e-12, scaled Jacobian = 1. The worker derives hard goals from the layout and asserts bottom = top exactly (integer rows read through the layout, not geometry).
  4. `loops2d::rectangle(3.0, 2.0)` at size 0.5 under `submap`: node set identical to the analytic lattice (bit-exact for representable values), 1 block, 24 quads.
  5. Property (proptest, seeded, <= 64 cases): random staircase polygon of k = 1..4 steps, integer widths and heights 1..4, unit spacing, counts = edge lengths (staircase builder test-local). Mesh node set = integer lattice points inside the polygon; quads = A; nodes = I + B; area exact; every scaled Jacobian = 1 within 1e-12; `validate` empty; blocks = k + 1, disjoint, total area A. A failure prints the seed.
  6. `lattice` unit tests: closure, orientation and simplicity accept the L and the staircases. Non-simple U: sides [(PlusI,5),(PlusJ,4),(MinusI,1),(MinusJ,4),(MinusI,2),(PlusJ,4),(MinusI,2),(MinusJ,4)] closes with positive area but the notch touches the base at (4,0), so `lattice_polygon` returns `SubmapError` whose text contains "lattice:" and "not simple". A clockwise sequence gives a positivity error; an unclosed sequence a closure error. Pick's A, B, I match brute-force lattice-point counting on 200 seeded staircases.
  7. Interval contract: hard counts that break a closure row (e.g. 6 vs 3 + 2 on the L) make `ReferenceIntervalSolver` and `IiaSolver` return `Infeasible` naming the involved rows. A seam edge on both sides of a direction cancels with no empty row.
  8. Fallback: with chain `submap -> pave` using the stub `Pave` (test registry built WITHOUT the colliding stub `Submap`; it registers the real `submap`), a U-face whose plan yields the case-6 lattice failure meshes through the stub `Pave` and the plan's attempt history records a `Constraint` failure of `submap`; without the chain `Driver::mesh` returns that error.
  9. `Patch::strip_from(key)` from every non-corner boundary node of the L (case 1) returns a line whose last node is a boundary node and whose length is the lattice extent + 1 along that column or row; `patch.grid` is `None`.
  10. Applicability: rectangle `yes(80)` (and map's 100 beats it); L `yes(80)`; corner sum != 4 gives `yes(20)`; `Reversal` (hard intent) is `no`; a face with a hole is `no`; params `{}` accepted and `{"x":1}` rejected.
  11. Determinism: `sm_testkit::determinism::check(3, 4, ..)` over `check::mesh_hash` of the case-1 mesh with `Sequential` and `ScopedThreads(4)`, several faces meshed in one wave; a cold second run is bit-identical; `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default hashes equal.
  12. `cargo build`, `cargo test -p sm-scheme-submap --all-features`, `cargo clippy -p sm-scheme-submap --all-targets -- -D warnings`, rustdoc (`-D warnings`) on every public item, `DESIGN.md` with positioning, lattice/cut algorithm, and the numeric invariants above.
- **Must NOT:** edit sm-scheme-api (the `Submap` stub stays the contract oracle), sm-testkit or sm-sizing; register vertex rules, pickers or solvers; depend (normal or dev) on any W3a crate still being built (pave, tri, sweep, tet, smooth, ...); depend on sm-scheme-curve or sm-mesher; claim edges or faces; use `HashMap`; pick a fallback scheme itself (the driver does); add a Reversal, hole or pcurve/periodic-seam path; assign interior nodes in thread-dependent order; combine the stub registry with the real `register()` (duplicate name error).

#### registers
Scheme "submap" (SchemeDim::Surface, caps QUAD | BLOCK_STRUCTURED) via sm_scheme_submap::register(&mut SchemeRegistry). No ops, queries, layers or export formats.

#### ACRs
[] (all needed contract items are stage-0 items: topo::planar_face, GenerateFailure::with_nodes, sm-quality at L2)

#### fixtures
Uses stage-0 `sm_testkit::topo::planar_face`. `staircase(steps: &[(u32,u32)]) -> Region2` is test-local (unit-spaced right-angle outer loop, End/Corner by turn, exact integer area).

#### depends
[] (no W3a crate dependencies)

#### baseline
Cubit submap (corner-type sum 4, direction interval sums, mapped rectangular sub-blocks) and Gmsh transfinite surface after rectilinear decomposition. Differs from the stub in real lattice validation and decomposition, and from sm-scheme-map in vertex rule and multi-block output. Advantage: pure, deterministic, conforming all-quad mesh with canonical keys and typed lattice-failure diagnostics feeding the driver's fallback chain.

#### risks
- The two closure rows do not guarantee a simple lattice polygon (spiral or deep U can self-overlap). Mitigated by the exact simplicity test and a Constraint failure that drives the fallback chain; the driver cannot detect it at interval-solve time.
- Straight uniform cut lines can give poor Jacobians when neighbouring boundary spacing is strongly graded (case 2 floor 0.5, measured and pinned). Graded spacing is Stretch.
- `snap_to_face` may be slow/inaccurate on strongly curved faces; the pcurve path and periodic faces are W3b.
- Private Coons kernel duplicates sm-scheme-map's tfi.rs; a later refactor could hoist it.
- The driver's first-side convention rotates the lattice frame relative to geometry: tests assert dirs and counts through the layout.
- Stub and real `submap` share a name: registries must never hold both.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
- Provisional score table (decision 12) is submap 80 for S = 4; the 20 for "needs picking" is this task's choice, confirm in W3b auto selection.
- Coons kernel sharing (sm-math) stays out of scope.

### W3-PAVE. `sm-scheme-pave` (L3, skeleton exists): unstructured all-quad surface scheme (`"pave"`)
- **Deps:** sm-core, sm-math (exact `orient2d`/`incircle`, `crates/sm-math/src/predicates.rs`), sm-geom-api, sm-topo-api, sm-scheme-api (uses `GenCtx::loop_uvs`, stage 0), serde (derive), serde_json, thiserror. Dev: sm-testkit (`loops2d`, `planar_face`, `annulus_face`; stage 0), sm-scheme-api[stubs], sm-mesh-api, sm-model-api, sm-quality (L2 after decision 12, already on main). The skeleton `Cargo.toml` lists only sm-scheme-api; add the rest (no root `Cargo.toml` edit: `members = crates/*`). No dependency (normal or dev) on sm-scheme-map, sm-scheme-curve or any other W3a crate (decision 12: a worker never depends on a W3a crate still being built). No dependency on sm-sizing: the wanted size arrives through `cx.size`.
- **Shared CDT kernel (decided, was an open item):** pave keeps a private `cdt` module. The duplication with sm-scheme-tri is accepted for W3a; a shared L2 crate is a W3b candidate. Do not add a new crate.
- **Algorithm (decision):** "Frontal-Delaunay + recombination". (1) Constrained Delaunay triangulation of the metric-corrected parameter-space polygon with the **given** boundary nodes fixed (never split). (2) Interior points inserted row by row from the front (Rebay-style frontal placement, but at the apex of a *square* on the front edge, so pairs of triangles form near-squares; Remacle et al. 2013 L-infinity frontal Delaunay, no cross field). (3) Triangles are paired into convex quads by an Edmonds-blossom matching (greedy-by-quality start, augmenting paths only for the leftovers). If no perfect matching exists, split the longest interior edge of each unmatched triangle (T grows by 2, boundary untouched) and retry, at most `max_rounds`. (4) Metric-aware smart-Laplacian smoothing. Why not Blacker-Stephenson paving: closure/seaming heuristics are where determinism and robustness break. Why not "coarse CDT + 3/4-split": it needs every sharp corner on an even boundary index. Our method needs only an even **total** (Cubit's rule, ratified: total over all loops, not per loop) and fails typed (`Geometry`) when no matching exists; the registry fallback takes over.
- **Landing order (commit each stage):** (a) CDT of a single-loop polygon + brick face; (b) frontal points; (c) matching; (d) constant-metric correction + cylinder; (e) smoothing; (f) scheme glue and driver tests; (g) Stretch items last.
- **Scope, kernel (pure, no topology; mirrors `sm-scheme-map/src/tfi.rs`):**
  - `pub struct PaveOptions { pub gradation: f64 /*0.3*/, pub smooth_iters: u32 /*30*/, pub max_rounds: u32 /*6*/ }` (+ `Default`).
  - `pub struct QuadMesh2 { pub points: Vec<[f64; 2]>, pub quads: Vec<[u32; 4]>, pub rounds: u32 }`. `points[..nb]` are the input boundary points in loop-concatenated order, bit-exact; the rest are interior. Invariant: for every input segment `a -> b` the quad that owns it lists `a` then `b` consecutively (interior on the left, handedness-independent).
  - `pub fn pave_region(loops: &[Vec<[f64; 2]>], metric: &dyn Fn([f64; 2]) -> [f64; 3], size: Option<&dyn Fn([f64; 2]) -> f64>, opts: &PaveOptions) -> Result<QuadMesh2, PaveError>`. `loops[0]` is outer, the rest are holes (the kernel accepts holes from the start, since the CDT constraint handling is the same; acceptance on holes is Stretch). Either handedness of the outer loop is accepted (signed area, reflected internally). `metric(uv) = [E, F, G]` is the first fundamental form `[du.du, du.dv, dv.dv]`; `size(uv)` is the wanted metric edge length (default: boundary segment lengths interpolated over the boundary-node triangulation, limited by `gradation`).
  - `#[non_exhaustive] pub enum PaveError { OddBoundary { total: usize }, LoopTooSmall { ring: usize, nodes: usize }, BoundaryIntersects, NoPerfectMatching { unmatched: usize }, BadMetric }` (`thiserror`).
  - Metric correction: global affine map `w = A (uv - uv0)` with `A = chol(mean G)` over the loop (exact isotropy for planes, cylinders, cones); residual metric `M(w) = A^-T G A^-1` for edge lengths (`sqrt(d^T M(mid) d)`), frontal placement (Cholesky of `M` at the edge midpoint), pairing weights and smoothing. Lawson flips use exact `incircle`; ties and priorities use `total_cmp` plus index; no `HashMap`/`HashSet`; the kernel is sequential.
- **Scope, scheme:**
  - `pub const NAME: &str = "pave"`; `pub struct Pave` (unit struct); `pub fn register(reg: &mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>`; `#[derive(Deserialize)] #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)] pub struct PaveParams { pub gradation: Option<f64>, pub smooth_iters: Option<u32> }`; an unknown field or out-of-range value gives `NotApplicable` with a reason.
  - `dim = SchemeDim::Surface`; `caps = Caps::QUAD` only (never `STRUCTURED`/`BLOCK_STRUCTURED`; a sweep may take a paved source but must reject it as a linking face); `supports_groups = false`.
  - `applicability`: yes(40, "unstructured quad") for a face with a surface carrier (score 40 is the decision 12 provisional table: map 100, submap 80, pave 40, trimesh 30; auto lists stay unchanged in W3a, so the score only matters for explicit `auto` tests and W3b). `NotApplicable` for: no surface carrier; a `CellFlags::DEGENERATE` edge (pole; deferred to W3b); embedded edges or vertices (`MeshInput::embedded_edges/embedded_vertices`; deferred to W3b); a periodic surface (`Domain1::is_periodic`) for which `GenCtx::loop_uvs` returns `None`.
  - `requirements`: `out.vertex_rule(f, VertexRule::Free)` only.
  - `interval_constraints` (even-count rule, replaces the stub's single row): `out.even(&all coedge edges of all loops)` with multiplicity (a seam used twice counts twice), plus per loop `out.linear(&terms, Rel::Ge, 4)`.
  - `generate`: (a) `cx.loops(f)`; boundary `(u, v)` per node from **`GenCtx::loop_uvs(face, lp)`** (stage 0; pcurve-based, seam-aware, projection fallback on non-periodic faces; seam uses give different `(u, v)` for the same `NodeKey`). `None` gives `FailureKind::Geometry` with the reason. No private copy of this logic. (b) `metric` from `ParametricSurface::jet`, `size` from `cx.size` (evaluated at `eval(uv)`). (c) `pave_region`. (d) Interior points: `eval_many`, key `NodeKey::new(f, i)` in kernel order, `Param::Surface([u, v])`; boundary nodes via `PatchBuilder::refer` (dedups seam keys); quads as `ElemShape::Quad`, no `Patch::grid`. (e) Checks: odd total gives `FailureKind::Constraint`; a quad with a repeated key, or whose normal disagrees with `geom::face_normal` at its centroid, or 3-D scaled Jacobian <= 0.05, gives `FailureKind::Quality`; kernel `BoundaryIntersects`/`NoPerfectMatching` give `Geometry`. Failures that name nodes use `GenerateFailure::with_nodes` (stage 0) where the offending nodes are known (quality failures).
  - **Scheme names:** the production fallback is `trimesh` (decision 12; there is no `tri` alias). The composer (not this task) registers `set_fallback(Surface, "pave", ["trimesh"])`.
- **Test registries:** real pave registers the name `"pave"`, which the stubs also use. Never combine `stubs::registry()` or `stubs::Pave` with the real crate. Build the registry from `stubs::Equal` and `stubs::Tri` (test-only, the stub name `"tri"` is used only as a fallback target inside tests) plus `sm_scheme_pave::register`.
- **Acceptance (numbers are proposals, ratified from the first run; a worker may raise a threshold, never lower one without reporting the measurement). Mandatory: 1-3, 4a, 5, 6, 7a, 8-10, 11 (kernel gate).**
  1. Kernel, fixtures `square`, `rectangle(3,2)`, `circle(1.5,32)`, `l_shape` (tests subdivide each loop uniformly to spacing 0.125, 0.25, 0.5 where the polygon is not already the boundary, adjusting to an even total): all quads; boundary points bit-exact; every boundary segment owned by exactly one quad in the right direction, every interior edge by exactly two; `sum(area) == Region2::area` within 1e-12 relative; all four corners of every quad strictly positive by exact `orient2d`; `V - E + F == 1`; interior points pass `Region2::contains`.
  2. Quality (corner-sine scaled Jacobian, computed in the test): min >= 0.40 and mean >= 0.85 on `square`, `rectangle(3,2)`, `circle`; `l_shape` min >= 0.35.
  3. Even rule: odd total gives `OddBoundary` for every mandatory fixture; a 2-node loop gives `LoopTooSmall`; a hand-built self-crossing loop gives `BoundaryIntersects`.
  4a. Constant metric: `[4, 0, 1]` on `[0,1] x [0,1]` meshes like a 2 x 1 rectangle: all metric edge lengths within [0.5 h, 2 h] and min scaled Jacobian (metric-evaluated) >= 0.40.
  5. Scheme, planar: `brick_setup([4,3,1])` +z face, hard counts (8, 6), selection = `[face]`, `Driver::mesh` with `ReferenceBuilder::surface()` (as `crates/sm-scheme-api/tests/surface_only.rs`) and the test registry above: `validate` empty, all `CellShape::Quad`, area 12 within 1e-12, boundary nodes are the edge nodes, every cell normal agrees with `geom::face_normal`, `sm_quality` ScaledJacobian min >= 0.40 / mean >= 0.85.
  6. Scheme, curved: cylinder lateral face (`fixtures::cylinder(topo, 3.0, 1.0)`; 72 nodes per circle, 4 on the seam; anisotropic in `(angle, z)`, `E = 9`): every node within 1e-12 of `x^2 + y^2 = 9`, `0 <= z <= 1`; `validate` empty (the seam closes, no duplicate vertices); area in `[2 pi r h cos(t/2), 2 pi r h]` with `t` the largest angular extent of any quad; per-quad longest/shortest edge <= 2.5; 3-D scaled Jacobian min >= 0.40 / mean >= 0.85.
  7a. Scheme, circle: cylinder bottom cap (32 nodes): area `ngon_area(32, r)` within 1e-12, normal -z, SJ gates as in 2.
  8. Even rule through the driver: soft counts (3,2,2,2) on a paved face are repaired to an even sum and mesh; hard odd counts give `SchemeError::Infeasible` with blame `(Target::Surface(face), "pave")` and, with a fallback in the intent, `Driver::mesh` lands on the fallback and records the `Attempt` (same shape as `crates/sm-scheme-api/tests/pave.rs`, fallback = test stub `tri`); a loop with sum < 4 is `Infeasible`.
  9. `NotApplicable` reasons for: embedded edge, sphere face (pole), unknown param field, face without surface carrier, periodic face whose `loop_uvs` is `None`.
  10. Determinism: `Sequential` vs `ScopedThreads(4)` give equal `topology_hash` and `geometry_hash` (bit-exact geometry) on items 5, 6, 7a; two runs give bit-identical `QuadMesh2`; CI also runs the test binary with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`; no `HashMap`/`HashSet` in the crate (grep test).
  11. Performance (kernel only, gated): `pave_region` on a 100 x 100 square (400 boundary points, about 10^4 quads) in under 2 s in release; record the figure in `DESIGN.md`. Through `Driver::mesh` the time is recorded only (it depends on the W3b bulk mesh commit, decision 12).
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance; cut in this order if time is short):**
  - S1. Holes: kernel fixtures `rectangle_with_holes` and `annulus(1,2,32)` meet acceptance 1-3 (with `V - E + F == 1 - holes`) and quality min >= 0.40 / mean >= 0.85; scheme: a face from stage-0 `planar_face(rectangle_with_holes(), z)` has area 50 within 1e-12, three loops, Euler `1 - 2`, and meshes through `Driver::mesh`; stage-0 `annulus_face(1, 2)` (two circle edges) meshes with area `pi (4 - 1)` within 1e-12.
  - S2. Graded boundary (spacing 0.05 on the left to 0.2 on the right): mean quad area for centroid x < 1 divided by that for x > 2 is <= 1/3.
  - S3. Spatially varying residual metric quality (non-constant `M`: NURBS-like faces): gates reported, not asserted.
- **Deferred to W3b (list in DESIGN.md, do not build):** embedded-member conformance, pole/degenerate-edge faces, blossom *minimum-weight* optimality, valence cleanup (diagonal collapse, valence-2 removal), Winslow/untangle (smoothing is a separate crate), anisotropic metric from `SizeField::metric`, intra-face parallelism, cross-field alignment, auto-selection of pave, a shared 2-D CDT crate.
- **Stage-0 items used (decision 12; referenced, not requested):** `GenCtx::loop_uvs`, `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`, sm-testkit `planar_face` and `annulus_face`, sm-quality at L2, skeleton crate. If an item has not landed when the worker starts, stop and report `blocked_on_api`; do not add a private copy.
- **Docs:** `DESIGN.md` in the style of `crates/sm-scheme-map/DESIGN.md` (Positioning "like Cubit pave / Gmsh Frontal-Delaunay for quads + Blossom recombination; differs in (A) metric-corrected parameter space with exact predicates and seam-aware boundary, (B) deterministic pure `generate`, (C) declarative even-sum rows"; phases table; failure mapping; Stretch and Deferred lists). Rustdoc on every public item.
- **Must NOT:** register fallback or auto lists (the composer does); claim curves or write curve meshes; produce `Patch::grid`; depend on sibling scheme crates, sm-sizing or any unfinished W3a crate; add a private `loop_uvs` copy; use `unwrap`/`expect` on input paths; use any `HashMap`/`HashSet`; time- or thread-based randomness; edit `sm-scheme-api`, `sm-testkit` or the root `Cargo.toml`; combine real pave with `stubs::Pave` in one registry.

#### registers
Scheme "pave" (SchemeDim::Surface, Caps::QUAD) via sm_scheme_pave::register(&mut SchemeRegistry). No ops, queries, layers or export formats.

#### ACRs
[]  (none: GenCtx::loop_uvs, GenerateFailure::with_nodes, planar_face and annulus_face are decision 12 stage-0 items, referenced by name above)

#### fixtures
Uses stage-0 sm-testkit `planar_face(&Region2, ..)` and `annulus_face(r_in, r_out)` (Stretch S1 only). Invariants it relies on: loops = 1 + holes, area == Region2::area (1e-12), Euler 1 - holes; annulus area pi (r_out^2 - r_in^2). Mandatory tests use only `brick_setup`, `fixtures::cylinder` and `loops2d`.

#### depends
[] (no unfinished W3a crate; stage 0 must have landed)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to Cubit's pave (Blacker-Stephenson) and Gmsh's Frontal-Delaunay-for-quads with Blossom recombination (Remacle et al. 2013): all-quad unstructured surface meshing that conforms to fixed boundary nodes and an even-interval rule. It differs in (A) working in a metric-corrected (u,v) space with exact sm-math predicates and a seam-aware boundary, so periodic faces (cylinder) mesh without a 3-D projection loop; (B) being a pure deterministic function of the plan (sequential kernel, total orders on every tie, no hash maps), so 1 and N threads are bit-identical; (C) being declarative (an even-sum row plus per-loop minimums for the interval solver, so soft counts are repaired before generation and hard odd counts blame pave and fall back). Advantage over classical paving: no closure heuristics; robustness comes from CDT plus matching with a typed failure instead of a malformed mesh. Quality is a little below Cubit's mature paving until valence cleanup and optimization smoothing arrive (W3b).

#### risks
No perfect matching on the triangle dual in rare cases; mitigation is bounded interior edge splitting (parity-preserving) and a typed Geometry failure so the driver falls back (production: trimesh), but termination of the retry loop is empirical.
Quality thresholds (min 0.40 / mean 0.85) are estimates; rhombus-like pairs and colliding fronts may land below them (valence cleanup deferred); thresholds are ratified from the first run.
Frontal placement with spatially varying metrics is heuristic (Delaunay exact only for constant residual metric); pass count is capped. Gated surfaces are constant-metric (plane, cylinder); varying metric is Stretch S3.
Scope is large for one session; land in the stated order and cut Stretch from S3 backwards.
CDT duplicated with sm-scheme-tri: accepted for W3a.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
Auto-selection score 40 vs the composer's auto list: only relevant in W3b (auto lists unchanged in W3a).
Whether faces with embedded edges/vertices should ever be paved (CDT constraint insertion makes it natural) or stay with the tri schemes: W3b.

#### escalation
1) Ratify or adjust quality thresholds after the first run (proposed min 0.40, mean 0.85, l_shape 0.35). 2) The composer must register `set_fallback(Surface, "pave", ["trimesh"])` (outside this task). 3) If a stage-0 item is missing at start, report `blocked_on_api`.

### W3-TRI. `sm-scheme-tri` (L3, skeleton exists): triangle surface schemes (constrained Delaunay + refinement mandatory; advancing front stretch)
- **Owns:** `crates/sm-scheme-tri/` only. The skeleton (stage 0) exists with only an `sm-scheme-api` dependency and a no-op `register`. The worker completes its own `Cargo.toml`. The root manifest globs `crates/*`; do not touch it.
- **Deps:** sm-core, sm-math (exact `orient2d`/`incircle` in `crates/sm-math/src/predicates.rs`; `sum::NeumaierSum`), sm-geom-api (`ParametricSurface::{jet,project,domain}`, `PCurve::eval`, `Domain1::{wrap,nearest_to}`), sm-topo-api (`TopologyView::{loops,pcurve,carrier,flags}`), sm-scheme-api, thiserror, serde, serde_json. Dev (decision 12: allowed only because every peer is already on main): sm-testkit (`loops2d::*`, `determinism::check`, and the stage-0 `topo::planar_face` / `topo::annulus_face`), sm-scheme-api[stubs] (`stubs::registry`, `brick_setup`), sm-mesh-api (`ReferenceBuilder`, `validate`), sm-mesh, sm-quality (L2 after decision 12), sm-scheme-curve (real `equal`), sm-topo-api fixtures (`cylinder`, `sphere`). **Never** depend, even in dev, on sm-scheme-pave, -submap, -sweep, -tet or any other W3a crate still being built. No dependency on sm-tess, sm-sizing, sm-mesher or any tet crate. **Tetmesh must not depend on this crate:** it reaches the triangles only through `GenCtx::surface_patch(face)` after imposing `Caps::TRI` on its faces; the composer registers both.
- **Stage-0 items this task uses by name (all exist or are being added; none is an ACR):** `GenCtx::loop_uvs(face, &BoundaryLoop) -> Option<Vec<[f64;2]>>` (seam-aware boundary uv: pcurve first, projection fallback; `FaceChart::lift` calls it and does its own pole and Newell-plane handling on top); `GenerateFailure::with_nodes(Vec<NodeKey>)` (serde default; `Quality` failures list the nodes of the worst triangle); `sm_testkit::topo::{planar_face, annulus_face}`; the sm-quality L2 re-ladder. `sphere_zone(r, u, v)` is optional in testkit; if absent the worker builds it in `tests/common` (it is not an ACR).
- **Contract facts this design rests on (read in `crates/sm-scheme-api/src/{scheme,patch,geom}.rs`):**
  - Boundary nodes are fixed before the face runs. `GenCtx::loops(face)` returns node keys per ring, `GenCtx::patches.node(key) -> (Point3, Param)` gives positions and `Param::Curve(t)`. A face scheme cannot add or move edge nodes (neighbours share them) unless it claims children, which `tri` must not. Boundary conformity is by construction and **no Steiner point may be placed on a boundary segment** (decision 12: no boundary Steiner points in W3a).
  - `GenCtx::embedded(face)` chains must be conformed to: every chain node is a mesh vertex and every chain segment a mesh edge, else `check_patch` rejects the patch.
  - `GenCtx::size: Option<&dyn SizeField>` gives `size(p)` and `metric(p) -> SymMat3`.
- **Scope, mandatory:**
  - **Schemes** (`dim = Surface`, `caps = Caps::TRI`, `requirements`: `out.vertex_rule(face, VertexRule::Free)`, no interval constraints, no claims, no groups): `"trimesh"` (default; same algorithm as `"tridelaunay"`) and `"tridelaunay"` (CDT + refinement). `pub fn register(reg: &mut sm_scheme_api::SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>` registers them (plus `"triadvance"` only if the Stretch item below is accepted). It never registers `"tri"` (the stub's name), a fallback chain or an auto list; auto lists stay unchanged in W3a (decision 12). Test registries must not combine `stubs::registry()` with this crate's `register` (name `tri` is not registered, but `brick_setup` needs the other stubs; build the registry from stubs minus any colliding name or register only what the test needs).
  - `pub struct TriParams { pub min_angle: f64 /*20.0 deg*/, pub fail_angle: f64 /*12.0 deg*/, pub size_factor: f64 /*1.0*/, pub optimize: bool /*true*/, pub max_points: u32 /*2_000_000*/ }`, serde with `deny_unknown_fields`. Bad values give `Applicability::no(reason)`, never a panic. `applicability` is cheap: Surface target, TRI-capable. **Provisional score 30** for `trimesh` and `tridelaunay` (decision 12 table: map 100, submap 80, pave 40, trimesh 30). The table is for W3b auto selection; auto lists do not change in W3a.
  - **Chart** (`pub mod chart`): `pub struct FaceChart`, `FaceChart::lift(cx: &GenCtx<'_>, face: EntityId) -> Result<(FaceChart, Vec<ChartLoop>), TriError>`. Boundary uv comes from `GenCtx::loop_uvs` (pcurve evaluated at the node's `Param::Curve(t)`, vertex ends via `geom::edge_param(&cc, 0.0 | 1.0)`, so seam and pole duplicates appear naturally; else `surface.project(p, hint)` with periodic unwrapping against the previous uv); a face without a surface carrier uses its Newell plane. Chart coordinates are `q = (±u, v)` (negate `u` when the carrier `Sense` is `Reversed`, so loops stay counter-clockwise) mapped through the Cholesky factor of the reference metric `G = Jᵀ·M·J` at the loop centroid (`M = size.metric(p)`, default `I/h²`), translated and scaled by a power of two into `[1, 2)`. All predicates run on these exact doubles. `FaceChart::{lift3, jacobian, is_isometric}`: plane and cylinder are isometric. A lifted loop that does not close within `1e-9` of the uv extent gives `TriError::OpenLoop`.
  - **Pure 2D kernel** (`pub mod cdt`, usable and tested without topology):
    - `pub struct Pslg { pub points: Vec<[f64; 2]>, pub segments: Vec<Segment> }`, `pub struct Segment { pub a: u32, pub b: u32, pub kind: SegmentKind }`, `pub enum SegmentKind { Boundary, Embedded }`.
    - `Cdt::new(p: &Pslg) -> Result<Cdt, TriError>`. Inserts points in Hilbert order of coordinates quantized on a fixed 2^-40 grid (platform independent), recovers segments by Sloan flips, keeps the inside by parity flood fill (a Boundary segment toggles in/out, an Embedded one does not; holes and islands fall out). Predicates are exact `orient2d`/`incircle` only; ties are "no flip". Coordinates outside 2^±100 are refused via `try_orient2d`. Crossing segments give `TriError::SelfIntersection{a, b}`.
    - `Cdt::{points, triangles /* CCW */, is_constrained, neighbors}`.
    - `pub trait Geometry2 { fn lift(&self, q: [f64; 2]) -> Point3; fn size(&self, p: Point3) -> f64; fn is_isometric(&self) -> bool; }`
    - `Cdt::refine(&mut self, g: &dyn Geometry2, o: &RefineOptions) -> RefineReport`. A triangle is bad if its 3-D min angle `< min_angle` or its 3-D circumradius `> size_factor·h(centroid)·0.67` (0.67 ≈ 1.15/√3). Served worst-first in a `BinaryHeap` keyed on `(f64::to_bits(badness), Reverse(creation id))`. The circumcenter is inserted by Bowyer-Watson with constrained edges as walls. A circumcenter that would encroach a constrained segment (inside its diametral circle) or leave the domain is rejected and the triangle goes to `RefineReport::unfixable` (Triangle's `-Y` behaviour).
    - `Cdt::optimize(&mut self, g, o)`: at most 4 sweeps of Jacobi Laplacian smoothing in the chart (interior free points only, fixed order by point id; a move is rejected unless exact `orient2d` stays positive and the local 3-D min angle does not drop), then flips to a fixed point (exact `incircle` when `g.is_isometric()`, otherwise the 3-D opposite-angle-sum `> π`; flip only when both new triangles stay exactly positive in the chart).
  - **Poles** (needed for the full sphere fixture; implemented after chart/seams in the order below): a degenerate coedge (flag `DEGENERATE`, pcurve a horizontal uv segment) is replaced in the chart by a ring of `m = 6` face-owned nodes at geodesic distance `≈ h` from the pole, evenly spaced in `u`. The ring is part of the boundary; the pole fan is `m` explicit triangles `(pole, ring_i, ring_{i+1})`, so no triangle holds the pole node twice. Ring nodes are fixed in `optimize`. Hemisphere and cone-apex cases with no degenerate coedge, and any sphere with `r < 2h`, give `Unsupported`.
  - **Patch assembly:** interior nodes are `NodeKey::new(face, i)`, `i = 0..` in insertion order, with `Param::Surface([u, v])` wrapped into the principal domain; boundary nodes are `PatchBuilder::refer(key)`. Elements are `ElemShape::Tri`, counter-clockwise in the chart (face-normal oriented). Seam duplicates collapse to one key. A triangle with a repeated key gives `FailureKind::Geometry`. Default `h` without a size field = mean 3-D length of the face's boundary segments.
  - **Failure mapping:** `TriError` is a `thiserror` enum (`OpenLoop`, `SelfIntersection`, `Lift{key}`, `TooManyPoints`, `Quality{min_angle, limit}`, and under Stretch `Stuck{front_edges}`). Geometry and lift errors map to `FailureKind::Geometry`, an unsupported pole case to `Unsupported`, and `Quality` to `GenerateFailure::fallback(..).with_nodes(worst triangle nodes)`. Quality gate: `min_angle_3d >= min(fail_angle, 0.9·smallest input corner angle)` and every triangle has a positive 3-D normal dot with `geom::face_normal`.
- **Implementation order:** cdt -> refine -> chart/seams -> poles -> optimize -> (Stretch) front.
- **Acceptance (mandatory; all analytic; "area" sums use `NeumaierSum`):**
  1. Exactness: a cocircular 11×11 lattice, a square with 100 collinear boundary points and a PSLG scaled by 2^±60 all triangulate; every triangle has exact `orient2d == Positive`, and `Cdt::new` returns the same triangle set (by coordinates, up to relabelling) over 3 shuffles of point order.
  2. Delaunay: with `optimize = false`, for every unconstrained interior edge the opposite vertex is not strictly inside the circumcircle (`incircle != Positive`, exact, in chart coordinates) on square, `loops2d::annulus`, `l_shape` and the cylinder lateral face. With `optimize = true` it also holds on planar faces.
  3. Conformity: on `loops2d::{square, rectangle_with_holes, annulus, circle, l_shape}` the boundary edges of the output equal the input segments exactly (as key pairs; none added, none lost). Euler `V − E + F = 1 − holes`. An embedded horizontal chain across a square has every chain node as a vertex and every chain segment as an edge. An embedded isolated vertex is a mesh vertex. Total area equals `signed_area(outer) − Σ signed_area(holes)` within 1e-12 relative.
  4. Angles: unit square with 10 intervals per side, default `min_angle`: all triangles have min angle ≥ 20.0°, `N ∈ [180, 330]` (ideal `A/(√3/4·h²) = 231`), `unfixable` empty. The same 20° bound holds for the disc (n = 64), the annulus (r 1 and 3, n = 48/96), the L-shape (270° corner) and the cylinder lateral face (r = 1, h = 2, `equal` 12 around, 6 along; angles measured in 3-D). If a fixture cannot reach 20° because of boundary encroachment, the worker reports the achieved value and escalates; it does not relax the test silently.
  5. Gate (negative): a 10×0.1 rectangle with 10 intervals on the long sides and 1 on the short ones is forced below 12°, so it returns `FailureKind::Quality` with the achieved angle in the reason and the worst triangle's nodes in `GenerateFailure::nodes`. A `Driver` run with `fallbacks = []` reports that single attempt. (The two-attempt `trimesh` -> `triadvance` message moves to Stretch item S2.)
  6. Curved faces: all nodes lie on the surface (`|project(p) − p| ≤ 1e-12·r`). The cylinder's uv-area sum equals `2π·h` within 1e-13 relative, and its 3-D area is within the derived inscribed-triangle bound, asserted. The full sphere face (r = 1, fixture `sphere`, 16 intervals on the seam) has exactly 6 triangles at each pole, every triangle holds three distinct keys, and `4πr²·(1 − (R_max/r)²) ≤ A ≤ 4πr²`, where `R_max` is the largest 3-D circumradius (derived: a flat triangle with angular circumradius φ has `A_flat/A_sph ≥ cos²φ`). A sphere zone (u ∈ [0, π/2], v ∈ ±π/4; testkit `sphere_zone` or `tests/common`; area `r²(u1−u0)(sin v1 − sin v0)`) has min 3-D angle ≥ 20°. `mesh_valid` and `closed` hold for the sphere surface-only mesh (`ReferenceBuilder::surface()`, `V − E + F = 2`), seam and poles included.
  7. Size fields: with a test-local `SizeField` `h(x) = 0.05 + 0.15x` on the unit square (driver intervals from `geom::edge_size_goal`), `N_ideal = (4/√3)·∫₀¹ dx/h(x)² = (4/√3)·100 ≈ 230.9`; `N ∈ [0.8, 1.6]×N_ideal`; ≥ 90% of interior edges have `len/h(mid) ∈ [0.5, 1.5]` and none exceeds 2.2. An anisotropic `metric()` stretched 4:1 in x gives an edge-length ratio (mean |Δx|/|Δy| over edges) in `[2.5, 5]`.
  8. Driver integration with `sm_scheme_api::stubs::registry()` plus `register` (colliding names removed, see Scope): a surface-only mesh of the six faces of a `brick_setup` brick (`WholeFaces`, `ReferenceBuilder::surface()`; this is a library-level test through `Driver`, not the W3b `mesh.run` op) with `trimesh` forced gives a closed triangle surface, `validate` empty, Euler 2, total area exact to 1e-12, shared edges not duplicated. A planar face with two embedded edges conforms.
  9. Determinism: `sm_testkit::determinism::check` over the pure kernel and the `Driver` (`Sequential` vs `ScopedThreads(4)`, 2 runs, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default): equal `topology_hash` and `geometry_hash`, bit-equal node coordinates. `rg 'HashMap|HashSet' crates/sm-scheme-tri/src` is empty. Hilbert ordering, heap tie-breaks and grid cells use only integer keys.
  10. Performance (release, `#[ignore]`, record-only, note in `DESIGN.md`): a planar 10⁵-triangle face; target < 3 s single-threaded, recorded not gated (decision 12). No per-triangle heap allocation (SoA `Vec<[u32; 3]>`).
  11. Hygiene: no `unwrap` on input paths, rustdoc on every public item, `cargo clippy -p sm-scheme-tri --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `DESIGN.md` with positioning, the pole-fan rationale and the guarantee statement.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-11 pass; ship as `known_gaps` in `DESIGN.md` if late):**
  - S1. `"triadvance"` scheme (score 20, below trimesh), `pub fn advance(p: &Pslg, g: &dyn Geometry2, o: &AdvanceOptions) -> Result<Cdt, TriError>` in module `front`: advancing front in the chart, shortest front edge first (ties by id), ideal apex at `h` along the metric bisector, candidates the ideal point and existing nodes within `1.5h` ranked by 3-D quality, exact `orient2d` intersection tests, deterministic uniform grid (`BTreeMap` cells), ends with `optimize`, returns `TriError::Stuck{front_edges}` when stuck. Registered only if S1 passes; the composer then sets `set_fallback(Surface, "trimesh", &["triadvance"])`, else the fallback list is empty.
  - S2. Acceptance for S1: on square, disc, annulus and cylinder lateral face, min angle ≥ 20°, all conformity properties of item 3 hold, `N ∈ [0.7, 1.6]×N_ideal`; it terminates under an iteration cap of `20·(2·N_boundary + N_ideal)` asserted by test (fails with `Stuck` or `Geometry`, never loops); with `fallbacks = [triadvance]` on the item-5 rectangle both attempts fail and the `Driver` error names both.
- **Guarantee statement (must be in `DESIGN.md` and rustdoc; it is the documented tri -> tet contract of decision 12):** the boundary is fixed. The Ruppert/Chew angle bound holds only where boundary spacing is compatible with `h` and input corners are ≥ 60°. Elsewhere the scheme reports the achieved angle and either accepts (above the gate) or returns `Quality` so the driver can fall back. The documented minimum is 20° where the fixed boundary allows it (not 25°); tetmesh calibrates against 20° input and accepts any valid closed surface. The chart is constrained-Delaunay (exact) with 3-D-measured quality. It is exactly Delaunay-and-isometric on plane and cylinder faces, and approximately so on other curved faces.
- **Deferred to W3b / later:** auto scheme selection and the final score table; `tripave`, `trimap`, `triprimitive`, `circle`, `hole` and other Cubit tri variants; anisotropic boundary-layer triangles; hemisphere, rim-winding and cone-apex faces (need a model-level partition); off-center insertion (Üngör); parallel refinement within a face; curvature-adaptive size functions (sm-sizing); quad-dominant output; the `mesh.run`-on-faces op (surface-only meshing through the op layer); a shared CDT crate with pave (the 2D CDT is duplicated between pave and tri in W3a, by decision).
- **Milestone note:** the integration script sets `mesh.scheme trimesh` explicitly on the faces of the tet-meshed volume (V3); `trimesh` is not on any auto list in W3a.
- **Must NOT:** edit any other crate, `sm-testkit` or `sm-scheme-api` (stage 0 owns them); add Steiner points on boundary edges or claim children; use `HashMap`/`HashSet` iteration; use floating-point predicates for orientation or incircle; depend (even in dev) on a W3a crate still being built, on a tet crate, sm-sizing or sm-mesher; register `"tri"`, fallback chains or auto lists; combine `stubs::registry()` with a colliding real registration.

scheme trimesh (SchemeDim::Surface, Caps::TRI)
scheme tridelaunay (SchemeDim::Surface, Caps::TRI)
scheme triadvance (SchemeDim::Surface, Caps::TRI), only if Stretch S1 is accepted
pub fn register(&mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>
no ops, queries, layers or export formats

#### ACRs
[]
(None. The skeleton, `GenCtx::loop_uvs`, `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`, `topo::{planar_face, annulus_face}` and the sm-quality L2 re-ladder are decision-12 stage-0 items referenced above by name.)

#### fixtures
Stage-0 (by name, no ACR): `sm_testkit::topo::{planar_face(&Region2, ..), annulus_face(r_in, r_out)}` (annulus area pi(r_out^2 - r_in^2)); `sm_testkit::loops2d::{square, rectangle, rectangle_with_holes, annulus, circle, l_shape}`; `sm_topo_api::fixtures::{cylinder (lateral face has Line2 pcurves), sphere (pole coedges have pcurves)}`.
Optional: `sm_testkit::topo::sphere_zone(r, u: [f64;2], v: [f64;2])`; if absent the worker builds it in `tests/common` (4-edge face, two meridian arcs and two latitude circles, Line2 pcurves, no degenerate coedges, area r^2 (u1-u0)(sin v1 - sin v0)). The old `planar_face_with_holes` request is dropped in favour of `planar_face` + `annulus_face`.

#### depends
[]  (nothing in W3a; all dev peers are already on main)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to Shewchuk's Triangle (constrained Delaunay + Ruppert/Chew refinement in `-Y` mode, no Steiner points on boundary segments) and to Gmsh's 2D Delaunay in parameter space (meshGFace), plus Cubit's trimesh/tridelaunay/triadvance names. It differs in four ways. (1) The boundary is a fixed, shared set of NodeKeys from the curve schemes, so conformity with neighbouring faces (and the tet mesh on top) holds by construction. (2) Every orientation and incircle test is an exact sm-math predicate in a power-of-two-normalised chart, and output is bit-identical for any thread count. (3) Quality is measured in 3-D on the surface with the metric JᵀMJ from the SizeField, and the face's pcurves give seam and pole handling (pole fans). (4) An honest, reported guarantee: the angle bound holds where the fixed boundary allows, otherwise the driver gets a Quality failure (with the offending nodes) instead of a silent bad mesh.

#### risks
The angle guarantee cannot be unconditional (fixed boundary nodes). Mitigation: unfixable list, Quality gate, documented 20 degree contract with tetmesh. A 20 degree bound on every listed fixture is a hypothesis the worker must confirm; off-center insertion is the first remedy (deferred, so escalate rather than relax).
Non-constant metric surfaces (sphere zone, cone, torus): chart-Delaunay is only approximate; 3-D quality is enforced by measurement. Wrong lift near seams is the likeliest bug source; `loop_uvs` uses pcurves first.
Pole fan with m = 6 assumes a disc of radius about h around the pole and r >= 2h; otherwise Unsupported.
Sloan segment recovery must handle collinear overlapping segments; near-coincident seam copies that are distinct in uv but equal in 3-D are caught by the repeated-key check plus seam tests.
Advancing front (Stretch) has no guarantee and is last; if it slips, only trimesh/tridelaunay are registered and the fallback list stays empty.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
