Is `triadvance` score 20 acceptable (decision 12 fixes only map 100, submap 80, pave 40, trimesh 30)? It only matters for W3b auto selection.
Default `min_angle` 20 and `fail_angle` 12: right gates for Sn/MOC versus Monte Carlo goals? They are per-call params.
Test-registry recipe for `brick_setup` without colliding stub names: confirm which stub names the real crate would collide with (none for tri, since it does not register `tri`).

### W3-SWEEP. `sm-scheme-sweep` (L3, skeleton exists): 1-to-1 volume sweep (translate, twist, arbitrary linking, rotational)
- **Deps:** sm-core, sm-math (`solve3`, `sym3_eigen`, `det3`), sm-topo-api, sm-mesh-api (`CellShape`), sm-scheme-api, rayon, serde, serde_json, thiserror. Dev (decision 12: acyclic, peer already on main; sm-quality is now L2 so this is an ordinary lower-layer dev-dep): sm-testkit (stage-0 sweep fixtures, see below), sm-scheme-api[stubs] (individual stubs `Equal`, surface `Map`/`Submap`, curve schemes), sm-mesh, sm-mesh-api[serde], sm-model-api, sm-quality, proptest. No dependency on any other W3a crate, dev or normal (in particular not sm-scheme-curve/map/mapvol/pave/tri). Sources for the cylinder, annulus and polygon cases are test-local surface schemes; the real pave/tri/polygon schemes are tied in at integration.
- **Stage-0 items this task uses (they exist on main before the worker starts; reference by name, do not re-specify, do not edit sm-testkit or sm-scheme-api):**
  - sm-testkit sweep fixtures `extruded_l(a,t,h)`, `twisted_prism(side,h,twist)`, `annular_sector(r_in,r_out,h,sweep)`, `hollow_cylinder(r_in,r_out,h)`, `regular_prism(n,r,h)` (decision 12 list; invariants below under "fixtures used").
  - `GenerateFailure::with_nodes(Vec<NodeKey>)` and its serde-default `nodes` field (decision 12). Sweep uses it for every `Quality` failure.
  - sm-quality at L2.
  - If a fixture is not on main when the worker starts, the worker stops with `status: blocked_on_api`; it does not hand-build topology.
- **Scope:** the real `"sweep"` volume scheme, ported and generalised from the test-only stub `Sweep` (`crates/sm-scheme-api/src/stubs.rs` ~1000-1120 and `discover`/`generate_stack` ~472-1000). Read those first. Structure to keep: `Role::{SweepSource,SweepTarget,SweepLinking}`, `Requirements::{role,require_caps,copy,claim,set_data}`, `GenCtx::{edge_nodes,strip_from,point,surface_patch}`, `Patch`/`PatchBuilder`/`Elements::push_polyhedron` (`sm-scheme-api/src/patch.rs`), and the `NodeKey` convention of `copy.rs` (a copied target's node keys are `(target_face, k.index)` for source interior nodes `k`).
  - `pub const NAME: &str = "sweep"; pub struct Sweep; impl Scheme for Sweep` (dim `Volume`, `caps()` = `HEX | WEDGE | POLY`); `pub fn register(reg: &mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>`. The stub also registers `"sweep"`: test registries register the individual stubs they need (`Equal`, `Map`, `Submap`, ...) plus `sm_scheme_sweep::register`, and never call `stubs::registry()` together with it (duplicate-name error). The standard distribution registers only the real crate.
  - **Params** (typed `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` struct `SweepParams`; a bad value gives `SchemeError::BadParams`):
    - `source: [EntityId; 1]?`, `target: [EntityId; 1]?`: both or neither. If neither, search face pairs that discover valid columns, lowest ids first (as the stub does). The planner's `PieceShape::Sweep` hint (`planner.rs` ~1341) passes exactly `{source:[id], target:[id]}`.
    - `target_mode: "auto" | "copy"` (default `"auto"`). `copy`: register `CopyConstraint(source -> target, transform, CopyReason::SweepTarget)` with the vertex map from the columns. `auto` = `copy` when `fit_target_transform` accepts, otherwise `NotApplicable` with the residual in the reason. The value `"project"` is parsed but returns `BadParams("target_mode project is not implemented")` in the mandatory scope (see Stretch).
    - `placement: "auto" | "affine" | "interpolate"` (default `"auto"`: affine, then interpolate if the Jacobian check fails).
    - `shepard_power: f64` (default 3.0, > 0), `support: u32` (k nearest boundary nodes, default 16, 0 = all), `min_scaled_jacobian: f64` (default 0.0: every corner Jacobian must be > 0; a larger value is also enforced), `layers: u32?` (adds an `IntervalSink::linear` row on the first column: the total layer count).
  - **Discovery** `fn discover_columns(...)`: for each source vertex, follow the single non-source/target/interface linking edge to a target vertex (straight-continuation check for composite stacks, dot > 0.9). Supports a single volume, a composite piece (stack of merged volumes, layer count = sum of the step counts) and a source with several loops (holes). Returns `NotApplicable` with a reason when a source vertex has no column, the source and target share a vertex, or the vertex counts differ (many-to-one is W4).
  - **requirements**: roles; `require_caps(linking, QUAD | BLOCK_STRUCTURED, Hard)` on each linking face; the copy constraint; `set_data` stores the discovered columns.
  - **interval_constraints**: all columns have equal total layer count (`equal` rows between corresponding column edges, as `column_equalities`) plus the `layers` row.
  - **Target transform** `pub fn fit_target_transform(src: &[Point3], dst: &[Point3], n_src: Vector3, n_dst: Vector3, tol: f64) -> Option<sm_core::Transform>`: least-squares affine map from the source vertices to the target vertices. Both sets are coplanar, so add one virtual pair `(c_s + l*n_s) -> (c_t - l*n_t)` (outward normals, `l` = mean vertex distance from the centroid). This makes the fit full rank, a translation come out as a pure translation, and a rotation by phi as `Rz(phi)`. Accept only if the max vertex residual is <= tol (1e-9 * source bbox diagonal, a constant: the model tolerance is not reachable through `PlanCtx`) and `det > 0`.
  - **Layer placement** `pub fn place_layers(...)`, one layer `l = 1..L-1` at a time. `B` = source boundary nodes (vertices and edge nodes, sorted by `NodeKey`), `c_b[l]` = their column node at layer `l` (vertex columns from `edge_nodes` along the path; edge-owned nodes by `GenCtx::strip_from` across the linking faces).
    1. *Translation fast path:* if all `c_b[l] - b` agree within 1e-12 * scale, `M = I`, `d = mean` (bit-exact lattices).
    2. *Affine:* `pub fn fit_affine(src: &[Point3], dst: &[Point3]) -> Result<Transform, SweepError>`: centred least squares `M = C B^T (B B^T)^+`, with the pseudo-inverse from `sym3_eigen` of `B B^T` dropping eigenvalues < 1e-10 * max (a planar source has a null normal direction), and `t = c_bar - M b_bar`. Sums run in sorted-key order.
    3. *Residual:* `r_b = c_b[l] - M(b)`; the interior node `x` is placed at `M(x) + sum_j w_j(x) r_j`, `w_j ~ d_j^-p / sum` over the `support` nearest boundary nodes (ties broken by `NodeKey`; uniform-grid k-NN; sparse weights computed once per node and reused for all layers). Boundary nodes are placed exactly.
    4. `interpolate`: skip the affine part: `x + sum w_j (c_j[l] - b_j)`.
  - **Emission**: node slots by sorted `NodeKey`; interior layer node key `(volume, slot*(L-1)+l-1)` (stub convention). Cells are emitted in source-element order, then layer order. `Tri -> Wedge`, `Quad -> Hex`, `Polygon(n>=5) -> Polyhedron` (faces: reversed bottom loop, top loop, n side quads, all outward). Orientation is decided per element from the signed volume at layer 0; the template node order is the one in `sm-mesh-api/src/shape.rs` (bottom ring counter-clockwise from +z, top above). Other source shapes give `FailureKind::Unsupported`.
  - **Quality gate**: after placement every cell's corner Jacobians (Hex 8 corners, Wedge 6; Polyhedron: signed volume and all face-pyramid volumes from the centroid > 0) must exceed `min_scaled_jacobian`. A failure is `FailureKind::Quality` whose reason names the layer, the source element index, the `NodeKey`s and the Jacobian, and which also carries the offending `NodeKey`s through `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`, so the driver can fall back and diagnostics can locate it. Parallel over layers and elements with `rayon`, ordered collects.
  - Public items beyond the scheme: `SweepParams`, `TargetMode`, `Placement`, `SweepError` (thiserror), `fit_affine`, `fit_target_transform`, `place_layers`, each with rustdoc. `DESIGN.md` documents the positioning, the formulas (LS affine, Shepard weights, the volume formulas used by the tests) and the recorded performance number.
- **Fixtures used (stage 0; invariants the worker re-asserts in its own tests before relying on them):** `extruded_l(2,1,2)`: source area 3, volume 6, 8 faces, 12 vertices, 18 edges, 6 four-sided linking faces. `twisted_prism(2,3,pi/2)`: volume side^2 h (2+cos t)/3 = 8, bilinear linking faces, straight column chords. `annular_sector(1,2,1,pi/2)` and `(…,3pi/2)`: volume sweep/2 (r_out^2 - r_in^2) h, source at angle 0 in the xz-plane, target = Rz(sweep) source, two cylinder and two planar linking faces. `hollow_cylinder(1,2,2)`: volume 6 pi, two-loop annuli, seam edges. `regular_prism(6,1,3)`: base area 2.598076211353316, volume 7.794228634059948, 8 faces.
- **Acceptance (mandatory; analytic; all through `Driver::mesh` with the individual stubs `Equal`/`Map`/`Submap` and the reference `MeshBuild`, then `sm_mesh_api::validate` clean):**
  1. Brick 4x3x5 with counts (4,3,5): 60 hexes, 120 nodes, volume 60 to 1e-12, nodes on the integer lattice bit-exact (translation fast path), min scaled Jacobian 1 to 1e-12. Port `sm-scheme-api/tests/brick.rs::sweep_brick_is_analytic` and the fallback/copy/Hard tests (`tests/brick.rs`, `copies.rs`, `claims.rs`) to the real scheme with a registry built from individual stubs; results equal the stub's.
  2. Layer counts from intervals: a column edge with count 7 gives 7 layers; `size 2` on a height-10 brick gives 5; a composite of two merged bricks with column counts 3+4 gives 7 layers; `bias` ratio 1.2 on the column edge gives layer heights with consecutive ratio 1.2 to 1e-12, and interior nodes' z equals the boundary column's z at the same layer to 1e-12. `layers: 6` on a column of two edges forces the linear row. A conflicting hard count gives `Infeasible` naming both.
  3. Extruded L (`extruded_l(2,1,2)`, size 0.5, source meshed by stub `Submap`): 12 source quads x 4 layers = 48 hexes, 21x5 = 105 nodes, volume 6 to 1e-12, min scaled Jacobian 1 to 1e-12; the column at the re-entrant corner is shared by two linking faces and meshes without gaps.
  4. Axial cylinder (`cylinder(1,2)`; test-local triangle-fan source, 16 circle intervals, 4 layers): 64 wedges (true 6-node `Wedge`), 85 nodes, every lateral node within 1e-12 of r = 1, volume = (16/2) sin(2 pi/16) * 2 = 6.122934918 to 1e-12 (inscribed prism), target meshed by copy.
  5. Hollow cylinder (`hollow_cylinder(1,2,2)`; test-local ring-quad source, 16 intervals, 4 layers): 64 hexes, 2x16x5 = 160 nodes, volume = 8 sin(2 pi/16) * 3 * 2 = 18.368804 to 1e-9 relative, all nodes on r = 1 or r = 2 to 1e-12 (source with two loops; exercises the seam `strip_from` path).
  6. Rotational (`annular_sector(1,2,1,pi/2)`, source rectangle 3x4 quads, 9 layers; and sweep = 3pi/2 with 18 layers): 108 resp. 216 hexes; every node's radius equals its source node's to 1e-12; node angle = layer * sweep/L to 1e-12; mesh volume = (L/2)(r_out^2 - r_in^2) h sin(sweep/L) to 1e-12 relative (= 2.3442504 for the 90 degree case), within the relative deficit 1 - sin(x)/x <= x^2/6 (x = sweep/L) of the exact `sweep/2 (r_out^2 - r_in^2) h`; the detected copy transform is `Rz(sweep)` (entries to 1e-12).
  7. Twisted prism (`twisted_prism(2,3,pi/2)`, 4x4 source quads, 6 layers): the node at layer l equals `((1-t) I + t Rz(pi/2)) x` with `z = 3t`, `t = l/6`, to 1e-12 (zero residual; copy transform `Rz(pi/2)`); mesh volume = 8 to 1e-12 relative by exact 2x2x2 Gauss integration of the trilinear Jacobian; all Jacobians > 0; the measured min scaled Jacobian is recorded in DESIGN.md.
  8. Polyhedral prism (`regular_prism(6,1,3)`; test-local one-`Polygon`-element source, 3 layers): 3 `Polyhedron` cells, each with V = 12, E = 18, F = 8; total volume 7.794228634059948 to 1e-12; `validate` clean, including outward face orientation.
  9. `fit_affine` unit tests: recovers a random affine map (det > 0) from 6+ points to 1e-12; a planar point set (null normal) returns the in-plane map; fewer than 4 non-collinear points returns `SweepError`. `fit_target_transform` tests: a pure translation returns a bit-exact translation; a rotation by phi returns `Rz(phi)` to 1e-12; a non-affine vertex correspondence returns `None`.
  10. `place_layers` unit tests on synthetic data (this exercises the Shepard path that no fixture needs): with a uniform boundary residual `r` every interior node shifts by exactly `r` (1e-12, partition of unity); boundary nodes are placed exactly; `interpolate` and `affine` agree when the residual is zero; ties are broken by `NodeKey` (permuting the input order leaves the output bit-identical).
  11. Failure modes: no pair of faces gives columns -> `NotApplicable` with the reason; a non-affine target in `auto` -> `NotApplicable` naming the residual; a source with a hanging non-quad/tri/polygon element -> `Unsupported`; an inverted layer (forced test hook with a 170 degree bend through `place_layers`) -> `Quality` whose reason carries layer, element and node keys, whose `nodes` field equals those keys, and the driver records the attempt and falls back.
  12. Determinism: `sm_testkit::determinism::check` over a 40x40 (stub `Submap`) x 25 layers L-prism (40k hexes, above the rayon threshold); `Sequential` vs `ScopedThreads(4)` vs a one-thread rayon pool give bit-identical node coordinates and cell lists; the whole suite is also run under `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`.
  13. proptest: for twist in [0, 150] degrees, side in [0.5, 3], h in [0.5, 4], layers 1..8, source m x m with m in 1..6: volume = side^2 h (2 + cos twist)/3 to 1e-10 relative and all Jacobians > 0.
  14. Performance, record-only (the bulk mesh commit is W3b, decision 12): a 100x100 source x 100 layers sweep (10^6 hexes), `generate` time on 4 threads, reference 4.7 s driver-only; report the number and the commit-bound share in DESIGN.md. Not a gate.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-14 pass; may slip to W3b without blocking the wave):**
  - `target_mode: "project"` and the `auto` fallback to it: `requirements` calls `out.claim(Target::Surface(target))`, caps add `MESHES_CHILDREN`, generate writes the target patch from the last layer (nodes snapped with `geom::snap_to_face`, keys `(target, k.index)`). Acceptance S1: a 2x2x3 brick whose target edges carry a geometric bias (ratio 1.5, same counts) with `target_mode: "project"`: the target face is written by the sweep (claimed), all its nodes on z = 3 to 1e-12, boundary nodes equal the edge nodes, total volume 12 to 1e-12, all Jacobians > 0; `target_mode: "copy"` on the same input gives a copy conflict or a `Verify` mismatch error, never a silent bad mesh.
- **Deferred to W3b/W4:** full 360-degree revolve (source and target the same face) and pole columns on the rotation axis (needs a topology decision on periodic revolve and degenerate linking faces in `TopologyView`); many-to-one, many-to-many and multisweep (W4); smoothing (a `MeshTransform`); auto scheme selection and a registry fallback for sweep (composer's choice, W3b); a model-tolerance-based copy tolerance (needs a `PlanCtx` accessor).
- **Must NOT:** edit sm-scheme-api or sm-testkit (the stubs stay; they test the driver); register ops, queries or layers; depend on sm-mesher, sm-quality (non-dev) or any other W3a crate; mesh the source face, claim the linking faces, or smooth; implement any item in "Deferred"; tune cells to any exporter or external tool; combine `stubs::registry()` with the real `register`; use `HashMap` iteration order or time/thread-based randomness; use `unwrap()` on input-reachable paths.

#### registers
scheme "sweep" (SchemeDim::Volume), caps HEX | WEDGE | POLY, via `sm_scheme_sweep::register(&mut SchemeRegistry)`. No ops, queries, layers or export formats. User entry is sm-mesher's existing `mesh.scheme {targets, scheme:"sweep", params:{...}}`; the planner's `PieceShape::Sweep` hint already emits `{source:[ids], target:[ids]}`, which this scheme accepts unchanged. Volume auto lists and W2 hashes are unchanged.

#### ACRs
None. All contract work this task needs is in the decision 12 stage-0 list: the five sweep fixtures and `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`.

#### depends
[] (stage 0 only)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to Cubit's 1-to-1 `sweep` volume scheme and to Roca-Sarrate-Huerta layer placement (affine map fitted on the boundary, residual interpolated by Shepard weights). Differences: (1) constraint-first inside the sm-scheme-api driver: it imposes QUAD|BLOCK_STRUCTURED on linking faces, declares roles, registers a copy constraint for the target, contributes column interval equalities to the global integer solver, and generates as a pure function of the solved plan. (2) Exact cell shapes: Quad -> Hex, Tri -> 6-node Wedge, Polygon -> prismatic Polyhedron, so a polygonal source gives polyhedral cells for the in-house Sn/MOC/Monte Carlo code, which Cubit cannot do. (3) Twist and rotational sweeps need no special code: layers are affine images of the source, so placement is exact and analytically testable. (4) Bit-identical output for 1 vs N threads with canonical node keys. Gaps against Cubit in W3a: no projected (non-copy) target and no pole columns (see Stretch/Deferred).

#### risks
- `strip_from` on linking faces: the cylinder seam (an edge used twice by one face) and lateral faces with two Circle-arc column edges are untested paths. Fallback: `NodeGrid::line_from` through the face grid. The worker adds a unit test on the stub `Map` for the seam case before relying on it (acceptance 5 depends on it).
- The affine+Shepard placement can invert cells for strongly non-affine linking surfaces (long sweeps bending 180 degrees or more, sharp re-entrant source features). The scheme fails with `FailureKind::Quality` so the driver can fall back; it does not smooth. Since `project` is Stretch, non-affine targets are `NotApplicable` in `auto` in the mandatory scope.
- Shepard weights are O(N_interior * k); the weights are computed once per source node, not per layer. Perf is record-only.
- Polyhedron prism side faces are non-planar when the swept polygon twists. `validate` only checks declared planar faces, but exporters (OpenFOAM) may flag face warp. That is an exporter-conformance question, not a sweep bug.
- `auto` copy detection decides on a geometric affine fit of the vertex correspondence. A false positive (affine-equivalent vertices, differently meshed edges) makes the planner report a copy conflict; the remedy is `project` (Stretch), so until then the user gets a clear `NotApplicable`/conflict message.
- Test-local source schemes (triangle fan, ring quads, one polygon) stand in for pave/tri/polygon until integration; their node numbering must be deterministic.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
- Is the constant 1e-9 * bbox-diagonal copy tolerance acceptable, or should the model tolerance reach schemes through `PlanCtx`? (Constant used meanwhile.)
- Does W3 need a solid-cylinder revolve (pole columns)? Deferred to W3b; it needs a topology decision first.
- Should the distribution register a registry fallback for sweep (e.g. to the tet scheme)? Composer's choice, not specified here.

#### escalation
Stop with `blocked_on_api` if a stage-0 fixture or `GenerateFailure::with_nodes` is missing from main. Otherwise none.

### W3-TET. `sm-scheme-tet` (L3, skeleton exists): tetrahedral volume mesher (Delaunay + recovery + refinement + optimization)
- **Deps (decisions 9, 11, 12):** internal runtime: sm-core, sm-math (`orient3d`, `insphere`, `try_orient3d`, `sos::{insphere_sos, orient3d_sos, Tagged}`), sm-topo-api, sm-mesh-api (`CellShape::Tet`, `Param`), sm-scheme-api. External: rayon (deterministic use only), serde, thiserror. Dev: sm-testkit, sm-scheme-api[stubs], sm-mesh, sm-quality (L2, on main), sm-mesh-api (`validate`, `cell_volume`). You may edit your own `crates/sm-scheme-tet/Cargo.toml` (the skeleton has only sm-scheme-api). Must NOT depend (normal or dev) on sm-scheme-tri, sm-scheme-pave or any other W3a crate still being built; the surface fixtures come from sm-testkit and the TRI surface scheme used in adapter tests is a test-local one in `tests/`.
- **Stage-0 items this task relies on (by name; already on main or being added, do NOT request or re-implement them):** the skeleton crate `sm-scheme-tet` (no root `Cargo.toml` edit; members = `crates/*`); the `sm_testkit::surf3` fixtures (`tri_cube`, `icosphere`, `tri_torus`, `cube_with_cavity`, `two_cubes_shared`, `schonhardt`, `noisy_sphere`, `open_cube_missing_tri`, `SurfaceFixture`, `VolumeSurface`, `oriented_tris`); `GenerateFailure::with_nodes` (serde default); the `tet_block` family is not needed here. If a stage-0 signature differs from what this entry assumes, adapt to the landed one. If something you need is missing, stop and return `blocked_on_api`; never add duplicates of it.
- **Convention traps (read first):**
  - `sm_math::orient3d(a,b,c,d) > 0` when `d` is *below* the plane of CCW-from-above `a,b,c`. The `CellShape::Tet` template (`sm-mesh-api/src/shape.rs:9`) has apex 3 *above* 0,1,2, so a positive-volume template tet has `orient3d(p0,p1,p2,p3) < 0`. Pin this on day one with a test against `sm_testkit::meshes::single_tet` and `sm_mesh_api::cell_volume`.
  - `insphere` is positive for "inside" only if `orient3d(a,b,c,d) > 0`. Keep all internal tets in one orientation and wrap in one function `in_sphere(t, p)`.
  - Face use `Side::Left` means the face normal points OUT of the volume; `Side::Right` means INTO it (`sm-topo-api/src/types.rs:89`). Void shells (ring > 0, `ShellKind::Void`) use the same rule, so their outward-from-volume normals point into the cavity.
- **Scope A, pure core (no scheme-api types in signatures; this is what the tests drive):**
  - `pub struct SurfaceInput { pub points: Vec<[f64;3]>, pub tris: Vec<[u32;3]> }`. Triangles are wound so the right-hand normal points OUT of the region to mesh. Shells are derived (edge-connected components); outer and cavity shells both allowed. Boundary vertices and triangles are *frozen*.
  - `pub struct TetOptions { size: SizeSpec, radius_edge: f64 /*2.0*/, gradation: f64 /*1.3*/, optimize: u8 /*0..=2, default 2; see Stretch for 3*/, max_steiner: usize /*default 8 * n_input_points + 100_000*/, min_dihedral_goal_deg: f64 /*15.0*/ }` with `Default`. `pub enum SizeSpec<'a> { FromBoundary, Constant(f64), Field(&'a (dyn Fn([f64;3]) -> f64 + Sync)) }`.
  - `pub fn tetrahedralize(input: &SurfaceInput, opts: &TetOptions) -> Result<TetMesh, TetError>`.
  - `pub struct TetMesh { pub points: Vec<[f64;3]> /* first n_input are the input points, bit-identical */, pub n_input: usize, pub tets: Vec<[u32;4]> /* template order, positive volume */, pub stats: TetStats }`. `TetStats { n_steiner, flips, smooth_passes, unrefinable: usize, min_dihedral_deg, max_dihedral_deg, max_radius_edge, min_mean_ratio, mean_mean_ratio, stage_ms: [f64;5] }` (`stage_ms` is excluded from every hash and equality).
  - `pub fn delaunay(points: &[[f64;3]]) -> Result<TetMesh, TetError>`: stage 2 alone (convex hull tetrahedralization), exposed for tests and benches.
  - `TetError` (thiserror, `#[non_exhaustive]`; every variant carries indices so the adapter can locate it): `NotClosed{edge:[u32;2]}`, `NonManifold{edge}`, `DuplicatePoint{a,b}`, `InvertedShell{tri}`, `DegenerateTri{tri}`, `RecoveryFailed{tri:u32, edge:Option<[u32;2]>, reason}`, `SelfIntersection{tri_a,tri_b}` (best effort: used when recovery proves two input triangles cross), `SteinerBudget{limit}`, `BadOption(String)`, `OutOfRange(PredicateError)`, `Internal(String)`. Never panic on any input.
  - Stages, all deterministic:
    1. **Validate:** every undirected edge has exactly two tris used in opposite directions; unique coordinates; each shell has the right signed volume sign (outer > 0, cavity < 0 w.r.t. the enclosing region); `try_orient3d` range check; `optimize` in 0..=2 else `BadOption`.
    2. **Delaunay by Bowyer-Watson with a ghost vertex** (no bounding super-tet, so the hull is exact). Points ordered by BRIO rounds plus a Hilbert key of quantized coordinates (seed from `n` only). Walk location from the last inserted tet. `insphere_sos`/`orient3d_sos` with `Tagged` id = point index, so the result is unique and independent of insertion order. SoA layout (`verts: Vec<[u32;4]>`, `nbr: Vec<[u32;4]>`, free list), reusable cavity buffers, no per-insertion heap allocation.
    3. **Boundary recovery, interior Steiner points only** (the surface mesh is shared by neighbouring volumes and by the tri patch, so boundary points and triangles are never moved, split or added). Order: (a) missing edges by flip sequences (2-3, 3-2, 4-4, Si's crossing-edge removal); (b) missing triangles by removing the crossed tets and re-tetrahedralizing the two cavities (gift wrapping with an empty-sphere preference); (c) if a cavity cannot be filled (Schönhardt-like), insert an interior Steiner point inside it (centroid, then kernel search) and star it; at most 3 tries, then `RecoveryFailed` naming the triangle and the edge.
    4. **Classify:** flood fill from the ghost tets, toggling in/out when crossing a frozen triangle against or along its normal. Check orientation consistency (a contradiction gives `InvertedShell`). Drop outside tets. Postcondition, asserted in debug and tested: each input triangle is a face of exactly one kept tet, on its inner side.
    5. **Refine:** queue of tets with circumradius/shortest-edge > `radius_edge` or oversize (`R > 0.75 * h(centroid)`). The priority key is `(-badness, sorted vertex tuple)`, never a slot id. Insert the circumcenter by a constrained cavity (grown only through unfrozen faces, star-shape check). If the point lies outside the region, is closer than `0.3*h` to a vertex, or breaks star-shape, retry on the segment centroid-to-circumcenter at t = 1/2, 1/4, else count the tet in `unrefinable`. Size: `FromBoundary` is `h_v` = mean incident boundary edge length, interpolated by the containing tet's corners with `h(p) = min_c (h_c + (gradation-1)*|p-c|)`. A user field is combined by `min`.
    6. **Optimize** (level 0 none; 1 flips; 2 flips + smoothing): flips maximise the minimum mean ratio `12*(3V)^(2/3)/sum(l^2)` of the affected tets and never remove a frozen face or edge; smoothing moves *interior* vertices only (Laplacian blend plus backtracking line search accepted only if the local min mean ratio rises and every incident `orient3d` stays strictly valid). Smoothing runs by colour class: greedy colouring in vertex-index order, one class at a time in parallel with rayon, results written in index order.
    7. **Canonical output:** drop unused Steiner points keeping creation order, rotate each tet by the lexicographically smallest even permutation, sort tets lexicographically.
  - Parallelism is deterministic by construction: insertion and recovery are sequential; rayon is used for quality evaluation, queue seeding, colour-class smoothing and output sorting (fixed-order reductions via `sm_math::sum`).
- **Scope B, scheme adapter:**
  - `pub struct TetVolume;` with `impl Scheme` (name `"tetmesh"`, `dim = Volume`, `caps = Caps::TET`; boundary requirement `Caps::TRI`) and `pub fn register(reg: &mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>`. It registers no ops or layers. Params `TetParams { size?: f64, radius_edge?: f64, optimize?: u8, gradation?: f64 }` as a serde struct inside `Params`. Unknown keys or bad values give `NotApplicable` with a reason. The scheme name `tetmesh` does not collide with a stub; tests must still never combine `stubs::registry()` with a real crate that reuses a stub name (decision 12): build the test registry by hand.
  - `applicability`: score 10 (last-resort; auto lists stay unchanged in W3a, so no W2 hash moves). `NotApplicable` if the piece has a `ShellKind::Internal` shell, `Side::Both` uses, embedded members (`MeshInput::embedded_edges/vertices` non-empty) or a non-closed shell.
  - `requirements`: `out.require_caps(Target::Surface(f), Caps::TRI, Firmness::Hard)` for each `input.piece_faces(target)`. The milestone satisfies this by setting `mesh.scheme trimesh` on the tetmesh volumes' faces explicitly (decision 12); auto lists are not touched.
  - `generate`: for each face use read `Incidence.side` from `topo.boundary(volume)` (`Left`: keep the winding of `cx.surface_patch(f)` triangles, `Right`: flip). Do not infer orientation from geometry. Collect every triangle corner as a `NodeKey` via `Patch::key(local)`, assign dense point ids in ascending `NodeKey` order, and fetch coordinates with `cx.point(key)`. Call `tetrahedralize` with `size = cx.size` if present. Emit a `PatchBuilder` patch: Steiner points as `pb.node(NodeKey::new(owner, k), p, Param::None)` with `owner` = the piece id, boundary points as `pb.refer(key)`, tets as `ElemShape::Cell(CellShape::Tet)` in template order. A `TetError` becomes `GenerateFailure::fatal(...).with_nodes(keys of the offending triangle's corners)` (stage-0 `with_nodes`); the message names the `TopoFace` that owns the triangle (via the node key owner), the location, and a suggested action (for example "refine intervals on curves of Face#12" for `RecoveryFailed`).
  - Input contract: any valid closed oriented surface is accepted (decision 12); the adapter never assumes a surface-quality guarantee beyond validity. Recovery and refinement thresholds are calibrated on inputs with minimum angle about 20 degrees (the tri scheme's documented target where the boundary allows it), not 25.
- **Acceptance** (numbered; all analytic or exact; tests live in `tests/`; fixtures from `sm_testkit::surf3`; no sm-scheme-tri at build time). Items 1-11 are mandatory; item 12 is recorded, not gated.
  1. `delaunay` of 2000 seeded random points plus the 8 cube corners: for every tet and every other vertex `insphere` is not Positive (exact, brute force); total volume equals 1 within 1e-12 relative; permuting the input order and re-running (with ids carried by coordinates) gives the identical canonical tet set.
  2. `tri_cube(4)` (192 tris): output volume 1 within 1e-12 relative; every input triangle is a face of exactly one tet with the tet on the inner side (exact `orient3d`); the first 98 points are bit-identical to the input; every tet has strictly valid exact orientation; the mesh built with `sm_testkit::meshes::build_mesh` passes `sm_mesh_api::validate` (no violations).
  3. `icosphere(1.0, level 3)` (642 points, 1280 tris): volume equals the polyhedron volume from the divergence theorem within 1e-12 relative, and lies in [0.95, 1.0] of 4/3*pi. `tri_torus(2.0, 0.7, 48, 24)`: polyhedron volume within 1e-12, within 3% of 2*pi^2*R*r^2, and the boundary is preserved exactly (non-convex, genus 1).
  4. `cube_with_cavity(n=4)` (outer side 2, inner side 1): volume 7 within 1e-12; no tet centroid lies inside the cavity; both shells' tris are each a face of exactly one tet.
  5. `two_cubes_shared(n=4)`: meshing each volume separately gives 2.0 total within 1e-12, and the shared interface tris are faces of exactly one tet in each volume, with bit-identical interface points (the union passes `sm_testkit::check::watertight` and `mesh_valid`).
  6. `schonhardt()` yields >= 1 interior Steiner point, an exact boundary and exact volume. `noisy_sphere(seed=1)` (non-Delaunay surface) preserves all tris; `n_steiner` and `unrefinable` are reported.
  7. Size control: `tri_cube(10)` (edge 0.1) with `Constant(0.1)` gives N tets in [4000, 20000] and >= 99% of tets with longest edge <= 1.6h; the graded field `h = 0.1 + 0.3*x` on `tri_cube(4)` gives adjacent-tet size ratio <= 1.5 (gradation 1.3 plus tolerance).
  8. Quality floors on `tri_cube(10)`, `icosphere(1.0,4)`, `tri_torus`. These numbers are provisional: do one calibration pass first, record the measured values in `DESIGN.md`, then freeze; a floor that cannot be met is escalated, never loosened. `optimize=0`: >= 98% of tets have radius-edge <= 2.0 and `unrefinable <= 0.5%`. Default `optimize=2`: min dihedral >= 10 deg, max dihedral <= 165 deg, min mean ratio >= 0.10, average mean ratio >= 0.75, and min mean ratio never below the `optimize=0` value (monotone). Cross-check mean ratio and radius-edge against `sm_quality` `RadiusRatio` / `AspectFrobenius` on the same mesh (agreement to 1e-9 after the documented metric mapping).
  9. Errors, never a panic: one triangle removed (`open_cube_missing_tri`) gives `NotClosed` naming the edge; a duplicated point gives `DuplicatePoint`; all tris flipped gives `InvertedShell`; an edge shared by 4 tris gives `NonManifold`; `optimize=3` gives `BadOption`; two interpenetrating cubes as one soup give `RecoveryFailed` or `SelfIntersection` and never an invalid mesh. A proptest perturbing the vertices of `icosphere(1,2)` radially by up to 30% gives `Ok` + valid or a typed `Err`. Coordinates beyond the predicate range give `OutOfRange`.
  10. Adapter through `sm_scheme_api::Driver` on `brick_setup` (stub `equal` curves, a test-only surface scheme in `tests/` that splits the stub grid into triangles, registered as `Caps::TRI`): a 10-brick with size 2 meshes, `validate` is clean, the boundary faces of the cells equal the surface patch (count and node keys), the classification is complete, `Delta`-level counts are stable, and `Sequential` vs `ScopedThreads(4)` give byte-identical state. A brick with an embedded edge or a baffle is `NotApplicable`. A forced `RecoveryFailed` message names a `TopoFace` id and the failure carries the triangle's `NodeKey`s via `with_nodes`.
  11. Determinism (`sm_testkit::determinism::check`): hash of `(points bits, tets, n_steiner, flips)` is equal over 3 runs, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default, and with a shuffled `NodeKey` insertion order in the adapter; `stage_ms` excluded.
  12. Performance, RECORD-ONLY (criterion bench plus an `#[ignore]` release test, 4 vCPU; no gate, because 1M-tet end-to-end depends on the bulk mesh commit that is W3b, decision 12): measure and write into `DESIGN.md` `delaunay` of 200k random points (points/s) and the full pipeline (refine + `optimize=2`) on an icosphere-bounded ball producing >= 1M tets (tets/s, with the per-stage split, measured on the core `tetrahedralize` and separately through the adapter). Expectation for context only: ~100k points/s and ~40k tets/s, the order of TetGen single-thread; no claim against parallel HXT.
- **Docs:** `DESIGN.md` states the boundary-preservation decision, the shell/orientation convention, the quality definitions (dihedral, radius-edge, mean ratio) next to the `sm_quality` metric they cross-check, the calibration numbers, the perf record, the positioning against TetGen, HXT and fTetWild, the 20-degree input assumption, and the Stretch and Deferred lists.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-11 pass; in this order):** (S1) edge removal (Klincsek DP) and interior-vertex collapse for tets with min dihedral < `min_dihedral_goal_deg`, exposed as `optimize=3` (until then `3` is `BadOption`); (S2) a quality gate of min dihedral >= 15 deg at `optimize=3` on the acceptance-8 fixtures; (S3) a non-ignored perf smoke test with a generous bound. Cut order if time runs short: S1/S2 are already cut; then simplify smoothing to plain Laplacian with the validity check; never cut flips, the recovery fallback or acceptance 1-11.
- **Deferred to W3b or later:** boundary Steiner points with coordinated surface re-meshing (needs a driver retry hook, contract change); anisotropic metric fields; embedded wires and points; internal baffles; parallel (HXT-style) insertion; sliver exudation with weighted Delaunay; neighbour adjacency exposure on `TetMesh`; higher-order tets; tet-to-polyhedron dual (sm-poly consumes the committed mesh through the mesh IR via `mesh.to_poly`, not `TetMesh`); 1M-tet perf gate (needs bulk commit); auto scheme selection.
- **Must NOT:** add, split or move boundary points or triangles; use `HashMap`/`HashSet` iteration order, thread or time seeds; use `unsafe`; use `unwrap` on input-reachable paths; depend on sm-scheme-tri or any other W3a crate still being built, or sm-mesher; register ops or layers; implement anisotropic metrics, embedded curves/points, baffles, periodic or curved-geometry projection; edit `sm-testkit`, `sm-scheme-api`, the root `Cargo.toml` or docs outside your crate; add duplicate fixtures of the stage-0 `surf3` set.

#### registers
scheme 'tetmesh' (SchemeDim::Volume, Caps::TET, requires Caps::TRI on boundary faces, provisional score 10, not in any auto list) via sm_scheme_tet::register(&mut SchemeRegistry); no ops, queries, layers or export formats

#### ACRs
[]
(None. The skeleton, the `sm_testkit::surf3` fixtures and `GenerateFailure::with_nodes` are stage-0 items from decision 12, referenced by name above.)

#### fixtures
Consumed from stage-0 `sm_testkit::surf3` (no new fixtures requested): tri_cube(n) [6n^2+2 pts, 12n^2 tris, vol 1], icosphere(r, level) [level 3: 642/1280, level 4: 2562/5120], tri_torus(R, r, nu, nv) [R=2, r=0.7, 48x24: 1152/2304, genus 1], cube_with_cavity(n) [vol 7, two shells, cavity tris wound toward the cavity], two_cubes_shared(n) [shared interface ids, each volume 1], schonhardt() [6 pts, 8 tris], noisy_sphere(seed) [icosphere level 3, radial 1+0.1u, SplitMix64], open_cube_missing_tri(n). The reference volumes are polyhedron volumes by divergence theorem.

#### depends
[]

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to TetGen (Delaunay insertion, then constrained boundary recovery, then Delaunay refinement and flip/smooth optimization) and to Gmsh Delaunay/HXT (the quality comparator for the W3 milestone). It differs in four ways. (1) The boundary is frozen: TetGen splits boundary facets and edges with Steiner points, HXT and fTetWild also modify or approximate the surface, while we add only interior points (decision 12), so every neighbouring volume and the tri patch stay conforming by construction. The caveat is that recovery can fail on bad surface meshes, and it then fails with a located, actionable error. (2) Exact predicates with SoS (sm-math) on a ghost-vertex Delaunay structure make the result unique and independent of insertion order, where TetGen uses Shewchuk predicates with a bounding tetrahedron. (3) Bit-identical output for 1 and N threads is a hard requirement, so insertion is sequential and parallelism sits in smoothing and evaluation (HXT is faster on many cores but not bit-reproducible). (4) It is driven by the scheme framework: size fields from GenCtx, classification and node keys from the driver, failures reported as GenerateFailure with face and node keys for MCP/CLI diagnostics. W3a goal: TetGen-class single-thread throughput (recorded, not gated) with Gmsh-comparable quality (min dihedral >= 10 deg after optimize=2; >= 15 deg is a stretch with optimize=3).

#### risks
- Boundary recovery with no boundary Steiner points is the hardest part: flips plus cavity retetrahedralization can fail on skinny or non-Gabriel surface meshes. Mitigation: interior Steiner fallback, a strict typed error naming the triangle, the noisy_sphere and schonhardt fixtures. Integration risk (untested here): if the tri scheme's surfaces are worse than its documented 20 degrees, recovery failures show up in the milestone; the thresholds here are calibrated on 20 degree input and the failure message names the face.
- The radius-edge bound cannot be proved with a frozen boundary. Acceptance 8 numbers are empirical: calibrate once, record, freeze; escalate rather than loosen.
- Sequential Bowyer-Watson limits throughput on 4 vCPU; perf is record-only, and level 2 is the default. The independent-volume parallelism comes from the driver.
- Sign conventions (orient3d vs the Tet template, insphere sign, Side::Left) are easy to get wrong; mitigated by the first-day tests against single_tet and cell_volume.
- Scope is large for one session; cut order is in Stretch (edge removal / collapse are already out of the mandatory set).
- sm-math predicates are exact only for |x| in [2^-100, 2^100]; the adapter surfaces `OutOfRange` as a typed failure.
- V2|V3 shared triangle in the milestone: tet needs TRI on V3's faces while the neighbour is a prism sweep; this interface is tested only in the milestone, not here (acceptance 5 covers the two-tet-volume case).

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
- Minimum distance of interior Steiner points to the boundary: current rule is >= 0.3h from any vertex; should the distance to boundary triangles also be enforced?
- Is `Param::None` right for interior nodes of a PartitionCell piece and does Driver commit accept nodes keyed on a PartitionCell owner? (Resolve in acceptance 10; if not, report `blocked_on_api`.)
- Does the final surf3 constructor set match this entry's names? (Adapt on landing.)

#### escalation
Decisions for the orchestrator: (1) the quality floors in acceptance 8 are calibrated by estimation; allow one calibration pass and have the Opus reviewer confirm them against Gmsh on the same fixtures (pip, see docs/design/w3/gmsh-polyhedra.md). (2) The milestone script must set `mesh.scheme trimesh` on the tetmesh volumes' faces (decision 12); integration owns that. No contract changes are requested.

### W3-SMOOTH. `sm-smooth` (L3, new): classification-constrained smoothing suite

- **Deps (internal crates only; decision 9 applies):** sm-core, sm-math, sm-geom-api, sm-topo-api, sm-model-api, sm-mesh-api, sm-quality (runtime; legal, sm-quality is L2 per decision 12), sm-op-api, sm-layer-api, rayon, serde, schemars, thiserror. Dev: sm-testkit, sm-mesh, sm-scheme-api[stubs], sm-mesher (on main since W2, acyclic, dev only, to make a real `mesh.run` mesh), serde_json, criterion. No dependency (normal or dev) on any W3a crate still being built; sm-testkit fixtures decouple them. Reads `crates/sm-quality/src/{metric,driver}.rs`, `sm-mesh-api/src/{read,build,classify}.rs`, `sm-layer-api/src/mesh.rs`, `sm-topo-api/src/view.rs`, `sm-model-api/src/boundary.rs`.
- **Stage-0 items used (reference by name, not ACRs):**
  - `MeshLayer::replace_mesh(mesh, host, alloc_after)` (decision 12 list). It is the write path of `mesh.smooth`. Fallback if it is not on main when the worker starts: `MeshLayer::get_mut` + `clone_host()` + assigning the pub `MeshEntry.mesh` (both exist today). No `MeshEntry::edit` is requested.
  - `sm_testkit::lattice::{hex, tet_kuhn, quad, tube_quad, perturb}` and `LatticeFixture { topo, model, mesh, exact, h }` (the lattice and tet_block fixtures from the decision 12 list). These are REQUIRED, not optional: the worker does not rebuild them in `tests/common`. If a name or invariant differs from this entry at start, use what stage 0 delivered and record the mapping in `DESIGN.md`; if a fixture is missing entirely, return `blocked`.
  - Already on main: `sm_testkit::topo::mirror_chamber`, `sm_topo_api::fixtures::{brick, cylinder}`, `sm_mesh_api::reference::hex_block`, `sm_testkit::determinism::check_f64_bits`, `Session::state_at_from_root`.
- **Who registers `mesh.smooth`:** sm-smooth itself, through `SmoothPlugin`. The plugin registers no layer, needs `CoreLayers` installed first and writes only the `meshes` layer. Wiring `SmoothPlugin` into `sm_server::standard()` is an integration item (D3 distribution list), not part of this task.
- **Library (pure, no engine needed):**
  - `pub struct SmoothInput<'a> { pub mesh: &'a dyn MeshRead, pub topo: &'a dyn TopologyView, pub model: &'a Model }`
  - `pub struct SmoothRequest { pub nodes: NodeSelect, pub method: Method, pub iterations: u32, pub tolerance: f64, pub schedule: Schedule, pub fix_boundary: bool, pub guard_floor: f64 }`, with `Default` (10 iterations, `tolerance` 1e-6, `Colored`, `fix_boundary` true, `guard_floor` 1e-3).
  - `pub enum NodeSelect { All, Entities(Vec<EntityId>), Vertices(Vec<VertexIx>) }`.
  - `#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Method { Laplacian{relax}, SmartLaplacian{relax}, CentroidArea{relax}, Optimize{objective: Objective, p: f64} }`. `relax` is in (0, 2) and defaults to 1. `p` is in [1, 32] and defaults to 4. Stretch variant `Winslow{relax}` is added only if delivered (see Stretch).
  - `#[non_exhaustive] pub enum Objective { MeanRatio }` (stretch adds `Condition`) and `pub enum Schedule { Jacobi, Colored }`.
  - `pub fn smooth(input: &SmoothInput<'_>, req: &SmoothRequest) -> Result<SmoothOutcome, SmoothError>`.
  - `SmoothOutcome { points: Vec<[f64; 3]> (new position of every vertex), classes: Vec<(VertexIx, Classification)> (refreshed Param for moved edge and face nodes), report: SmoothReport }`.
  - `pub fn apply_outcome(m: &mut dyn MeshEdit, o: &SmoothOutcome) -> Result<(), SmoothError>` runs `set_point` for each moved vertex, then one `set_classes`.
  - `SmoothReport { iterations_run, converged, max_move_last, movable, pinned: BTreeMap<String, usize> (reason to count), accepted, rejected_guard, rejected_geometry, before: QualityStats, after: QualityStats, worst_after: Vec<CellIx> (at most 10) }`.
  - `QualityStats { min_shape, mean_shape, max_condition, n_inverted, n_degenerate }`, computed with `sm_quality::compute_many` and `validity`.
  - `SmoothError` (thiserror): `Unsupported(&'static str)`, `BadParams(String)`, `UnknownEntity(EntityId)`, `NothingToSmooth`, `Mesh(MeshError)`.
- **Constraint table (per node, from `mesh.vertex_class`; the stored `Param` is the projection hint, never a thread-dependent source):**

  | Classified on | Motion |
  |---|---|
  | `TopoVertex` | fixed |
  | `TopoEdge` | candidate projected with `ParametricCurve::project(p, Some(t))`; reject if `t` leaves `CurveCarrier.range` |
  | `TopoFace` | `ParametricSurface::project(p, Some(uv))`, then `classify_point_on_face`; reject if `uv` lands outside the trimmed face |
  | `TopoVolume` | free in R³ |
  | `Partition*`, composite or implicit-only carriers | pinned, reason `unprojectable` |

  - Curves and surfaces are resolved through `topo.carrier(e)` and `topo.shapes().{curve,surface}`.
  - **In-entity neighbours.** A node on an edge or face smooths only against nodes joined to it by a mesh edge of an in-entity mesh face. The neighbour's classified entity must lie in the closure of the node's entity (computed through `topo.boundary`). Interior nodes use every edge neighbour.
  - **Selection.**
    - A listed `TopoVolume` selects the nodes classified on it.
    - A listed `TopoFace` or `TopoEdge` selects its nodes (explicit, regardless of `fix_boundary`).
    - With `fix_boundary: false`, face and edge nodes of the closure of the selected volumes also move, but only when every `TopoVolume` in their upward closure (via `coboundary`) is selected. This keeps a face shared with an unselected volume conformal.
    - `NodeSelect::All` with `fix_boundary: true` moves only volume-classified nodes. Vertices never move.
  - **Pins (diagnostic `smooth.pinned_*`, counted in `report.pinned`).**
    - Nodes on faces or edges of a translation or rotation deck pair (`FaceRole::Deck{side: A|B}` with `Deck::{Translation,Rotation}`) are frozen. This is the decision 12 ruling ("Smoothing pins deck-pair nodes in W3a; tied smoothing is W4"). It deliberately deviates from the `smooth` concept in `concepts.yaml` ("paired nodes paired"); updating that text is a stage-0 docs item, not this task's.
    - Nodes on explicit `Interface` boundary faces are frozen.
    - Mirror decks need no special case: the face carrier is the plane, so projection keeps nodes in it.
    - Nodes of polyhedral cells are frozen, reason `polyhedral` (polyhedra come from `mesh.to_poly` on an all-tet mesh; smooth the tets before dualising).
    - A mesh with any `high_order_cell` or `face_curve` gives `Unsupported`, because mid-edge nodes would go stale.
  - For dim-2 meshes the reference normal of a cell comes from a private `CarrierNormals: sm_mesh_api::FaceNormals` over `TopologyView` (carrier normal × `Sense`) and is passed as `SurfaceNormals::Field`. No contract change is needed. (`MeshRead::cell_normal` is the Newell normal only, per decision 12 of W2; it is not the carrier-oriented normal.)
- **Methods (mandatory):**
  - `Laplacian`: `x ← x + relax·(mean(in-entity neighbours) − x)`, then project.
  - `SmartLaplacian`: the same, but also accepted only if `min` incident `Metric::Shape` does not decrease.
  - `CentroidArea`: `x ← Σ A_c·centroid_c / Σ A_c` over incident cells (volume-weighted in 3-D). Edge and face nodes use their in-entity faces or edges.
  - `Optimize{MeanRatio, p}`: per-node local minimisation of `F_v = Σ_{c∋v} β_c^p` with `β_c = 1/Metric::Shape`, evaluated with `sm_quality::eval` on the template corner lists.
    - The gradient is a central finite difference with `δ = 1e-6·ℓ_v`, where `ℓ_v` is the mean incident edge length.
    - The direction is the negative gradient projected on the tangent space (R³, tangent plane from the surface normal, or curve tangent).
    - Armijo backtracking (`c1=1e-4`, halving, at most 10 steps) from an initial step of `0.25·ℓ_v`. Each trial is projected to the geometry and must pass the guard.
    - One code path serves all template shapes (hex, tet, quad, tri). Analytic gradients are deferred.
- **Guard (always on; this is the "never inverts" contract):** a candidate for node v is accepted only if, for every incident template cell c, `ScaledJacobian'_c ≥ min(ScaledJacobian_c, guard_floor)` and `Volume'_c > 0` whenever `Volume_c > 0`. A cell that is already inverted may not get worse. The mesh must be conformal, so the guard looks at every incident cell, including cells of unselected volumes.
- **Schedules (bit-identical for any thread count):**
  - `Colored`: greedy first-fit colouring in ascending node index; the conflict graph is movable nodes sharing a template cell. Colours are processed in order. Within a colour, nodes are computed in parallel from the current positions and written in ascending index order. Same-colour nodes share no cell, so the global `Σβ^p` of `Optimize` is non-increasing each sweep.
  - `Jacobi`: all candidates are computed from the old positions, then applied. Then a repair loop reverts every moved node of any cell that now violates the guard against the pre-sweep state (at most 20 passes, then revert the rest). Counted in `rejected_guard`.
  - Stopping: iterations are exhausted, or `max_v |Δx_v|/ℓ_v < tolerance` (a max, so order independent).
- **Op:** `mesh.smooth { mesh: EntityId[Mesh], entities?: EntityId[TopoVolume|TopoFace|TopoEdge][], method: Method, iterations?: u32 (1..=10000, default 10), tolerance?: f64, schedule?: "jacobi"|"colored", fix_boundary?: bool, guard_floor?: f64 }`.
  - `method` is internally tagged, e.g. `{"kind":"laplacian","relax":1.0}` or `{"kind":"optimize","objective":"mean_ratio","p":4}`. `deny_unknown_fields`; every id field carries `x-entity-kind`.
  - Body: read the entry, `clone_host()` its host, run `smooth`, `apply_outcome` on the clone, then `MeshLayer::replace_mesh(mesh, Arc::from(edited), alloc_after)` (alloc unchanged: no ids are minted or retired, `NO_OUTPUTS = true`). `replace_mesh` is checked and atomic, so any failure leaves the state byte-identical. Fallback when `replace_mesh` is absent: `get_mut` and assigning `entry.mesh`, with the same atomicity test.
  - Diagnostics, so MCP and CLI results are actionable: `ctx.info("smooth.report", <report JSON>, &[mesh])`. Warnings: `smooth.inverted_input` (count, with a hint that untangling is deferred), `smooth.no_improvement` (min shape unchanged, with a suggestion to change intervals, scheme or `fix_boundary`), `smooth.not_converged`, `smooth.pinned_deck`, `smooth.pinned_polyhedral`. Each lists the entities involved.
  - The diagnostics suggestion table in sm-diag reads the op registry, so `mesh.smooth` being registered here is all it needs; this task adds no code to sm-diag.
- **Query:** `mesh.smooth_preview` takes the same arguments as the op and returns `SmoothReport` without changing state.
- **Acceptance (mandatory; analytic; fixtures from `sm_testkit::lattice`, no local rebuilds):**
  1. *Recovery, 3-D.* A 4×4×4-cell hex lattice (`lattice::hex`) on a classified unit brick, with every non-vertex node displaced by `lattice::perturb(seed, 0.25)` (inside its entity), is restored by `Laplacian` (`NodeSelect::All`, `fix_boundary: false`, relax 1, both schedules, ≤ 150 iterations, `tolerance` 1e-12) to within 1e-9·h of `LatticeFixture.exact`. The Jacobi spectral radius is cos(π/4) ≈ 0.707, so 0.707^150 is far below 1e-9. Vertex nodes are bit-identical to `exact`.
  2. *Recovery, 2-D.* The same for a 6×6 planar quad lattice (`lattice::quad`, `fix_boundary: false`) with `Laplacian` (relax 1, ≤ 300 iterations, 1e-9·h; the Jacobi radius is cos(π/6) ≈ 0.866, so 0.866^300 ≈ 1e-19) and with `CentroidArea` (≤ 400 iterations, 1e-6·h).
  3. *Optimize.* A 6×6×6 hex lattice with jitter 0.2h (precondition asserted: initial min `Shape` < 0.9). `Optimize{MeanRatio, p=4}`, Colored, ≤ 300 iterations, gives min `Shape` ≥ 0.99. In `Colored` mode `Σβ^p` is non-increasing at every sweep (relative slack 1e-12). On a Kuhn-tet 4×4×4 lattice (`lattice::tet_kuhn`) with jitter 0.2h, `SmartLaplacian` and `Optimize` end with mean `Shape` ≥ the unperturbed lattice's mean − 1e-3. The worker may adjust a threshold only with the measurement recorded in `DESIGN.md`.
  4. *Never inverts.* A 12×12 planar quad lattice with jitter 0.45h (precondition `validity(..).all_valid()`) is run through every mandatory method × both schedules × `relax ∈ {1.0, 1.5, 1.9}` (for `Optimize`, `p ∈ {2, 4}`) × 1..=20 iterations: the result is always `all_valid()` and min `ScaledJacobian` ≥ min(initial, 1e-3). An L-shaped quad region exists where an unguarded Laplacian written in the test inverts at least one cell (this proves the guard is exercised); ours does not. An input with one inverted cell gives non-decreasing min `ScaledJacobian`, a non-increasing `n_inverted`, and the `smooth.inverted_input` diagnostic.
  5. *Stays on geometry.* A 12×6 quad tube (`lattice::tube_quad(12, 6, r=1, h=2)`) on the cylinder face of `sm_topo_api::fixtures::cylinder`, classified on the lateral face, both circle edges, the seam edge and the 2 vertices. After each mandatory method:
     - face nodes satisfy `|hypot(x,y) − 1| ≤ 1e-12`;
     - circle nodes lie on z∈{0,2} and r=1 within 1e-12;
     - seam nodes are on the seam line within 1e-12;
     - vertex nodes are bit-exact;
     - the refreshed `Param` re-evaluates to the stored point within 1e-12 (`Surface::eval(uv)`, `Curve::eval(t)`) and `t` stays in range;
     - `Laplacian` and `CentroidArea`, 400 iterations from jitter, restore equal angular spacing 2π/12 and equal z spacing 2/6 within 1e-6.
     A unit test of the trim-reject path feeds a `uv` outside the face and expects `rejected_geometry += 1` with the node unmoved. If the seam closure in `tube_quad` proves unusable, the stage-0 owner substitutes a planar annular sector fixture; this task does not rebuild it.
  6. *Decks and interfaces.* On `sm_testkit::topo::mirror_chamber()` meshed with the stub driver and jittered, `fix_boundary:false` leaves the deck-face nodes at z = 1/2 within 1e-12, with at least one moved by more than 1e-6. For a translation-deck pair declared on two opposite brick faces (`Model::add_boundary` + `assign_pairs`), every node on them and on their edges is bit-identical afterwards and `smooth.pinned_deck` names the faces. The same holds for an explicit `Interface` face.
  7. *Op level.* A 10³ brick through the stub `mesh.run`, jittered by a test-only op, then `mesh.smooth` Laplacian (100 iterations) returns every node within 1e-6 of the lattice, `mesh.info.volume` = 1000 within 1e-9, `Delta.created` is empty and `touched_layers == ["meshes"]`. A `smooth.report` diagnostic is present. Undo, redo and cold replay (`Session::state_at_from_root`) give no `Diverged`. `entities:[face]` moves only that face's nodes (all others bit-identical). Unknown mesh, a `TopoFace` with no nodes in the mesh, a high-order mesh, and `NothingToSmooth` each fail with the state hash unchanged. `mesh.smooth_preview` returns the same report as the op without changing the state hash. Params round-trip through the schema. A real `mesh.run` mesh (sm-mesher dev-dep) passes `sm_mesh_api::validate` and `validate_geometry` before and after.
  8. *Determinism.* `sm_testkit::determinism::check_f64_bits(3 runs, 4 threads)` on `SmoothOutcome::points` for every delivered method × schedule, plus `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` against the default, with equality on the **exact** `geometry_hash` (the quantized hash hides sub-`quanta.length` moves) and on the state hash.
  9. *Bench (criterion, record-only, no CI threshold):* 64³ hex lattice (274k nodes) × 10 `Laplacian` iterations (Colored and Jacobi), and 16³ × 10 `Optimize`. Record the numbers in `DESIGN.md`. The target is at least 5·10⁶ node-updates/s for `Laplacian` on 4 threads, recorded not gated, because the whole-mesh clone in `replace_mesh` and the `set_classes` rebuild are bulk-commit costs (W3b backlog item) and the 64³ op-level time is dominated by them.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-9 pass; in this order, each ships with its own tests or is listed in the README as known_gap):**
  1. `Objective::Condition` (`β_c = Metric::Condition`) with acceptance: on the 6×6×6 hex case of item 3, max `Condition` ≤ 1.01, plus the same never-inverts and determinism coverage.
  2. `Method::Winslow{relax}`, 2-D quad only. A node is eligible if it has exactly 4 incident quads. With ξ = (E,W), η = (N,S) and central differences: `α=|x_η|²`, `γ=|x_ξ|²`, `β=x_ξ·x_η`, `x ← [α(x_E+x_W) + γ(x_N+x_S) − β/2·(x_NE−x_NW−x_SE+x_SW)] / (2(α+γ))`. Other nodes are pinned, reason `winslow_unstructured`. Acceptance: recovery to 1e-9·h on the 6×6 quad lattice and on its image under a fixed affine shear (Winslow is affine invariant), and inclusion in the never-inverts matrix (4) and determinism (8).
- **`DESIGN.md`:** every formula, the constraint table, the guard predicate, the colouring, the repair loop, the measured thresholds and bench numbers, and the deviations from Mesquite and Cubit.
- **Deferred to W3b (list in the crate README):**
  - 3-D Winslow, equipotential, untangle, randomize.
  - Analytic gradients or Hessians (Newton).
  - Polyhedral cells and high-order meshes.
  - Parallel colouring (it is sequential here).
  - Targeting by stored mesh set or group (`mesh.quality` storing worst cells as a set, consumed by `mesh.smooth`; use `NodeSelect::Vertices` meanwhile). Needs a set-naming decision and a `set` argument.
  - Surface-feature preservation by dihedral angle (classification already gives features).
  - Perf gates that depend on the bulk mesh commit (record-only in W3a).
  - Tied smoothing of translation or rotation deck pairs is W4 (decision 12).
- **Must NOT:** edit contract crates or sibling crates (the stage-0 items above already exist or are added by the orchestrator); depend on a W3a crate still being built; duplicate sm-quality metrics; use `HashMap` iteration, unseeded randomness (test jitter is `lattice::perturb`, a hash of `(seed, vertex index)`) or `unwrap`/`expect` on input paths; mint ids; write the `model` layer; move a vertex-classified node; weaken the guard to make a convergence test pass.

#### registers
plugin: SmoothPlugin (id "smooth")
op: mesh.smooth { mesh, entities?, method, iterations?, tolerance?, schedule?, fix_boundary?, guard_floor? } (NO_OUTPUTS; writes only the meshes layer; diagnostics smooth.report / smooth.inverted_input / smooth.no_improvement / smooth.not_converged / smooth.pinned_deck / smooth.pinned_polyhedral)
query: mesh.smooth_preview (same args, returns SmoothReport, no state change)
no layers
no export formats

#### ACRs
None. Everything this task needs is a decision 12 stage-0 item (`MeshLayer::replace_mesh`, `sm_testkit::lattice` and the tet_block family) or already on main. The earlier `MeshEntry::edit` and local-fixture ACRs are dropped.

#### depends
[] (no dependency on any W3a task; sm-quality is L2 on main, fixtures come from stage 0)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to Mesquite (Brewer, Freitag, Knupp: Jacobi/Gauss-Seidel sweeps of node-local objective minimisation over target-matrix quality metrics) and to Cubit's `smooth` family (Laplacian, centroid area pull, Winslow, condition number, with surface/curve constraint handling). Differs in: (1) constraints come from our B-rep classification and the exact analytic carriers (`ParametricCurve/Surface::project`, trim check), not facets or a stored normal field; (2) the guard makes "never inverts" a contract on every method, not an option, and it also covers cells of unselected neighbours; (3) deck and interface boundaries are respected (mirror nodes slide in the plane, translation or rotation pairs and explicit interfaces are pinned); (4) the sweeps are deterministic and bit-identical for 1 or N threads (colouring plus ordered writes), and the op sits on the replayable, hashable timeline, with a preview query and located diagnostics for MCP and CLI. Disadvantage today: Mesquite has analytic gradients and Hessians (Feasible Newton) and handles more element types, so our finite-difference steepest descent is slower per node and may stall in flat valleys. Advantage: exact geometry projection and a deterministic, replayable result.

#### risks
Finite-difference gradients make Optimize roughly 10-30x slower per node than Laplacian; the 3-D 1e6-node case may take minutes for 10 iterations. Analytic gradients are deferred and the benchmark records the real number.
The dim-2 tube fixture closes a seam: seam-node classification and quad orientation are fixture risks owned by stage 0, not the smoother.
Guard-only inversion protection can freeze nodes in a tangled input: Laplacian reports success but moves nothing. The smooth.inverted_input and smooth.no_improvement diagnostics are the mitigation, and untangle is a separate (deferred) algorithm.
Surface projection of a face node near a trimmed boundary or a periodic seam can jump to a different uv branch. The stored Param hint and the trim check reduce this, but a tight concave trim can still reject many moves.
Pinning translation and rotation deck-pair faces leaves their boundary cells unsmoothed, which may cap the achievable quality on symmetric models until tied smoothing (W4) exists.
`replace_mesh` clones the whole mesh once per mesh.smooth op (O(mesh) copy and one set_classes index rebuild). Fine for W3 sizes; it is the bulk-commit backlog item (W3b), so op-level perf is record-only.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
See the separate open list returned with this entry (sm-server wiring, shared CarrierNormals, set targeting, stage-0 fixture names).

#### escalation
None remaining. Resolved by decision 12: sm-quality is L2 (runtime dependency legal); deck-pair nodes and explicit Interface nodes are pinned in W3a; polyhedral cells are frozen with a diagnostic; `MeshEntry::edit` is dropped in favour of `replace_mesh` (stage 0) or `get_mut` + `clone_host`; lattice fixtures are stage-0 deliverables.

### W3-EXO. `sm-exodus` (L2, new crate, skeleton exists from stage 0): Exodus II writer with its own netCDF encoder
- **Binding rulings:** decision 12 (own crate `sm-exodus` at L2; internal element names `POLYGON`/`POLYHEDRON`, the Exodus writer maps them to `NSIDED`/`NFACED`; W3a performance targets are recorded, not gated; no `tracking`), decision 9 (Deps lines bound internal crates only; contract-crate dev-deps and deterministic rayon are accepted), decision 11 (dev-deps on peers allowed only if acyclic and already on main; this task needs none).
- **Deps:** normal: sm-core, sm-mesh-api, thiserror, rayon. Dev: sm-testkit, proptest, criterion (and serde_json only if a test needs it). Add only path deps and crates already in `Cargo.lock`; do not add new external crates and do not commit `Cargo.lock` (the orchestrator owns it). No dependency, normal or dev, on sm-meshio, sm-fem or any other W3a crate. No `unsafe`. Edit only `crates/sm-exodus/`. Read first: `crates/sm-mesh-api/src/shape.rs` (`CellShape::local_faces`, `derive_corners`, `loop_relation`), `read.rs` (`MeshRead`), `sets.rs` (`SetMembers`, `MeshSet.label`); `crates/sm-meshio/src/{lib,vtu,le}.rs` (writer style, chunked parallel packing, error style) and `crates/sm-fem/src/{export,lower}.rs` (how sets reach a writer) for reading only.
- **Stage-0 items this task relies on (by name, not ACRs):** the `sm-exodus` skeleton; the ARCHITECTURE.md and `concepts.yaml` node for sm-exodus (L2, edge to sm-mesh-api); `sm_protocol::ExportFormat::{Exodus, Openfoam}` and `ExportResult.files`; existing `sm_testkit::meshes` fixtures (`single_hex`, `single_tet`, `single_wedge`, `single_pyramid`, `hex_block_2x2x2`, `mixed_transition`, `truncated_octahedron`, `hexagonal_prism`, `dual_patch`, `all_mesh_fixtures`). Nothing else is needed from stage 0.
- **Scope (public API):**
  - `pub mod netcdf`: a minimal classic-netCDF model and codec.
    - `pub enum NcFormat { Classic, Offset64, Data64 }` (CDF-1/2/5; magic `CDF\x01|\x02|\x05`).
    - `pub enum NcType { Byte, Char, Short, Int, Float, Double, Int64 }` (Int64 only in Data64).
    - `pub struct NcFile { .. }` builder: `new(format)`, `dim(name, len: Option<u64>) -> DimId` (None = the unlimited record dim, at most one), `global_attr(name, NcValue)`, `var(name, NcType, &[DimId]) -> VarId`, `var_attr(VarId, name, NcValue)`, `set_data(VarId, NcData)`. `NcData` is `Bytes(Vec<u8>)` (already big-endian) or `Lazy(Box<dyn Fn() -> Vec<u8> + Send + Sync>)` so the writer holds about one variable at a time.
    - `pub fn layout(&self) -> Result<NcLayout, ExodusError>` computes header size, per-variable `vsize` and `begin` without any data (so a 2^33-element dimension can be tested without allocating). Rules: every name, attribute value and non-record variable padded to 4 bytes with zeros; non-record variables in declaration order after the header; record variables (zero records here, `numrecs = 0`) after them; Classic errors `TooLarge` when any `begin` or `vsize` exceeds `u32::MAX`; Offset64 clamps `vsize` to `0xFFFF_FFFF` for a variable over 4 GiB and requires it to be the last non-record variable; Data64 widens counts, dimids and vsize to 8 bytes.
    - `pub fn write(&self, w: &mut dyn Write) -> Result<u64, ExodusError>`; `pub fn to_cdl(&self) -> String` renders `ncdump`-style CDL (header plus `data:`; doubles with `%.17g`, ints plain, char variables as quoted strings with trailing NULs trimmed, as ncdump does); `pub fn read(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<NcFile, ExodusError>` parses CDF-1/2/5 (rejects `\x89HDF` NetCDF-4 with `Unsupported("netCDF-4/HDF5 input")`).
  - `pub struct ExodusOptions` (`Default`, `#[non_exhaustive]`, builder methods): `format: NcFormat = Offset64`; `int64: bool = false` (needs Data64; sets `int64_status = 1` and writes ids/connectivity as NC_INT64); `title: String = "smart-mesh"`; `max_name_length: u32 = 32` (grows to the longest name, error above 255); `blocks: BlockSelection { Labelled (default), Names(Vec<String>), None }`; `faces_as_sidesets: bool = true`; `write_node_num_map: bool = false` (`elem_num_map` is always written); `polyhedra: bool = true`; `qa: Vec<[String; 4]>` (default one record `["smart-mesh", CARGO_PKG_VERSION, "1970/01/01", "00:00:00"]`, a fixed placeholder so output is deterministic); `info: Vec<String>`; `provenance: Option<String>` (becomes info record `smart-mesh provenance: <s>`; the same string sm-fem passes to `SmshWriter`).
  - `pub fn write_exodus(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &ExodusOptions, w: &mut dyn Write) -> Result<ExodusReport, ExodusError>`, `pub fn exodus_bytes(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &ExodusOptions) -> Result<Vec<u8>, ExodusError>`, `pub fn plan(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &ExodusOptions) -> Result<ExodusPlan, ExodusError>` (block/set layout, no I/O). `ExodusReport { bytes, format, num_nodes, num_elem, blocks: Vec<BlockSummary { id, name, elem_type, count }>, sidesets: Vec<SetSummary { id, name, count }>, nodesets: Vec<SetSummary>, skipped_sides: usize }`. `BlockSummary.elem_type` holds the Exodus spelling.
  - Side tables: `pub const fn exodus_side(shape: CellShape, local_face: usize) -> Option<u8>` and `pub const fn local_face(shape: CellShape, side: u8) -> Option<usize>` (1-based Exodus side; `local_face` indexes `CellShape::local_faces()`); `pub const fn exodus_element_type(shape: CellShape) -> &'static str` (Tet `TET4`, Hex `HEX8`, Wedge `WEDGE6`, Pyramid `PYRAMID5`, Tri `TRI3`, Quad `QUAD4`, Polygon `NSIDED`, Polyhedron `NFACED`). The function is named `exodus_element_type`, not `element_type_name`, because internal and `.smsh` 1.1 names are `POLYGON`/`POLYHEDRON` (decision 12) and the two must not be confused. Document the tables in rustdoc and `DESIGN.md` (ours -> Exodus):
    - HEX8: our faces [bottom, top, y0, x1, y1, x0] -> sides [5, 6, 1, 2, 3, 4] (side 1 = nodes 1,2,6,5 = the -y face of the unit cube).
    - TET4: [0,2,1] -> 4, [0,1,3] -> 1, [1,2,3] -> 2, [2,0,3] -> 3.
    - WEDGE6: [bottom tri, top tri, 0154, 1254, 2035] -> [4, 5, 1, 2, 3].
    - PYRAMID5: [base, 014, 124, 234, 304] -> [5, 1, 2, 3, 4].
    - QUAD4 / TRI3 (2-D): edge `i` -> side `i + 1`.
    - NFACED / NSIDED: side = 1-based position in the element's face (edge) list as written.
  - `pub mod read`: `pub fn read_exodus(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<ExodusData, ExodusError>`; `ExodusData` is an owned decode (title, dims, coordinates, blocks with id/name/type/connectivity/entity counts, face blocks, side/node sets with names, `elem_num_map`, `node_num_map`, qa, info). Internal, for round-trip tests; documented as not a general Exodus reader.
  - `ExodusError` (thiserror, `#[non_exhaustive]`): `Io`, `Mesh(MeshError)`, `Unsupported(String)`, `TooLarge { what, value, limit }`, `EmptyMesh`, `OverlappingBlocks { cell, a, b }`, `BadLabel { set, label }`, `NameTooLong { name }`, `Malformed(String)`.
- **Mapping rules (all deterministic):**
  1. Nodes = `vertex_count()` vertices in index order (extra high-order points are ignored only when no cell has a `high_order_cell`; otherwise `Unsupported("high-order cells")`). `num_dim = 3`; a dimension-2 mesh with every z == 0.0 gives `num_dim = 2`, `coordz` omitted.
  2. Only active cells (`cell_active`) are written. Blocks: candidate sets are the `SetMembers::Cells` sets selected by `opts.blocks`; they must be disjoint. A set with one shape becomes one block, `id = label` (label must be > 0, else `BadLabel`), name = set name. A mixed set splits by shape: the lowest `CellShape::code` keeps label and name, the others get fresh ids (above the largest label, ascending by shape code) and name `<set>_<ELEMTYPE>` (Exodus spelling). Cells in no block go to `unassigned_<ELEMTYPE>` blocks (fresh ids, shape code ascending). Blocks are ordered by id; elements are numbered block by block, ascending `CellIx` inside a block; `elem_num_map[e] = CellIx + 1`.
  3. Template cells write `cell_corners` as 1-based `connect<k>(num_el_in_blk<k>, num_nod_per_el<k>)` with attribute `elem_type`. Our template corner order already equals Exodus (hex, wedge, pyramid, tet, quad, tri), so no permutation; a test asserts it.
  4. Sidesets: `SetMembers::Sides` gives one entry per `(face, side)`: the cell is `face_left` or `face_right`, the Exodus side is `exodus_side(shape, k)` where `k` is the template face whose vertex cycle equals `face_vertices(f)` (`loop_relation`: `Same` for Left, `Reversed` for Right, else `Mesh(..)` inconsistency error). `SetMembers::Faces` (deck face sets) become sidesets on the face's Left cell when `faces_as_sidesets`. Entries stay in set order; an entry whose cell is inactive is counted in `skipped_sides`. Sideset id = label, else the smallest unused positive id above the maximum label (own id space). Empty sets are not written.
  5. Nodesets: `SetMembers::Vertices` (and `Points` below `vertex_count`) -> `node_ns<k>`; same id rule. Only user-attached or classification-derived sets are exported. The sm-fem lowering does not produce vertex sets today; adding that is outside this task (see Open).
  6. `POLYGON` cells -> `NSIDED` block (`connect<k>(num_nod_per_el<k>)` total nodes, `ebepecnt<k>(num_el_in_blk<k>)` = nodes per element). `POLYHEDRON` cells -> `NFACED` block (`facconn<k>(num_fa_in_blk<k>)`, `ebepecnt<k>`) referencing a face block `NSIDED` (`fbconn`, `fbepecnt`, `num_fa_in_fblk<k>`, `num_fa`, `num_fa_blk`). Fixed rule (resolves the sharing question): one outward-oriented face copy per (cell, face), face loop reversed for `Right` entries; element face order = `cell_faces` order; no shared-face NFACED. Take every dimension/variable name from the `exodusII.h` macros if reachable; if the sources are unreachable, ship NFACED behind `ExodusOptions::polyhedra = true` and report it in the return message.
  7. Variables written (all otherwise absent): global attrs `api_version`, `version` (float 8.11), `floating_point_word_size` (4-byte int, = 8), `file_size` (1), `maximum_name_length`, `int64_status`, `title`; dims `time_step` (unlimited), `len_string` 33, `len_line` 81, `four` 4, `len_name`, `num_dim`, `num_nodes`, `num_elem`, `num_el_blk`, `num_node_sets`, `num_side_sets`, `num_qa_rec`, `num_info`; vars `time_whole(time_step)`, `coordx|y|z`, `coor_names`, `eb_status`, `eb_prop1` (attr `name = "ID"`), `eb_names`, `connect<k>`, `ns_*`, `node_ns<k>`, `ss_*`, `elem_ss<k>`, `side_ss<k>`, `elem_num_map`, `node_num_map` (only when requested), `qa_records(num_qa_rec, four, len_string)`, `info_records`. Names are char arrays padded with NUL to `len_name`.
- **Acceptance (analytic, numbered):**
  1. Header bytes, `single_hex`, Offset64: bytes 0..4 = `43 44 46 02`, numrecs = 0, next word `0x0000000A` (NC_DIMENSION); for Classic and Data64 the magic byte is 1 and 5. Every `begin` is 4-aligned and strictly ascending, every `vsize` a multiple of 4, and the file length equals last `begin` + last `vsize`. A hand-assembled one-dim/one-attribute/one-variable netCDF (bytes derived from the netCDF file-format BNF in the test, not from our writer) is byte-equal to `NcFile::write` for all three formats.
  2. Golden CDL, `single_hex` with set `ns` = vertices {1,2,5,6} (0-based) and a side set on face x = 1 (Left): `to_cdl()` equals the CDL string stored in the test: `num_nodes = 8`, `num_elem = 1`, `num_el_blk = 1`, `num_el_in_blk1 = 1`, `num_nod_per_el1 = 8`, `connect1 = 1..8`, `connect1:elem_type = "HEX8"`, `coordx = 0,1,1,0,0,1,1,0`, `node_ns1 = 2,3,6,7` (0-based vertices {1,2,5,6} are 1-based nodes 2,3,6,7, all with x = 1), `elem_ss1 = 1`, `side_ss1 = 2`, `eb_prop1 = 1`. Same test for `single_tet`, `single_wedge`, `single_pyramid` (block name, `elem_type`, connectivity and coordinates; no sets).
  3. Side geometry, unit elements, hand-written (not via our table): for each shape and each side 1..n the plane or centroid of the Exodus-named face is the expected one. Hex: sides 1..6 centroids (.5,0,.5), (1,.5,.5), (.5,1,.5), (0,.5,.5), (.5,.5,0), (.5,.5,1); tet: s1 y=0, s3 x=0, s4 z=0, s2 slanted; wedge: s1 y=0, s3 x=0, s4 z=0, s5 z=1; pyramid: s5 base, s1 y=0 triangle.
  4. Property over all `all_mesh_fixtures()` template cells plus proptest random `hex_block` dims: for every cell and local face, the node loop of the Exodus side read from the file's connectivity equals `face_vertices` as a cycle with the same orientation (Left) or reversed (Right), and its normal points away from the cell centroid.
  5. `hex_block_2x2x2` with a `Sides` set of the four x = 1 boundary faces: 4 entries, all `side = 2`, each named element's face centroid x = 1.0 exactly. Two disjoint block sets (4 + 4 cells) give blocks 1 and 2 and `elem_num_map` a permutation of 1..8; `mixed_transition` gives 4 blocks HEX8/WEDGE6/PYRAMID5/TET4 of one element each, ordered by id.
  6. Round trip: `read_exodus(exodus_bytes(m))` reproduces coordinates bit-for-bit, per-cell connectivity (`cell_corners + 1`), set members, names, ids and qa/info, for every fixture and for Classic/Offset64/Data64 (the decoded `ExodusData` is equal across formats, except the format field).
  7. Polyhedra: `truncated_octahedron` gives one `NFACED` element with `ebepecnt = [14]`, face block of 14 faces (8 hexagons, 6 quads), every face copy's Newell normal has positive dot with (face centroid - cell centroid); `hexagonal_prism` gives `ebepecnt = [8]`; `dual_patch` writes mixed `NFACED` and template blocks. A side set on a polyhedral cell uses side = 1-based position in its face list. The report and the file say `NFACED`/`NSIDED`, never `POLYHEDRON`/`POLYGON`.
  8. Determinism: bytes identical across two runs and for `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs the default thread count (`sm_testkit::determinism::check`), with and without sets, and the SHA of a 20^3 hex block file is stored as a regression constant.
  9. Limits (`layout()` only, no allocation): Classic with a total over 4 GiB gives `TooLarge`; Offset64 with a 5 GiB variable that is not last gives `TooLarge`, as the last variable gives `vsize = 0xFFFFFFFF`; Data64 accepts both.
  10. Errors, never panics: empty mesh -> `EmptyMesh`; overlapping block sets -> `OverlappingBlocks`; block label 0 -> `BadLabel`; name of 300 bytes -> `NameTooLong`; a mesh with a high-order cell -> `Unsupported`; a truncated or HDF5 input to `read` -> `Malformed` / `Unsupported`.
  11. Bench (criterion), RECORD-ONLY (not a gate, per decision 12): 1e6-hex file to `io::sink`; target under 2 s on 4 vCPU; record the measured number in `DESIGN.md`.
  12. Gate: `cargo build`, `cargo test -p sm-exodus --all-features`, `cargo clippy -p sm-exodus --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo doc --no-deps -p sm-exodus` with `RUSTDOCFLAGS='-D warnings'`, rustdoc on every public item, `DESIGN.md` with the positioning, the side tables and the variable list.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-12 pass):**
  - S1. External cross-checks, skipped with a message when the tool is missing (as `sm-meshio/tests/python_reader.rs` does): `ncdump -h` and `ncdump` (normalised whitespace) equal `to_cdl()`; `scipy.io.netcdf_file` reads Classic and Offset64 files and gives equal coordinates and connectivity.
  - S2. `ExodusOptions::frames: Vec<CoordinateFrame { id: i64, origin: [f64;3], axis: [f64;3], point: [f64;3] }>` with dims `num_cframes`/`num_cframes_9` and vars `frame_coordinates|frame_ids|frame_tags`, plus decode in `ExodusData`.
  - S3. `sm_testkit::meshes::with_sets(fixture, spec)` helper (belongs to sm-testkit; propose it to the orchestrator, do not edit sm-testkit).
- **Deferred to W3b or later (state in DESIGN.md):** netCDF-4/HDF5 output; time steps and results (nodal/element/global variables); high-order types (HEX20/27, TET10, WEDGE15, PYRAMID13, QUAD8, TRI6) until degree elevation exists; distribution factors; element attributes; assemblies; edge blocks and edge/face/element sets; shell side sets (`SetMembers::ShellSides`, SHELL4 / TRISHELL3) and `num_dim = 3` surface meshes; shared-face NFACED; reading general third-party Exodus files; parallel (Nemesis) files; a vertex-set (node set) lowering in sm-fem; wiring into `session.export`/CLI/MCP (integration step, see escalation).
- **Must NOT:** edit sm-meshio, sm-fem, sm-server, sm-protocol, sm-testkit, `concepts.yaml`, ARCHITECTURE.md, the root `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` or any contract crate; depend on sm-meshio or any other W3a crate; call libnetcdf/HDF5 or shell out to tools outside the optional tests; write wall-clock time into the file; let `HashMap` order or thread count affect bytes; use `unwrap()` on input paths; use `unsafe`; tune the mesh to any tool (exact internal cells; only the writer maps to Exodus conventions); emit `POLYHEDRON`/`POLYGON` into the file.

#### registers
