No ops, queries or layers. Registers one export format by name: "exodus" (file extensions .exo, .e, .g), exposed only as the pub fns write_exodus / exodus_bytes. Wiring into session.export (the stage-0 `ExportFormat::Exodus` variant and `ExportResult.files`), the CLI and MCP is done by the integration step (D1/D2 owners) after merge, calling `sm_exodus::write_exodus`.

#### ACRs
[]

#### fixtures
None required. Tests use existing sm_testkit::meshes fixtures (single_hex, single_tet, single_wedge, single_pyramid, hex_block_2x2x2, mixed_transition, truncated_octahedron, hexagonal_prism, dual_patch, all_mesh_fixtures) and attach sets in-test with MeshEdit::add_set / set_label on the ReferenceMesh (fresh SerialAlloc).

#### depends
[]  (no dependency on any W3a crate; stage-0 skeleton only)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to the Exodus II writer in SEACAS/libexodus (ex_create, ex_put_*) over netCDF-C, and to the Exodus writers in meshio and Cubit. It differs in four ways. (1) It is pure Rust with its own netCDF classic/64-bit-offset/CDF-5 encoder, so there is no C or HDF5 dependency (libexodus defaults to netCDF-4/HDF5 today; we write only the classic family that every Exodus reader still opens). (2) It reads the face-first IR through MeshRead only, so true polyhedra (NFACED), polygons (NSIDED), mixed blocks, classification-derived sets and the cell/face orientation are first-class. Template shapes keep their exact internal order and each side is mapped to the Exodus side number per element type. (3) Output is a pure function of the mesh and options: byte-identical across runs and thread counts (libexodus embeds the time in QA records). (4) The sideset side numbering is verified by geometry in tests (the face the Exodus table names is the face the mesh set names), not by round trip through the same table. The advantage over meshio is polyhedra, sidesets and nodesets with names and ids, and no netCDF4/h5py dependency. The advantage over libexodus is determinism, no native build, and streaming output to any Write.

#### risks
Exodus NFACED/NSIDED variable and dimension names and the face orientation rule are written from memory of libexodus (ex_put_block, ex_put_entity_count_per_polyhedra); the worker must check them against exodusII.h / exodusII_int.h macros (DIM_*, VAR_*) if the SEACAS source or docs are reachable. Per-cell outward face copies make orientation unambiguous; if sources are unreachable, NFACED stays behind `ExodusOptions::polyhedra = true` and the report says so.
The Exodus side tables are from memory of the Exodus II manual figures. The unit-element geometric golden tests guard against transcription errors but cannot prove the manual says the same; the integration harness (ParaView/meshio) is the external judge.
netCDF classic layout details that are easy to get wrong: 4-byte padding of every name, attribute value and non-record variable; record-variable vsize and the begin of the record section when numrecs = 0; CDF-2 vsize clamp (0xFFFFFFFF) for a variable over 4 GiB, which must be the last non-record variable; CDF-5 widens counts, dimids, vsize to 8 bytes. Mitigated by hand-derived byte tests (acceptance 1) and the stretch scipy/ncdump cross-checks.
Memory: building each variable as a Vec<u8> is O(largest variable); lazy providers keep peak at about one variable (about 3.2 GB for 1e8 hexes in int32). Acceptable for W3a.
Reader acceptance of CDF-2 by every consumer: MOOSE/libMesh/ParaView/Cubit read it through netCDF-C and are fine; some old tools only accept CDF-1. NcFormat is an option for that reason.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
Ruled in this revision (no longer open): NFACED face sharing = per-cell copies; default flavor = Offset64 (Data64 only for 64-bit ids); `elem_num_map` always written, `node_num_map` on request; provenance goes into an info record.
Still open: (1) Node sets: sm-fem lowering does not produce `SetMembers::Vertices` today; vertex-color/node-set lowering is an sm-fem owner decision (W3b unless the integration step needs it). The milestone Exodus export therefore carries element blocks and sidesets only. (2) Whether real NFACED conventions can be verified against SEACAS sources or a real polyhedral Exodus file; if not, the integration step must treat NFACED output as experimental.

#### escalation
["Wiring after merge: sm-fem match arm for ExportFormat::Exodus (enum and ExportResult.files already exist from stage 0) calling sm_exodus::write_exodus on the lowered mesh, plus the sm-exodus dependency; format names in sm-lang session.export and sm-server/sm-cli/sm-mcp; the \"exodus\" row in docs. Assigned to the integration step (D1 owners), against the sm-exodus names in this entry (not sm_meshio::exodus, ExodusFile::decode or an ExodusOptions with block_sets/side_sets/node_sets). This task does not touch them.", "Decision needed only if NFACED conventions cannot be verified: ship NFACED behind the polyhedra flag (as planned) and report; do not block the merge."]

### W3-MESHIO. `sm-meshio` (L2, existing crate): OpenFOAM polyMesh ASCII writer, `.smsh` 1.1, loss reports, VTU polyhedron check
- **Deps:** unchanged (sm-core, sm-mesh-api[bytemuck], sm-model-api, thiserror, rayon, serde, serde_json). No new crates. Dev: sm-testkit (`meshes::*`, `all_mesh_fixtures()`, and the stage-0 `nonconvex_poly` fixture), proptest, criterion. Runtime inputs only through `&dyn MeshRead` (`crates/sm-mesh-api/src/read.rs`); never from sm-fem (L3). No dependency on any W3a crate still being built (decision 12); stage-0 fixtures in sm-testkit decouple.
- **Stage-0 items used (by name, not ACRs):** `ExportFormat::{Exodus, Openfoam}` and `ExportResult.files` in sm-protocol (this task does not touch them; wiring into `session.export` is the integration task); sm-testkit fixtures `all_mesh_fixtures()`, `nonconvex_poly`; skeleton and layer ruling for sm-exodus (separate crate, so no Exodus code in sm-meshio). Element-type spelling is decision 12: `POLYHEDRON` / `POLYGON` internally and in `.smsh` 1.1; exporters map them (Exodus NFACED/NSIDED in sm-exodus, VTK `VTK_POLYHEDRON`, OpenFOAM faces).
- **Layout (new/changed):** `src/report.rs`; `src/foam/{mod,build,write,parse,check,tar}.rs`; `src/smsh/{format,writer,reader}.rs`; `src/vtu.rs` (loss report only); `readers/smsh.py`; `examples/foam_fixtures.rs`; `FOAM_FORMAT.md`; update `SMSH_FORMAT.md` (1.1), `DESIGN.md`, `tests/`, `benches/export.rs`. `MeshioError` gains `Foam(#[from] FoamError)`.
- **Scope A: loss report (shared by all exporters).**
  - `pub struct LossItem { pub code: String, pub severity: LossSeverity, pub count: u64, pub detail: String }`; `pub enum LossSeverity { Info, Degraded, Dropped }`; `pub struct LossReport { pub items: Vec<LossItem> }` (serde, sorted by `code` then `detail`; `is_lossless()`, `push`, `get(code)`).
  - `pub fn vtu_loss_report(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &VtuOptions) -> LossReport`. Codes: `vtu.high_order_dropped` (cells with a HO record), `vtu.face_curves_dropped`, `vtu.face_sets_dropped` (Faces/Sides/ShellSides sets), `vtu.edge_table_dropped`, `vtu.extra_points_unused` (`np - nv`).
  - `SmshWriter::loss_report(&self) -> LossReport`: always empty (lossless); a test pins that.
- **Scope B: OpenFOAM polyMesh, ASCII only** (`sm_meshio::foam`). Source: the face-first IR, internal cells untouched. polyMesh stores no cell shapes, so a true 6-node prism is five faces (2 tri, 3 quad) and needs no `wedge` handling. `wedge`/`empty` are only user-selected patch types (never inferred).
  - Types (all `pub`, rustdoc, serde snake_case): `FoamPatchType {Patch, Wall, SymmetryPlane, Symmetry, Empty}` (`as_str`); `PatchSpec {set: String, name: Option<String>, patch_type: FoamPatchType}`; `PatchSource {Sets, Explicit(Vec<PatchSpec>), Classification}`; `ZoneSelect {All, None, Names(Vec<String>)}`; `FoamOptions {patches: PatchSource = Sets, default_patch: String = "defaultFaces", default_patch_type = Patch, cell_zones: ZoneSelect = All, face_zones: Vec<String> = [], decks: Vec<DeckRecord> = []}` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, `Default`, builder methods). JSON shape for sm-fem/protocol: `{"patches":"sets"|"classification"|{"explicit":[{"set":"xmin","name":"inlet","patch_type":"patch"}]},"cell_zones":"all"|"none"|{"names":["b1"]},"face_zones":["s"],"decks":[...]}`.
  - Output model: `PolyMesh {points: Vec<[f64;3]>, face_offsets: Vec<u64>, face_points: Vec<u32>, owner: Vec<u32>, neighbour: Vec<u32>, n_cells: u32, patches: Vec<PolyPatch{name, patch_type, start_face: u32, n_faces: u32, origin: PatchOrigin}>, cell_zones: Vec<PolyZone{name, labels: Vec<u32>}>, face_zones: Vec<PolyFaceZone{name, labels, flips: Vec<bool>}>}` with `n_faces()`, `n_internal()`, `face(i) -> &[u32]`. `PatchOrigin {Set(String), Classification(EntityId), Default, NonConformal, PartitionCut}`.
  - `pub struct FoamBuild {pub mesh: PolyMesh, pub face_of_foam: Vec<u32>, pub point_of_foam: Vec<u32>, pub report: FoamReport}`; `FoamReport {n_points, n_cells, n_faces, n_internal_faces, shape_counts: {hexahedra, prisms, pyramids, tetrahedra, polyhedra} (OpenFOAM checkMesh names, from `CellShape`), patches: Vec<PatchSummary>, loss: LossReport}`.
  - `pub fn build_polymesh(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &FoamOptions) -> Result<FoamBuild>`.
  - `pub struct FoamFile {pub name: String, pub bytes: Vec<u8>}`; `PolyMesh::files(&self) -> Result<Vec<FoamFile>>` (`points faces owner neighbour boundary`, plus `cellZones`/`faceZones` only when non-empty); `PolyMesh::write_dir(&self, dir: &Path) -> Result<()>` (creates dir, removes stale `cellZones`/`faceZones`); `PolyMesh::write_tar<W: Write>(&self, w: &mut W, prefix: &str) -> Result<()>` (deterministic ustar: mtime 0, uid/gid 0, mode 0644, sorted names, so a multi-file format fits the `Write`-based export contract); `pub fn write_polymesh(mesh, opts, dir) -> Result<FoamReport>`. These are the names the integration task's export bundle must call (`foam::build_polymesh`, `PolyMesh::files`, `write_tar`).
  - `pub fn parse_polymesh(files: &[FoamFile]) -> Result<PolyMesh>` and `pub fn read_polymesh_dir(dir: &Path) -> Result<PolyMesh>`: ASCII only (`format binary` gives `Unsupported`), handles `//` and `/* */` comments, the `FoamFile` header, `N(...)`, `N\n(\n...\n)` and `List<label> N(...)` list forms, nested `3(0 1 2)` face lists, and the boundary dictionary. Hostile input returns `Malformed`, never panics, and never allocates more than the input length implies.
  - `pub fn check_polymesh(m: &PolyMesh, tol: f64) -> Vec<FoamIssue>` (`FoamIssue {code: &'static str, face: Option<u32>, cell: Option<u32>, detail}`): codes `foam.index_range`, `foam.owner_not_below_neighbour`, `foam.not_upper_triangular`, `foam.patch_range`, `foam.point_unused`, `foam.cell_too_few_faces`, `foam.cell_unclosed` (`|sum Sf| > tol * sum |Sf|`), `foam.cell_nonpositive_volume`, `foam.face_orientation` (`Sf . (fc - cc) <= 0` for owner). Mirrors checkMesh's topology part in-process; it does not decide mesh validity (that stays `sm_mesh_api::validate`).
  - **Ordering rules.** Cells keep our indices (no renumbering; `renumberMesh` is the user's tool), so `cellZones` labels are our cell indices. Face order is `[internal][patch 0][patch 1]...`. Internal faces are those with `face_right` present and kind `Interior`, ordered by `(owner=face_left, neighbour=face_right, source index)`. If the source order already has that key non-decreasing, take it as is; otherwise `par_sort_unstable_by_key`. A face with `left >= right` is `FoamError::Orientation{face}`, not a silent flip. Patch faces keep ascending source index. Loops are written as stored (smallest vertex first, outward from `left`, which is the owner). Points are our vertices in order; unreferenced vertices are dropped and the rest renumbered in order (`point_of_foam`, loss `foam.unused_points_dropped`). High-order extra points are never written.
  - **Patch assignment** (sequential, deterministic; sets taken in ascending set id). `Sets`: every `Faces` or `Sides` set whose members are all `FaceKind::Boundary` (Sides: `Left` only) becomes a patch named by the sanitized set name; the first set to claim a face wins and the rest is loss `foam.patch_overlap`; ineligible sets are loss `foam.set_skipped`. `Explicit`: only the listed sets, in listed order; overlap is `FoamError::PatchOverlap{face, a, b}`; an unknown set is `UnknownSet`. `Classification`: one patch `topo_face_<serial>` per `TopoFace` of `face_class`, ascending serial. Unassigned `Boundary` faces go to `default_patch` (loss `foam.default_patch`, count). `NonConformal` and `PartitionCut` faces go to patches `nonConformal` and `partitionCut` of type `patch` (loss `foam.nonconformal_as_patch`, `foam.partition_cut_as_patch`). Empty patches are omitted. A patch whose set is a mirror deck's `face_set` (matched through `opts.decks`) gets `SymmetryPlane` only if all its faces are coplanar (`max |n.(p-p0)| <= 1e-9 * bbox diagonal`); otherwise `Patch` plus loss `foam.symmetry_not_planar`. Translation and rotation decks are written as plain patches (loss `foam.deck_cyclic_not_emitted`).
  - **Zones.** `cellZones`: each non-empty `Cells` set per `ZoneSelect`, in set id order, labels ascending. `faceZones`: the named `Faces` or `Sides` sets; `flips[i] = true` iff the entry is a `Right` side; a face listed with both sides keeps the `Left` entry (loss `foam.zone_both_sides`). Labels are mapped through the foam face order.
  - **Names.** Characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9_.+-]` become `_`. An empty result or a collision after sanitizing is `FoamError::BadName`/`DuplicateName`; nothing is renamed silently.
  - **Files.** Header `FoamFile {version 2.0; format ascii; arch "LSB;label=32;scalar=64"; class ...; location "constant/polyMesh"; object ...;}`, with `note "nPoints:.. nCells:.. nFaces:.. nInternalFaces:.."` on `faces`/`owner`/`neighbour`. Classes: `vectorField`, `faceList`, `labelList`, `polyBoundaryMesh`, `regIOobject`. Scalars use Rust shortest round-trip text (`{:?}`), so parse-back is bit-exact; NaN or infinity is `FoamError::NonFinite`. The boundary entry is `type`, optional `inGroups List<word> 1(wall);` for `wall`, then `nFaces`, `startFace`. Formatting is parallel over fixed 4096-line chunks concatenated in order.
  - `FoamError` (thiserror): `NotThreeDimensional`, `InactiveCells{count}`, `NonFinite`, `Orientation{face}`, `UnknownSet`, `PatchOverlap`, `BadName`, `DuplicateName`, `Parse{file, line, detail}`, `Unsupported`, `Malformed`.
  - `examples/foam_fixtures.rs` (`cargo run -p sm-meshio --example foam_fixtures -- OUT`): for each `all_mesh_fixtures()` entry (plus a sets-bearing 2x2x2 block) writes `OUT/<name>/constant/polyMesh/*` and `OUT/<name>/expected.json` (the `FoamReport`). `tools/conformance.sh` (orchestrator-owned) feeds this to `checkMesh -allGeometry -allTopology`. Not run in worker CI.
- **Scope C: `.smsh` 1.1** (`VERSION_MINOR = 1`; reader accepts 1.0 and 1.1). No tracking section in W3a (decision 12: the tracker cache is a `.smrt` sidecar; embedding as a `.smsh` chunk is W3b).
  - Header `element_types: [{set, element_type, shape}]` (always written, possibly `[]`; `set` names a cell set, `shape` is the `cell_shape` code). Names: `HEX8 HEX20 HEX27 TET4 TET10 WEDGE6 WEDGE15 PYRAMID5 PYRAMID13 QUAD4 QUAD8 TRI3 TRI6 POLYHEDRON POLYGON` (decision 12 spelling; the same spellings as the stage-0/integration `sm_fem::ElementType::name()` including `Polyhedron`; Exodus `NFACED`/`NSIDED` is an exporter mapping in sm-exodus, never written to `.smsh`). API: `SmshWriter::element_type(self, set: impl Into<String>, element_type: impl Into<String>) -> Self`; `pub fn element_type_shape(name: &str) -> Option<CellShape>`. Checked at write: the set exists and is a `Cells` set; every member's `cell_shape` equals the table shape; HO names need a HO record with the right node count on every member (HEX20 20, HEX27 27, TET10 10, WEDGE15 15, PYRAMID13 13, QUAD8 8, TRI6 6); one record per set; a cell in two typed sets must get the same type. Violations are `Inconsistent`/`Unsupported`.
  - `mesh.stats`: `{n_tet, n_hex, n_wedge, n_pyramid, n_polyhedron, n_tri, n_quad, n_polygon, n_interior_faces, n_boundary_faces, n_nonconformal_faces, n_partition_cut_faces, max_cell_faces, max_face_vertices}` (derived; for consumers that preallocate ray-tracing and transport scratch). `SmshFile::decode` recomputes and returns `Malformed` on mismatch.
  - No other polyhedral additions: `cell_faces` CSR, `cell_shape` 4 and zero-length corner rows already hold arbitrary polyhedra exactly. Say so in `SMSH_FORMAT.md`.
  - `SmshMesh` gains `element_types: Vec<(String, String)>`; `readers/smsh.py` gains `element_types()` and `stats()`; the v1.0 reader rule stays (a minor bump adds only optional fields).
- **Scope D: VTU polyhedron check.** `VTK_POLYHEDRON` with `faces`/`faceoffsets` already exists (`src/vtu.rs`, oracle test `fixed_oracle_polyhedron`). Add tests only: `dual_patch`, `hexagonal_prism`, the stage-0 `nonconvex_poly` fixture, and a hex stacked on `split_face_cube` (hex plus polyhedron: `faceoffsets` is `-1` for the hex, `faces` has outward loops) decode to the fixture volumes via the divergence theorem. No writer change expected; if a defect appears, fix it.
- **Acceptance (mandatory; all analytic, run in `cargo test -p sm-meshio`):**
  1. Counts (points/cells/faces/internal): `hex_block_2x2x2` 27/8/36/12 (24 boundary faces), `mixed_transition` 12/4/17/3 (14), `dual_patch` 48/7/44/12 (32), `hexagonal_prism(1,2)` 12/1/8/0, `single_hex` 8/1/6/0, `single_tet` 4/1/4/0. Every fixture of `all_mesh_fixtures()` gives `n_points = count(Vertex)`, `n_cells = count(Cell)`, `n_faces = count(Face)`. `shape_counts`: `mixed_transition` hexahedra 1, prisms 1, pyramids 1, tetrahedra 1, polyhedra 0; `dual_patch` polyhedra 7 (the hexagonal prisms are 8-face polyhedra). Verify the `dual_patch` classification against `CellShape` before pinning; if the fixture's shapes differ, pin the fixture's true values.
  2. Round trip: `parse_polymesh(files()) == build.mesh` with bit-equal points, for all fixtures.
  3. Orientation: for every cell, `|sum of owner-outward minus neighbour-inward Sf| <= 1e-12 * sum |Sf|`, and the volume from the foam arrays equals `fixture.cell_volumes[c]` to relative 1e-12 (`dual_patch`: `3 sqrt3 / 2`). `check_polymesh(.., 1e-9)` returns no issues on every fixture; `sm_mesh_api::validate` is clean on the input.
  4. Ordering: `owner[i] < neighbour[i]`; internal `(owner, neighbour)` strictly increasing for the conformal fixtures; patch starts are `n_internal + prefix sums` and the sizes sum to `n_faces - n_internal`; `face_of_foam` is a permutation; each foam face has the same vertex set as its source face, and an internal face has `owner == face_left`, `neighbour == face_right`. A hand-built mesh whose boundary faces interleave with interior faces (cell 0 with a boundary face between two interior faces) proves the reordering.
  5. Patches and zones: a 2x2x2 block with face sets `xmin xmax ymin ymax zmin zmax` gives 6 patches of 4 faces in set-id order; block sets `b1` (cells 0..3) and `b2` (4..7) give `cellZones` with exactly those labels; an unlisted boundary face lands in `defaultFaces` with loss `foam.default_patch` count 1; overlapping sets give `foam.patch_overlap` (Sets) and `PatchOverlap` (Explicit); `Classification` gives one patch per `TopoFace` (6 for the box); a `NonConformal` face (test wrapper over `MeshRead`, as in `tests/vtu.rs`) goes to `nonConformal`; a non-planar set marked mirror gives `Patch` plus `foam.symmetry_not_planar`; a faceZone from a `Sides` set with `Right` entries has the matching `flips`.
  6. Golden bytes: `tests/data/foam/single_hex/{points,faces,owner,neighbour,boundary}` are hand-written and equal the output (cube, outward faces from the stored loops, all owner 0).
  7. Hostile parse: truncated file, `format binary`, negative or huge counts, an index past `n_points`, and proptest random bytes all give `Err` and never panic or allocate beyond the input size.
  8. Tar: `write_tar` output re-read by a 30-line test untar equals `files()`, header checksums valid, mtime 0, byte-identical across runs.
  9. `.smsh` 1.1: a committed v1.0 golden (`tests/data/v10_hex_block.smsh`, produced by the unmodified writer as the first commit of the task) still decodes; a 1.1 file with `element_types=[{block_1,HEX8,1}]` on a 1000-cell block round-trips; a `POLYHEDRON` record on a `dual_patch` cell set round-trips; writing `TET4` on hexes, an unknown set, a duplicate record, a missing HO record and the spelling `NFACED` each fail with a typed error; `stats` matches the mesh (`mixed_transition`: 1 hex, 1 wedge, 1 pyramid, 1 tet, 3 interior and 14 boundary faces, `max_cell_faces` 6); the Python reader returns the same values; `tests/corrupt.rs` gains a tampered `stats`.
  10. Loss reports: `vtu_loss_report` of `ho_hex` gives `vtu.high_order_dropped` count 1 and `vtu.extra_points_unused` 19; of a mesh with a face set gives `vtu.face_sets_dropped`; `SmshWriter::loss_report` is empty.
  11. VTU polyhedron check (Scope D): the four meshes decode to fixture volumes to relative 1e-12.
  12. Determinism: `sm_testkit::determinism::check` over all foam files (every fixture plus a 40x40x40 hex block, larger than every chunk) for `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default and two runs; `.smsh` bytes with `element_types` likewise.
  13. `cargo build`, `cargo test -p sm-meshio` (also `--all-features`), `cargo clippy -p sm-meshio --all-targets -- -D warnings`, rustdoc on every public item.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance):** (a) `tests/vtu_check.py` running VTK's reader on the polyhedron outputs when `vtk` is importable; (b) criterion bench `foam_polymesh` on 64,000 hexes, reported in `W3_REPORT.md`, record-only (no gate; bulk commit is W3b); (c) `FOAM_FORMAT.md` cross-reference table to OpenFOAM v2506 reader quirks. If time is short, these slip, not the tar writer.
- **Must NOT:** change any frozen contract or other crate (sm-fem keeps its own `ElementType`; wiring `.element_type()` and OpenFOAM into `sm_fem::export`/`session.export` is the integration task); depend on a W3a crate still being built; write OpenFOAM binary; renumber cells or tune internal cells for OpenFOAM; emit cyclic/AMI/NCC patches; call `sm_mesh_api::validate` inside the writer; embed tracking in `.smsh`; write `NFACED`/`NSIDED` into `.smsh`; use `unwrap` on input paths; iterate a `HashMap`; add dependencies.
- **Deferred to W3b:** Fluent `.msh` export (spec in `docs/research/fluent-msh.md`, ASCII first), DAGMC h5m and Gmsh msh4 export, `.smsh` tracking chunk, `SmshMesh: MeshRead` (needed for a file-to-file CLI converter), dimension-2 meshes (`empty` patches), inactive-cell (refined parent) skipping, point zones, cyclic pairs from decks.

#### registers
No ops or queries (sm-meshio registers none).
Export format: OpenFOAM polyMesh ASCII (foam::build_polymesh, write_polymesh, PolyMesh::files/write_dir/write_tar).
Parser and checker: foam::parse_polymesh/read_polymesh_dir, foam::check_polymesh.
Export format: .smsh 1.1 (element_types with POLYHEDRON/POLYGON, mesh.stats).
Shared LossReport type; vtu_loss_report; SmshWriter::loss_report.
Cargo example foam_fixtures (for tools/conformance.sh).

#### ACRs
[]

#### fixtures
None new. Uses stage-0 sm-testkit fixtures (`all_mesh_fixtures()`, `nonconvex_poly`). Sets-bearing and interleaved-boundary meshes are built in tests/common with ReferenceBuilder and a MeshRead wrapper (the pattern in tests/vtu.rs); `ho_hex` stays in meshio's own tests/common.

#### depends
[] (no W3a crate; stage-0 only)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to OpenFOAM's own converters (gmshToFoam, fluent3DMeshToFoam, ideasUnvToFoam), which rebuild owner/neighbour by matching faces of cell-shape lists. It differs because it derives polyMesh directly from the face-first IR, whose left/right/outward-loop rule is already OpenFOAM's owner/neighbour/normal convention. So there is no face matching, polyhedra and true prisms stay exact, and patches and cellZones come from our sets and classification. It also returns a loss report and ships an in-process parser and checker, so exporter bugs can be found without OpenFOAM. Conformance failures in checkMesh point at the exporter, because our own validator already approved the mesh.

#### risks
OpenFOAM reader quirks that only the Docker conformance run will reveal: cellZones/faceZones list syntax (List<label> N(...), bool flipMap spelling), inGroups, the arch entry. Mitigation: the parser accepts several spellings; the format is hand-checked against v2506 sources.
Ties in (owner, neighbour) for two cells sharing two faces: checkMesh may require strictly increasing neighbours. Fixtures never hit this; tie-break is source index and the checker reports non-strict order only as a warning.
symmetryPlane patches need exactly planar faces; the exporter downgrades to patch with a loss item instead of failing.
Cell numbering is not bandwidth-optimised (renumberMesh is the user's concern).
Bit-exact float round trip depends on Rust Debug formatting staying shortest-round-trip (guaranteed by std, pinned by test).
Large meshes: PolyMesh duplicates the face CSR (about 12M u32 per million hex cells); acceptable, measured in the stretch bench.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
Default type of the unassigned-boundary patch: patch (chosen) or wall.
Should unlisted ineligible face sets (holding interior faces) become faceZones automatically? Chosen: no, opt-in by name.
Tar stream vs a directory sink for the multi-file export through the Write-based sm_fem::export and session.export contract (integration decides; `ExportResult.files` from stage 0 already allows listing several files).
Foam export refuses inactive cells (InactiveCells) rather than extracting the leaf mesh.

#### escalation
No stage-0 contract change is needed beyond items already in decision 12. For the orchestrator: (1) the integration task must call the names above and use `POLYHEDRON` in sm-fem; (2) whether `FoamReport`/`LossReport` is surfaced through `ExportResult` (a later additive protocol field) or only logged.

### W3-POLY. `sm-poly` (L3): whole-mesh tet -> polyhedral conversion (barycentric dual, classification-driven)

- **Decision: this is an IR -> IR op, not a `VolumeScheme`.** `crates/sm-scheme-api/DESIGN.md` ("Transforms") says element conversions are mesh passes over `MeshRead -> MeshBuild` in mesh-op crates. `MeshTransform::apply` cannot create nodes, and `Scheme::generate` sees one target's patch, never the finished mesh. `mesh.to_poly` is a pure function of (state, op), so replay is safe. The `PolyVol` stub (`stubs.rs`, one polyhedron per brick) is unrelated. The `scheme-poly -> mesh-driver` edge in the concept graph is superseded (stage-0 docs item; poly does not edit docs).
- **Ruling (decision 12, binding).** `mesh.to_poly {mesh, keep_source?: bool = true}` converts a WHOLE all-tet mesh entry into a NEW mesh entry. There is no `targets`: partial dualization of a mixed mesh needs conformize and is deferred to W3b. Output must pass `validate` and `validate_geometry` with default `GeomTol`: non-planar dual faces are split into planar pieces (volume conservation exact). The milestone calls poly in a timeline fork where the part is tet-meshed; the source mesh is kept by default so the main-line exports are unaffected.
- **Layer and deps.** L3. Normal deps: sm-core, sm-mesh-api, sm-mesh (production `MeshBuilder`), sm-op-api, sm-layer-api, sm-topo-api (`TopologyView`, for the resolver only), rayon, thiserror, serde, schemars (decision 9/11: serde, schemars, thiserror, rayon accepted). Dev: sm-testkit (stage-0 fixtures), proptest, criterion (optional). Not sm-quality, sm-mesher, any scheme crate, or any other W3a crate. All fixtures come from `sm_testkit::meshes` (stage 0); there is no inline-`build_mesh` fallback and no fixture ACR.
- **Stage-0 items this task relies on (exist or are being added; referenced, not requested):** `sm_testkit::meshes::{tet_block, tet_block_two_volumes, tet_block_warped, tet_lshape}` (decision 12 "tet_block family"; Kuhn 6-tet lattice classified as a CAD box with 8 TopoVertex, 12 TopoEdge, 6 TopoFace, 1 TopoVolume; needs n >= 2 per axis); `MeshLayer`/`insert_mesh`/`retire_mesh` (sm-layer-api, frozen); skeleton crate `crates/sm-poly` with `PolyPlugin` (unit struct, id `"poly"`; keep it); element-type spelling `POLYHEDRON`/`POLYGON` (decision 12). If `tet_block` or the `ModelLayer` built from its topology is not on main when work starts, STOP and report `blocked_on_api` (do not duplicate fixtures).

- **Scope (mandatory)**
  - **Dual rule (barycentric, polyDualMesh family).** Input: a conformal all-`CellShape::Tet` 3D mesh with `Interior`/`Boundary` faces only. A *region* is the set of tets sharing a cell classification (`TopoVolume`). Dual cells are keyed `(vertex, region)`: one per vertex per region touching it, so a vertex on a shared model face gets one cell per volume and no cell mixes materials. A triangle is *region-boundary* if the region has exactly one tet on it (true boundary or inter-region interface). Dual points: tet centroids (class: the tet's volume); region-boundary triangle centroids (class: the triangle's `TopoFace`); midpoint `m_e` of every region-boundary edge (class: `TopoFace` if both triangles lie on one face, else the feature `TopoEdge`); primal vertices on a model edge or vertex (own class). Dual points shared by two regions at an interface are one mesh vertex, so interface faces coincide.
  - **Edge faces.** Every primal edge of a region gives exactly one dual face between its two endpoint cells. Interior edge: the cyclic ring of tet centroids around it (`across` walking from the lowest tet index; orientation derived topologically, `debug_assert`ed geometrically). Region-boundary edge: the open path `fc_b1, c_1..c_k, fc_b2` closed by `m_e`.
  - **Boundary faces.** For each (vertex, region), group region-boundary triangles into fans connected through edges that are not *split edges*. A split edge is a boundary edge whose two triangles have different `TopoFace` classes, or whose edge class is a `TopoEdge`, or whose two triangle planes differ by more than the fixed internal coplanarity tolerance (relative, `1e-9`; not an option, so exact boundary tiling is kept). The test depends only on the unordered triangle pair, so both regions at an interface derive identical fans. Each fan is one polygon: closed ring `m_e1, fc_t1, m_e2, ...` inside a smooth planar patch; `v, m_e_first, fc.., m_e_last` for a fan ended by feature edges (feature curves and corners preserved exactly). A vertex with exactly one split edge returns `PolyError::UnsupportedFeature`.
  - **Planarization (always on in the op; library has a test-only mode).** `FaceMode::{Planar, Keep}`, default `Planar`. `Keep` emits one polygon per edge (faces may be non-planar; geometry follows the declared `smartmesh.fan_vertex_mean.v1` rule); it exists so tests can derive counts and cross-check `Planar`, and is NOT reachable from the op. `Planar`: an edge face whose best-plane deviation (relative to `sqrt(area)`, the `GeomTol::planarity` metric) exceeds the default `GeomTol` planarity is replaced by hub triangles `(m_e, p_i, p_{i+1})`; `m_e` becomes a mesh vertex only then, classified on the region volume. A boundary fan that is not planar within that tolerance is split into one quad `v, m_e1, fc_t, m_e2` per triangle (planar, since it lies in the triangle). Interior hub triangles tile the same surface, so volume is conserved exactly and two cells may share several faces.
  - **Edge resolver.** `EdgeResolver` maps (two `TopoFace` ids, endpoint classes) to the `TopoEdge` id of a feature boundary edge. `VertexClassResolver` uses an endpoint's `TopoEdge` class (needs >= 2 intervals per curve). `TopologyResolver<'a>(&'a dyn TopologyView)` intersects the faces' `coedges` and filters by endpoint vertices; the op uses it with `ModelLayer::topo()`. A source edge table, if present, is used first. Failure: `PolyError::AmbiguousEdge{a,b}`.
  - **Output classification.** Dual cells: the region class. Region-boundary faces: the triangle's `TopoFace`, set explicitly (also interface faces between two volumes). Interior faces: left to `finish`. Edge table (`edges: Option<bool>`, default = source has one): dual edges whose endpoints are both dim <= 1 take the dim-1 endpoint's class, the rest derive at `finish`. Classification is carried, never recomputed from geometry. Boundary points sit on the faceted surface (CAD projection deferred).
  - **Cell shapes.** The frozen `MeshBuild` infers `CellShape` from topology at `finish`, so a dual cell with hex/wedge/pyramid topology (e.g. the 4 cells of `single_tet`) may come out as a template shape; only cells with other topology are `CellShape::Polyhedron`. The element type of the output blocks is `POLYHEDRON` at the export/fem level (decision 12); poly does not touch the fem layer or force shapes (see Open).
  - **Public API** (options `#[non_exhaustive]` with setters, serde/schemars; rustdoc on every item):
    - `pub fn dualize<B: MeshBuild>(src: &dyn MeshRead, opts: &PolyOptions, edges: &dyn EdgeResolver, b: B, alloc: &mut SerialAlloc) -> Result<(Finished<B::Output>, DualReport), PolyError>`; `alloc` unchanged on `Err`.
    - `pub struct PolyOptions { pub faces: FaceMode /*Planar*/, pub edges: Option<bool> }` (agglomeration is Stretch and adds its field only if done).
    - `pub struct DualReport { pub cells, pub edge_faces, pub boundary_faces, pub hub_split_faces, pub points: usize, pub max_nonplanarity, pub min_cell_volume, pub total_volume: f64 }`.
    - `pub fn poly_stats(m: &dyn MeshRead) -> PolyStats` (faces per cell min/mean/max, vertices per face, max non-planarity, volume min/max/sum, boundary area).
    - `pub trait EdgeResolver: Send + Sync`, `VertexClassResolver`, `TopologyResolver<'a>`; `pub enum PolyError { NotTet{cell}, Dim2, UnsupportedFaceKind{face}, OpenRing{edge}, AmbiguousEdge{a,b}, UnsupportedFeature{vertex}, Mesh(MeshError) }` (thiserror, `#[non_exhaustive]`).
  - **Op `mesh.to_poly`** (exact shape): args `{ mesh: Mesh /*required*/, keep_source?: bool /*true*/ }`, `deny_unknown_fields`, `x-entity-kind: Mesh` on `mesh`; output role `mesh` (`Cardinality::One`). Body: read the source `MeshEntry` and the `ModelLayer` through Arc clones; refuse (typed `OpError::Invalid`, no state change) a non-3D mesh, a mesh with any non-tet cell (mapping `NotTet`/`Dim2`), a mesh that is already polyhedral, and a `NonConformal` face; run `dualize` with a cloned `SerialAlloc`, `sm_mesh::MeshBuilder`, `TopologyResolver` from `ModelLayer::topo()`; `insert_mesh(.., "mesh")`; copy `selection`, `plan`, `quanta`. `keep_source:false` additionally calls `retire_mesh(source)`. All atomic. With the default `keep_source:true` the volume then has two mesh entries: `session.export` without a mesh named returns the existing `ambiguous-mesh` error (decision 8), so callers name the mesh; document this in `DESIGN.md`.
  - **Query `mesh.poly_info {mesh} -> PolyStats`.** `PolyPlugin` registers the op and the query only, never layers (`CoreLayers` does).
  - **Determinism.** Dual points numbered in fixed order: tets, then region-boundary triangles ascending, then `m_e` by sorted vertex pair, then primal feature vertices. Rings, fans, planarity computed with rayon into pre-sized per-edge/per-vertex slots; the builder is fed serially in (region, vertex) order via `add_face` + `add_cell` with `SignedFace::new(f, Side::Left)` for the lower cell (`find_face` only for boundary/interface polygons). No `HashMap` iteration.

- **Acceptance** (numbered; derived counts are the spec, re-derive first and flag any disagreement as a spec error, never weaken silently)
  1. `single_tet` (test `EdgeResolver` minting ids from the face pair): 4 cells, each volume `(1/6)/4` to 1e-14 relative; 6 edge faces plus 12 boundary quads = 18 faces, 12 on the boundary; every face planar to 1e-13; `validate` and `validate_geometry(default)` empty.
  2. `tet_block([3,3,3], [1,1,1])`, `Keep`: 64 cells (= vertex count), 279 edge faces, 96 boundary faces (24x1 + 24x2 + 8x3 faces over boundary vertices), 375 faces, 464 dual points (162 `TopoVolume` + 234 `TopoFace` + 60 `TopoEdge` + 8 `TopoVertex`). Sum of cell volumes 27 to 1e-12 relative; the 8 interior cells each 1 to 1e-12; the 24 face-interior boundary cells each 0.5 to 1e-12; boundary area 54, with 9 on each of the 6 `TopoFace`s via `classified_on`. `validate` empty and `validate_geometry(GeomTol::new().with_planarity(f64::INFINITY))` empty; `report.max_nonplanarity` equals an independent recomputation.
  3. Same fixture through the op path (`Planar`): `validate_geometry(GeomTol::default())` empty (every face planar), sum 27 to 1e-12, interior cells still 1, boundary faces still 96, cells 64, faces >= 375, and `hub_split_faces` equals the number of `Keep` edge faces with non-planarity above the default tolerance (cross-check between the two modes); faces after splitting = 375 - hub_split_faces + (hub triangles emitted) is asserted exactly.
  4. `tet_block_two_volumes([4,2,2], [1,1,1], split=2)`: 54 cells (45 vertices plus 9 duplicated on the interface); no cell mixes volumes; volume 8.0 per `TopoVolume` to 1e-12; exactly 9 interior-interface faces whose two cells lie in different volumes, each classified on the interface `TopoFace`; `validate` and default `validate_geometry` empty (interface faces coincide).
  5. `tet_block_warped` (amp 0.1) and `tet_lshape(m=2, h=1)` (reflex edge): total volume equals the sum of tet volumes to 1e-12 relative; `validate_geometry(GeomTol::default())` empty for the op output (`Planar`), and for `Keep` with planarity infinite; any `GeomSideTet` finding on the L-shape in `Planar` is a test failure to be investigated, not silenced (if it is intrinsic to the reflex cell, report it as `blocked` with the offending cell, do not loosen the tolerance).
  6. Classification: for every source boundary `TopoFace`, its area is recovered as the sum of dual boundary faces classified on it (1e-12); with `edges = true` the edge table is fully classified, accepted by `validate`, and feature-curve edges sit on the right `TopoEdge` (checked on `tet_block([3,3,3])` and `tet_lshape`).
  7. Builder differential: `sm_mesh_api::reference::ReferenceBuilder` and `sm_mesh::MeshBuilder` give equal `topology_hash` and `geometry_hash_quantized`. `sm_testkit::determinism::check`: two runs and `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default give identical hashes; a cold replay of the op shows no `Diverged`.
  8. Op end to end (test-only op inserting a `tet_block` fixture and its model through `insert_mesh`): default call adds a new mesh entry and keeps the source (both ids live, id counts match); `keep_source:false` retires the source's `Mesh*` ids; copied `selection`/`plan`/`quanta` equal the source's. Each of these fails with `OpError::Invalid` and a byte-identical state hash: a second `mesh.to_poly` on the poly mesh, a hex mesh, a mesh with one non-tet cell, an unknown field, `targets` supplied, `mesh` missing. `poly_info` equals `poly_stats`.
  9. proptest: `n` in `[2,5]^3`, `h` in `[0.1,3]^3`: cells = vertices, sum of volumes = `n0 n1 n2 h0 h1 h2` to 1e-12 relative, `validate` and default `validate_geometry` empty for the op path.
  10. Errors: a non-tet cell gives `NotTet`, a dim-2 mesh `Dim2`, a `NonConformal` face `UnsupportedFaceKind`; all typed, none panics; no `unwrap` on input paths.
  11. `DESIGN.md` states the dual rule with a diagram, the count formulas, why interior face centroids are omitted (hub splitting instead), the keep_source/`ambiguous-mesh` consequence, and the polyDualMesh comparison method for the integration harness.

- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-11, in this order)**
  - S1. Bench (informational, never a gate): `tet_block([40,40,40])` (384k tets) dualizes in release; record time and peak RSS in `DESIGN.md`. Serial builder commit may dominate; bulk commit is W3b, so the figure is recorded, not gated.
  - S2. `Agglomerate{min_volume_ratio, max_concavity (0.1), max_rounds}` library option (default off, NOT exposed through the op in W3a): serial greedy matching on the internal `PolyData`, merging cells below `min_volume_ratio * median` with the equal-class neighbour of largest shared face area (ties lowest index) when shared faces form one edge-connected patch and concavity `max_{f,v}(x_v - apex_f).n_f / V^(1/3)` stays under the limit; never across classes. Acceptance if done: on `tet_block([4,4,4])`, ratio 0.75, cell count drops by exactly `report.merges`, volume sum unchanged to 1e-12, `validate`/default `validate_geometry` clean, interface faces survive on the two-volume fixture, `max_rounds = 0` is the identity. Merged cells must still be planar-faced (merging removes faces only).
  - S3. Op-exposed `faces: "keep"` and smooth-fan merging by dihedral angle (`merge_angle_deg`): only if a transport consumer asks; both give up default-tolerance validity or exact tiling.
- **Deferred to W3b (known gaps):** partial dualization of a mixed mesh via `targets` (needs conformize); circumcentric/Voronoi dual; vertex-moving planarization; projecting boundary dual points onto the CAD surface; hybrid (non-tet) input; seam edges (a `TopoEdge` with the same `TopoFace` on both sides) and a vertex with one split edge; parallel/bulk builder commit (backlog item; gates the 1e7-cell target); concavity repair beyond the agglomeration guard; `mesh.quality`-style diagnostics (sm-diag owns them).
- **Must NOT:** edit any contract crate, sm-testkit, `docs/` or the root `Cargo.toml`; add or request fixtures (use the stage-0 `tet_block` family); register a `Scheme`, `MeshTransform` or layer; depend on sm-quality, sm-mesher, sm-diag or any scheme crate; add `targets`, `faces`, `merge_angle_deg` or `agglomerate` to the `mesh.to_poly` args in W3a; move source vertices or mutate the input mesh; tune the dual to one tool (OpenFOAM/Fluent quirks belong to exporters); run `checkMesh` in unit tests (our `validate` is the judge); iterate a `HashMap` where order reaches output; wire `PolyPlugin` into the distribution (orchestrator); write the fem layer or set element types.

#### registers
op `mesh.to_poly` (PolyPlugin, id "poly"); query `mesh.poly_info`; no layers, no export formats.

#### ACRs
none (the former four sm-testkit fixture ACRs are stage-0 items: `tet_block`, `tet_block_two_volumes`, `tet_block_warped`, `tet_lshape`).

#### fixtures (stage 0, sm_testkit::meshes; for reference)
- `tet_block(n:[u32;3], h:[f64;3])`: V=(n0+1)(n1+1)(n2+1), T=6 n0 n1 n2, volume n0 n1 n2 h0 h1 h2. n=[3,3,3], h=1: 64 cells, 279 edge faces, 96 boundary faces, 464 dual points; interior cells h0h1h2, face-interior boundary cells half.
- `tet_block_two_volumes(n, h, split)`: n=[4,2,2], split=2: 54 cells, 9 interface faces, volume 8 per side.
- `tet_block_warped(n, h, amp)`: total volume = sum of tet volumes.
- `tet_lshape(m, h)`: volume 3 m^3 h^3, one reflex `TopoEdge`.

#### depends
[] (no other W3a crate; stage-0 testkit fixtures only)

#### baseline
Conceptually OpenFOAM `polyDualMesh` / STAR-CCM+ polyhedral conversion (one cell per vertex, one face per primal edge, tet centroids as dual points, boundary polygons from boundary-triangle fans). Differences: (1) feature edges and corners come from the exact model classification, not an angle threshold; (2) classification is carried onto every dual vertex, face and cell, so sidesets, blocks and the validator keep working; (3) multi-material aware, with one cell per (vertex, volume) and conformal interface faces; (4) volume is conserved by construction and every face is planar (non-planar rings split into planar pieces), so the output passes `validate_geometry` at default tolerance and gives ray tracing exact plane tests.

#### risks
- Boundary dual points are triangle centroids and edge midpoints on the faceted surface; curved bodies deviate by the chord error until a projection pass exists (W3b).
- Planar splitting raises faces per cell (roughly 3-4x where rings are non-planar) and lets two cells share several faces; downstream sweep/upwind code must accept multiple faces per cell pair.
- Reflex boundary corners (`tet_lshape`) may give concave dual cells and non-positive fan side tets; acceptance 5 tests it and reports, it does not repair.
- Feature-edge identity for a single-interval curve needs topology (`TopologyResolver`); `VertexClassResolver` fails there; tet generators must give >= 2 intervals per curve for pure-mesh tests.
- Ring walking assumes a conformal, edge-manifold tet mesh; slivers from the future tetmesh could give tiny or inverted dual cells.
- Interface conformity depends on both regions deriving identical fans and identical planarity decisions (set-symmetric split rule); covered by acceptance 4.
- The counts in acceptance 2 were derived by hand; the worker re-checks them first.
- Two live meshes per volume after the default `keep_source:true`: the `mesh.run` neighbour guard and no-mesh-named export see ambiguity (intended; documented).

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
(see the orchestrator list returned with this entry)

#### escalation (orchestrator)
1) Op name `mesh.to_poly` is fixed by decision 12 (no `mesh.convert_poly`, no `mesh.convert` front door in W3a). 2) Concept graph: `scheme-poly -> mesh-db` plus `classification-api` instead of `mesh-driver` (docs, stage 0). 3) `PolyPlugin` is installed after `CoreLayers` by sm-server/sm-cli at integration; the polyDualMesh comparison needs the OpenFOAM exporter and the tetmesh output. 4) Integration milestone must call `mesh.to_poly {mesh: <tet mesh>}` in a fork where the part is tet-meshed, and name the mesh explicitly on later exports.

### W3-DIAG. Validation and quality diagnostics: `sm-quality` FV delta (D1, L2) then new `sm-diag` (D2, L3)

- **Placement (decision 12 layers).** sm-quality is L2 (deps sm-core + sm-mesh-api only). sm-diag is L3 and depends on sm-quality (L2) and contract crates only. No same-layer dependency exists. sm-quality stays a pure numerics crate with no ops, serde or schemars. sm-diag owns the wire types, so the JSON shape is pinned in one place.
- **Ownership (decision 12, one-crate exception, already approved).** One worker owns `crates/sm-quality/` and `crates/sm-diag/`, doing D1 first and D2 second, in that order, in one worktree. It edits no other crate, no root manifest, no CI, no docs outside those two crates.
- **Stage-0 items this task relies on (by name; they exist on main when the task starts, per decision 12).**
  - `MeshLayer::replace_mesh` and `sm_layer_api::add_mesh_set(ctx, mesh, name, members, role) -> Result<EntityId, OpError>`. sm-diag uses `add_mesh_set` for `mesh.flag_cells`, and no other way to mint a set id.
  - The sm-diag skeleton (`crates/sm-diag/`, currently depending on sm-op-api only). The worker adds the dependencies below to its Cargo.toml and adds no new external crates beyond serde, schemars, serde_json, thiserror, rayon, which already exist in the lock file.
  - The re-laddering of sm-quality to L2 and the sm-diag node in ARCHITECTURE.md and concepts.yaml.
  - sm-testkit `meshes::build_mesh`, `meshes::all_mesh_fixtures`, `broken::Tampered`, `determinism::check`, and the stage-0 W3a fixture set. All fixtures needed here are built in-test with these; no new testkit fixture is required.
- **Deps of D2.** sm-core, sm-mesh-api (features `serde`, `schemars`), sm-quality, sm-op-api, sm-layer-api, rayon, serde, schemars, serde_json, thiserror. Dev: sm-testkit, sm-mesh (`generate::hex_block`), sm-layer-api (`CoreLayers`), sm-mesher (dev only, for the end-to-end `mesh.run` case; it is on main, acyclic). sm-diag depends on no unmerged W3a crate. Suggestions name ops by string only. sm-quality gains no new dependencies.

#### D1. `sm-quality` delta (files `fv.rs`, `profile.rs`, DESIGN.md section "Finite-volume metrics")
Conventions follow OpenFOAM `checkMesh` (owner = `face_left`, neighbour = `face_right`; `Sf` points left to right). Face centre and area vector use `sm_mesh_api::face_geometry` (OpenFOAM's rule: vertex-mean estimate, area-weighted triangle centroids). Cell centre is OpenFOAM's pyramid rule: `cEst` = mean of face centres, `pyr3Vol_f = Sf_out . (Cf - cEst)`, centre = `sum(pyr3Vol_f (3/4 Cf + 1/4 cEst)) / sum(pyr3Vol_f)`, volume = `sum(pyr3Vol_f)/3`. For planar faces the volume equals `sm_mesh_api::cell_volume`; for warped faces it differs and DESIGN says so. Pyramid volumes are not clipped to `vSmall`.
- Public API (rustdoc on all):
  - `fv_geometry(mesh: &dyn MeshRead) -> Result<FvGeometry, QualityError>`. `FvGeometry` holds SoA columns `face_area`, `face_centre`, `cell_centre`, `cell_volume`. A dimension-2 mesh gives `QualityError::BadArgument`. Inactive cells and faces give NaN.
  - `FvMetric` (`#[non_exhaustive]`): `NonOrthogonality`, `Skewness`, `OrthogonalQuality`, `VolumeRatio`, `InterpolationWeight`, `FaceFlatness`, `FaceWarp`, `FaceConcavity`, `CellDeterminant`. Methods: `ALL`, `entity() -> FvEntity{Face,Cell}`, `name()`, `from_name()`, `unit()`.
  - `fv_compute(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, geom: &FvGeometry, metrics: &[FvMetric]) -> Result<Vec<FvColumn>, QualityError>`. Each column is face- or cell-indexed. Parallel over fixed chunks of `CHUNK`, one output slot per entity, no cross-thread accumulation.
  - `face_to_cell(mesh: &dyn MeshRead, face_values: &[f64], r: Reduce) -> Vec<f64>` with `Reduce::{Max,Min}`; skips NaN.
  - `Metric::name() -> &'static str` and `Metric::from_name(&str) -> Option<Metric>` (existing Verdict `Metric`), snake_case of the variant, round-trip tested over `Metric::ALL`.
- Formulas (each written in DESIGN.md beside the checkMesh / Fluent definition, naming the OpenFOAM function it mirrors; anything the worker could not verify is marked "recalled, to be diffed against checkMesh by the conformance harness"):
  - `NonOrthogonality` (internal faces, degrees): `acos(clamp(d.Sf/(|d||Sf|)))`, `d = C_N - C_O`. checkMesh fails above 70. Cell value = max over its internal faces.
  - `Skewness` (dimensionless): `sv = Cpf - (Sf.Cpf)/(Sf.d) d`, `Cpf = Cf - C_O`. Boundary face: `d = n (n.Cpf)`. `fd = max(0.2|d|, max_p |svHat.(p - Cf)|)`, `skew = |sv|/fd`. checkMesh fails above 4 (internal) and 20 (boundary).
  - `OrthogonalQuality` (Fluent, cell): min over faces of `cos(Sf, Cf - C_c)`, and over internal faces also `cos(Sf, C_n - C_c)`. 1 is best. Fluent guidance: warn below 0.1, fail below 0.01.
  - `VolumeRatio` (internal face): `max(V_O,V_N)/min(V_O,V_N)` >= 1. OpenFOAM's `faceVolRatio` is the reciprocal; DESIGN states the conversion.
  - `InterpolationWeight` (internal face): `w = |Sf.(C_N - Cf)| / (|Sf.(C_N - Cf)| + |Sf.(Cf - C_O)|)`, reported as `min(w, 1-w)`. 0.5 ideal; checkMesh fails below 0.05.
  - `FaceFlatness`: `|Sf| / sum_i |A_i|` over fan triangles about `Cf`. 1 is flat; checkMesh warns below 0.8.
  - `FaceWarp` (degrees): max angle between a fan-triangle normal and `Sf`.
  - `FaceConcavity` (degrees): at each vertex where `(e0 x e1).n_hat < 0`, `asin(min(1, |e0 x e1|/(|e0||e1|)))`; the face value is the max, 0 if convex. checkMesh fails above 10.
  - `CellDeterminant`: `det(M)/(tr(M)/3)^3`, `M = sum_f Sf (x) Sf/|Sf|` over all faces. 1 for a cube; checkMesh fails below 1e-3.
- `profile.rs`: `Profile {Generic, OpenFoam, Fluent, Transport}` with `name()`/`from_name()`. `Measure {Verdict(Metric), Fv(FvMetric)}`. `Sense {LowerIsWorse, HigherIsWorse}`. `Rule {measure, sense, warn: Option<f64>, fail: f64, fail_boundary: Option<f64>, note: &'static str}` with `grade(v, boundary) -> Grade{Ok,Warn,Fail,Undefined}`. `rules(Profile) -> &'static [Rule]`. A value equal to a limit passes. Numbers are listed in DESIGN.md and marked provisional:
  - Generic: ScaledJacobian warn 0.2 / fail 0.05; EdgeRatio 10 / 100; NonOrthogonality 50 / 70; Skewness 2 / 4 (boundary 20); VolumeRatio 4 / 20; InterpolationWeight 0.2 / 0.05; FaceFlatness 0.9 / 0.8; FaceConcavity 5 / 10.
  - OpenFoam: exactly the checkMesh limits above.
  - Fluent: OrthogonalQuality only (equiangle skewness is a documented gap).
  - Transport: FaceFlatness 0.9999 / 0.999; FaceConcavity 0.1 / 1; Starness 0.2 / 0; EdgeRatio 20 / 200; VolumeRatio 10 / 100. Suits planar convex faces for ray tracing and sweeps; the user ratifies these numbers (see open).

#### D2. `sm-diag` (L3)
`DiagPlugin` (id `"diag"`) registers queries `mesh.validate` and `mesh.quality` and op `mesh.flag_cells`. It registers no layer and requires `CoreLayers`. sm-mcp and sm-cli expose registered queries and ops automatically. Installing `DiagPlugin` after `MeshPlugin` in `sm_server::standard()` is the sm-server owner's integration step, not this worker's.
- Common input `mesh?: EntityId` (`x-entity-kind = ["Mesh"]`). Defaults to the only mesh; with 0 or several meshes and no id, return `OpError::Invalid` listing the ids.
- Wire rules: no NaN or inf in JSON (undefined is `null`); the Verdict sentinel 1e30 stays a number; entities are `EntityId` strings (`"MeshCell#12"`) plus dense `index`; every output has `schema_version: 1`; all maps are `BTreeMap`.
- **`mesh.validate {mesh?, geometry?: bool=true, planarity?: f64=1e-6, min_volume?: f64=0, limit?: usize=100 (max 10000)}`.**
  - Pure core: `validate_mesh(m: &dyn MeshRead, o: &ValidateOptions, live: Option<&dyn Fn(EntityId) -> bool>) -> ValidateReport`.
  - Sources, in order: `sm_mesh_api::validate`; `validate_geometry` only if no structural code (`ids.order`, `index.range`, `loop.*`, `face.*`, `cell.*`, `shape.*`) was found, otherwise `geometry_checked: false` with a reason; `sm_quality::validity` mapped to `quality.inverted` / `quality.degenerate`; `mesh.stale` (warning) from the entry; `diag.class_retired` (a classification entity no longer live in the state).
  - Violation: `{code, severity: "error"|"warning", entity: {id, index}|null, detail, owner: EntityId|null, near: [EntityId], location: [f64;3]|null}`. `owner` is the entity's own classification. `near` is the sorted, deduplicated model entities of dimension below the owner's, from the entity's vertex classifications, capped at 8. `location` is the vertex point, edge midpoint, face centroid or cell centroid; `null` for an out-of-range index. This per-violation shape (no suggestion field) is the contract; suggestions live only in `mesh.quality`.
  - Report: `{valid, geometry_checked, total, by_code: {code: n}, by_owner: [{owner, count}] (top 10), violations (sorted by severity, code, kind, index, detail; cut to limit), truncated}`.
- **`mesh.quality {mesh?, profile?: "generic"|"openfoam"|"fluent"|"transport"="generic", metrics?: [name], thresholds?: {name: {warn?, fail?}}, worst_n?: usize=10 (max 100), bins?: usize=20 (2..=200)}`.**
  - Pure core: `quality_report(m: &dyn MeshRead, o: &QualityOptions, ops: &dyn Fn(&str) -> bool) -> Result<QualityReport, DiagError>`, where `ops` answers "is this op registered".
  - Metric set: the profile's rules, or `metrics` (graded only if a rule exists). A metric no cell supports (FV on a 2-D mesh, Verdict `Skew` on tets) goes to `skipped: [{metric, reason}]`, never an error.
  - Per metric: `{metric, entity: "face"|"cell", unit, sense, count, undefined, n_sentinel, summary {min,max,mean,std_dev}, threshold {warn,fail,fail_boundary}, n_warn, n_fail, histogram {lo,hi,counts,below,above}, worst: [...], clusters: [...]}`. Summaries and histograms exclude the 1e30 sentinel; `n_sentinel` counts those entities.
  - `worst`: at most `worst_n`, worst first (value by `total_cmp`, then index ascending). Entry `{entity, value, grade, location, owner, near, cells: [ids] (faces only)}`.
  - `clusters`: for each bad entity, every model entity of dimension <= 2 in its touch set (vertex classifications of the entity, or of its cells) gets `n_bad`. `n_total` is the number of entities touching that model entity, from one deterministic pass. `fraction = n_bad/n_total`, `enrichment = fraction/overall_bad_fraction`. Sort by fraction desc, n_bad desc, id asc; keep the top 10.
  - Top level: `{mesh, profile, cells, dim, verdict: "pass"|"warn"|"fail", validity {n_valid,n_degenerate,n_inverted}, metrics, skipped, suggestions}`.
  - Suggestion: `{id, kind, severity, metric, targets: [EntityId], op: string|null, args: object|null, params: object, reason, available: bool}`.
  - **Availability reads the op registry (decision 12).** `QueryCtx` has no registry handle, so sm-diag defines `pub struct OpNames(pub BTreeSet<String>)` and reads it with `ctx.service::<OpNames>()`. sm-server fills it from `Registry::op_names()` when it builds services (integration step, see open). `available = ops(op)`. If the service is absent (a bare test rig), every suggestion with an `op` has `available: false` and `reason` says "op registry not provided". There is no hard-coded op list and no hard-coded "no smoothing op yet": the `smooth` suggestion names op `mesh.smooth` and becomes available automatically when sm-smooth is installed.
  - Suggestion rules (fixed table in `suggest.rs`, documented in DESIGN, deterministic):
    - Cluster on a `TopoEdge` with fraction >= 0.5 and enrichment >= 2: `adjust_intervals`, op `mesh.intervals`, `args: null`, `params: {current_count}` (interior vertices classified on the edge + 1).
    - Cluster on a `TopoFace`: `change_scheme` (op `mesh.scheme`, `args: null`). For FaceFlatness, FaceWarp or FaceConcavity on the Transport profile: `refine_surface` (op `mesh.size`).
    - No cluster with enrichment >= 1.5: `smooth` (op `mesh.smooth`, `args: null`).
    - Inverted or degenerate cells: `fix_geometry_or_scheme`, targeted at the owning volume, `op: null`.
- **`mesh.flag_cells {mesh?, name: string (1..=64, [A-Za-z0-9_.-]), profile?="generic", metrics?: [name], level?: "fail"|"warn"="fail", include_invalid?: bool=true}`** (op, writes `meshes`).
  - Flags a cell if any selected metric grades at or above `level` (face metrics through `face_to_cell`), or if it is invalid (when `include_invalid`).
  - Output role `set` (`Cardinality::Optional`, kind `MeshSet`). The set is `SetMembers::Cells`, stored with `sm_layer_api::add_mesh_set(ctx, mesh, name, members, "set")`; the `meshes` digest changes because sets are inside `topology_hash`.
  - Empty selection succeeds with `ctx.warn("diag.nothing_to_flag", ..)` and no set. A name already used in the mesh gives `OpError::Invalid` and leaves state unchanged. `mesh.delete` retires the set id.
  - Follow-up ops declare `x-entity-kind = ["MeshSet"]` to target it.

#### Acceptance (analytic unless noted; tolerance 1e-12 absolute)
1. **Sheared pair.** Hex A = [0,1]^3 and hex B on [1,2] in x whose x=2 layer is shifted by 2 in y (B's vertices: x=1 layer y in [0,1], x=2 layer y in [2,3]); built with `build_mesh`. Volumes 1 and 1. Shared face (centre (1,0.5,0.5), `d = (1,1,0)`): non-orthogonality 45 degrees, skewness 1.0 (`sv = (0,-0.5,0)`, `fd = 0.5`), weight 0.5. OrthogonalQuality A = 1/sqrt 2, B = 1/sqrt 5. B's boundary face x=2 (centre (2,2.5,0.5), `Cpf = (0.5,1,0)`, `d = (0.5,0,0)`, `sv = (0,1,0)`, `fd = 0.5`) has skewness 2.0. The test also re-derives all values with an independent direct implementation.
2. **Stretched pair** (widths 1 and 2 along x): VolumeRatio 2, InterpolationWeight 1/3, non-orthogonality 0, skewness 0.
3. **CellDeterminant.** Unit cube 1; 1x1x2 brick `M = diag(4,4,2)`, 32*27/1000 = 0.864; regular hexagonal prism (edge 1, height 2, `hexagonal_prism`) `108*sqrt(3)/((12+3*sqrt(3))/3)^3`, about 0.9932.
4. **Face shape.** Saddle quad (0,0,h),(1,0,-h),(1,1,h),(0,1,-h), h=0.25: flatness `1/sqrt(1+4h^2)` = 0.894427191, warp `atan(2h)` = 26.5650511771 degrees. Dart quad (0,0),(2,0),(0.5,0.5),(0,2): concavity `asin(0.8)` = 53.1301023542 degrees. L-shaped hexagon: 90 degrees.
5. **Cross-checks.** FV volumes equal `sm_mesh_api::cell_volume` on planar fixtures (1e-12). `Metric::from_name(m.name()) == Some(m)` over `Metric::ALL`; same for `FvMetric` and `Profile`. Boundary faces give NaN for NonOrthogonality, VolumeRatio and InterpolationWeight; a 2-D mesh gives `BadArgument`.
6. **`mesh.validate` clean.** Every `all_mesh_fixtures()` mesh gives `valid: true` and no violations. The `mesh.run` output of the stub brick, through the same test rig the sm-fem op tests use with sm-mesher's `MeshPlugin`, also gives `valid: true`.
7. **`mesh.validate` on broken meshes.** Through the pure core on `Tampered` (swap_left_right, reverse_loop, move_vertex through the opposite face, declassify): each expected code appears at the right entity, owner equals the entity's classification, location lies inside the cell's bounding box; order is stable under reversal of the input violation list; `by_code` sums to `total`; geometry is skipped with a reason when a structural code is present; a tangled hex yields `quality.inverted`.
8. **`mesh.quality` on the sheared pair.** Profile openfoam: max non-orthogonality 45, verdict `pass`, histogram `counts + below + above + undefined == count`, `worst[0]` is the shared face with centroid (1, 0.5, 0.5). Profile generic with `thresholds: {non_orthogonality: {fail: 40}}`: verdict `fail`, `n_fail = 1`.
9. **Clusters and suggestions.** A 3x3x3 `hex_block` with the vertices classified on one `TopoEdge` displaced so adjacent cells skew: that edge ranks first in `clusters` with fraction and enrichment equal to an independent recount (fraction >= 0.5, enrichment >= 2). The suggestion is `adjust_intervals`, `targets = [that edge]`, `params.current_count = 3`, `op = "mesh.intervals"`. `available` is true when `OpNames` contains `mesh.intervals` and false when the service is absent. A second registry-driven case: `OpNames` without `mesh.smooth` gives `smooth.available = false`, with it `true`.
10. **`mesh.flag_cells`.** Members equal exactly the cells graded fail (computed independently). `outputs.set` is a live `MeshSet`; `count_of(MeshSet)` rises by 1; the state hash changes. A second call with the same name fails and leaves the state hash unchanged. `mesh.delete` retires the set id. Undo/redo and cold replay from the root give no `Diverged`. An empty selection gives a warning diagnostic and no new id.
11. **JSON stability.** Output schemas of both queries and the op's input are snapshotted (`tests/golden/*.schema.json`). One golden reply per query: structure exact, floats to 1e-9 relative. Replies contain no NaN or inf (scan the parsed value).
12. **Determinism.** A 20^3 perturbed block (8000 cells, LCG seeded from the cell index): `mesh.quality` and `mesh.validate` JSON bytes identical for `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` and N, over repeated runs and concurrent callers (`sm_testkit::determinism::check`). D1: every FV column hashed for 1/2/3/4 threads on all fixtures. Property test: a rigid motion, and a uniform scale, leave every FV metric unchanged (1e-9 relative), since all FV metrics are dimensionless or degrees.
13. **Bench (record only, in DESIGN, not asserted).** `mesh.quality` with the generic profile on a 100^3 hex block, 4 cores.

#### Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-12)
- `grow?: u32=0 (max 5)` on `mesh.flag_cells`: adds face-neighbour rings by deterministic BFS; test: `grow: 1` adds exactly the face neighbours.
- `sm_testkit`-style reusable `sheared_hex_pair(s)` helper, only if the orchestrator wants it in testkit (otherwise it stays test-local).
- Explicit `thresholds` override on `mesh.flag_cells` (as in `mesh.quality`).
- Check that every classification entity is a live entity of the ModelLayer topology, beyond state liveness.

#### Deferred to W3b
- Shell-orientation validation for dimension-2 meshes (needs `FaceNormals` over the topology).
- Cell-level aspect ratio in the checkMesh definition; equiangle and equivolume skewness (Fluent); face-pyramid volumes.
- Smoothing and refine ops themselves (suggestions only name them).
- `mesh.unflag_cells` (needs `MeshEdit::remove_set`).
- `DerivedCache` for repeated quality calls.
- Per-material or per-block profiles.
- The checkMesh / Fluent conformance harness (separate integration task; this task provides the numbers and DESIGN formulas).

#### Must NOT
- Edit any crate other than sm-quality and sm-diag, the root manifest, CI, or docs outside those crates; contract crates are untouched (the stage-0 items already exist).
- Call any external tool, or tune a threshold or formula to what an external tool accepts. Our validator is authoritative.
- Mutate state in a query.
- Use `HashMap` iteration, unseeded randomness, or `unwrap` on input paths.
- Emit NaN or inf in JSON.
- Mint ids outside `add_mesh_set`.
- Hard-code which ops exist (availability comes from `OpNames`).
- Depend (normal or dev) on any W3a crate not yet on main (sm-smooth, sm-poly, etc.).
- Duplicate the sm-mesh-api validator.
- Put serde or schemars into sm-quality.

#### registers
queries: mesh.validate, mesh.quality (sm-diag, `DiagPlugin` id "diag")
ops: mesh.flag_cells (sm-diag; role set, kind MeshSet, Cardinality::Optional)
services consumed: `sm_diag::OpNames` (optional; injected by sm-server)
layers: none; export formats: none
sm-quality public additions: FvGeometry, fv_geometry, FvMetric, FvEntity, FvColumn, fv_compute, face_to_cell, Reduce, Profile, Measure, Sense, Rule, Grade, rules, Metric::name/from_name
integration: sm_server::standard() installs DiagPlugin after MeshPlugin and provides `OpNames` from `Registry::op_names()` (sm-server owner); sm-mcp and sm-cli expose it automatically. The integration task must say `DiagPlugin`, not `QualityPlugin`.

#### depends
[] (stage-0 items listed above only)

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to OpenFOAM checkMesh and Fluent's mesh check (orthogonal quality), plus Cubit/Verdict quality reports. It differs in four ways. (1) It returns structured JSON for MCP/CLI where those tools print text; every finding is located, tied to CAD geometry through classification ("bad cells cluster on TopoFace#12 and TopoEdge#7", with an enrichment ratio), and paired with a rule-based suggested action naming a registered op. (2) Validity is decided by our validator (sm_mesh_api::validate plus corner-Jacobian validity), never by an external tool; FV metrics follow checkMesh definitions only so numbers can be diffed. (3) Offending cells can be stored as a MeshSet in the timeline so follow-up ops target them reproducibly. (4) Results are bit-identical for 1 or N threads and replayable.

#### risks
- Several FV formulas are recalled from OpenFOAM primitiveMeshTools (boundary skewness limit 20, flatness fan apex, determinant normalisation, concavity via asin, volume-ratio convention). DESIGN marks each unverified; the conformance harness diffs them. The analytic fixtures pin our own definitions regardless.
- Generic and Transport thresholds are provisional engineering guesses.
- D1 and D2 together are about 2500 lines with tests in one worker (accepted exception).
- `mesh.quality` on 10^6 cells computes all columns per call (about 8 MB per metric per 10^6 cells); no cache in W3a.
- `mesh.flag_cells` sets are append-only and flow into every export as ordinary sets; exporters may later need to skip a reserved prefix.
- Cluster and `near` output rely on classification quality; sloppy classification yields weak hot spots (reported as class.* findings).
- Metric names exposed over MCP become public API; freeze them once released.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
- Who adds `OpNames` to sm-server's services: it is an integration step for the sm-server owner. The orchestrator should add it to the integration task list (sm-diag ships the type and works without it).
- Threshold ratification: the user (nuclear engineer) should ratify the Transport profile before it is advertised.
- Integration task and its D5 text still assume a `QualityPlugin` and per-violation `class + suggestion`; they must adopt the shapes above.
- Whether FV metrics on high-order or edge-table cells use the corner skeleton only (plan assumes yes, matching Verdict).
- Defaulting `mesh` to the only mesh: kept (a pure function of state).

### W3-RT. `sm-rt` (L3): implements the frozen `sm-rt-api` contract (VOLUME_MESH + EXACT_PLANAR cell walking, facet input, BVH locate, cache image)
- **Binding rulings.** Decision 12 (frozen contract `sm-rt-api`, layer C, written in stage 0 from the panel synthesis; W3a implements VOLUME_MESH + EXACT_PLANAR, facet input and BVH locate; ANALYTIC and HIERARCHY are W3b; the cache is a `.smrt` sidecar in the contract's `CacheImage` format; no protocol `tracking` field; no `.smsh` chunk embedding in W3a). Decisions 9-11 (deps bounded to internal crates; dev-deps on contract crates and deterministic rayon allowed; never depend on a W3a crate still being built).
- **Names come from `sm-rt-api` as frozen. This entry uses the synthesis names (`Tracker`, `TrackState`, `Scratch`, `Step`, `StepEnd`, `Located`, `Closest`, `LostInfo`, `BuildInput`, `MeshInput`, `FacetInput`, `BuildOptions`, `BuildReport`, `BuildIssue`, `TrackerBuilder`, `CacheKey`, `CacheImage`, `ContentHash`, `TrackerInfo`, `TrackerCaps`, `RtError`, `RecoveryPolicy`, `BoundarySpec`). If the frozen crate spells one differently, the frozen spelling wins; if a needed capability is missing, return `status: blocked_on_api`. Do not duplicate contract types.**
- **Deps (sm-rt):** sm-core, sm-math (`orient3d`, `orient3d_sos` if present, else the SoS tie-break is local), sm-geom-api, sm-topo-api, sm-mesh-api (`MeshRead`, `face_apex`, `across`, `FAN_VERTEX_MEAN_V1`), sm-model-api (boundaries/decks via `BoundarySpec::add_model_face` only; no `flatten` in W3a), sm-rt-api, rayon, thiserror, crc32fast, serde (only if the contract's `serde` feature is needed). Dev: sm-testkit (`sm_mesh_api::reference::hex_block`, `truncated_octahedron`, `hexagonal_prism`, `mixed_transition`, `dual_patch`, `meshes::nonconvex_poly`, the `sm-rt-api` stub `AnalyticWorld` for cross-checks), proptest. **Also (ruling R2):** sm-op-api, sm-layer-api for the mandatory `RtPlugin`. **Not used:** sm-tess, sm-meshio, sm-engine, sm-rt-capi, `Hash256` (the contract's `ContentHash(u128)` is the hash type).
- **Idea.** `TrackerBuilder` for the mesh-native cell-walking tracker. Build once into flat SoA arrays (face planes, `cell_faces` CSR with signs, per-face fan apex, per-cell region, cell BVH, boundary table); every query runs on those arrays only. Facet input is converted to the same arrays (each closed shell becomes polyhedral cells; see Scope 4). `export_cache()` and `load(image)` give a bit-identical tracker.
- **Scope (mandatory):**
  1. `pub struct MeshTrackerBuilder` implementing `TrackerBuilder` (`builder_version`, `cache_key`, `build(&BuildInput, &BuildOptions) -> Result<(Box<dyn Tracker>, BuildReport), RtError>`, `load(&CacheImage)`). Accepts `BuildInput::Mesh` and `BuildInput::Facets`; every other variant returns `RtError::Unsupported` naming the variant (`Brep`, `World` are W3b). `caps() = VOLUME_MESH | EXACT_PLANAR`; the tracker never reports `ANALYTIC`, `HIERARCHY`, `PERIODIC` in W3a.
  2. **Cell walking.** Per cell, `step` finds the exit face. Convex cells (all faces planar within `planarity_tol`, cell classified convex at build): Cyrus-Beck on the face planes, entry face excluded by identity. Non-convex or non-planar-face cells: shared-edge-consistent Plücker. Face geometry is the `FAN_VERTEX_MEAN_V1` triangle fan about the vertex-mean apex, so chords agree with `cell_volume`. A fan triangle is crossed by the ray LINE iff the three edge functions `orient3d(o, o+d, a, b)` have equal sign; each edge's endpoints are ordered by vertex index (apex edges by `(face, i)`), so two neighbouring triangles always agree; an exact zero resolves by simulation of simplicity with ids = vertex indices, apex id = `n_vertices + face`. Use the filtered/adaptive `sm_math::orient3d`. Exit face = the crossing with minimum `t > t_cur` excluding the entry face by identity; an exit whose orientation is entering (`dir . n_out < 0`) is retried against crossings within 4 tol, else `StepEnd::Lost(cause: SenseMismatch)`. Determinism rules of the contract are binding: lowest `FaceId` wins at edge and vertex exits; a point on a face goes to the cell its direction points into, then the lowest `CellId`; `locate` with `dir = None` on a face uses the contract's hashed direction.
  3. **Events.** `StepEnd::{Limit, CellFace, RegionBoundary, Deck, DomainBoundary, Lost}` exactly as frozen. Deck faces (reflective, periodic) are applied inside `step`: mirror `dir' = dir - 2(dir.n)n` (bit-exact for axis-aligned faces), wrap via `BoundarySpec::wrap_transform` (position within 1e-12 of the analytic image; `entered_via` = mate). Vacuum, White, Albedo are returned as `DomainBoundary` with the state on the face; the tracker draws no RNG. `RecoveryPolicy::Fail | Nudge{tries}`: nudge is `tol * 2^k` along `dir`, counted in `TrackerInfo`/`LostInfo.retries`.
  4. **Facet input.** `FacetInput::check_watertight()` (implemented here if the contract leaves it to the implementor, else the contract's provided method is used and only tested): returns located `BuildIssue{code, entity, location, gap, suggestion}` with codes `facet.open_edge`, `facet.nonmanifold_edge`, `facet.inconsistent_orientation`, `deck.mismatch`; diagnostic only, never repairs. `build` refuses a non-watertight input with `RtError::Build(issues)`. A watertight facet shell set builds one polyhedral cell per region (faces = facets grouped by `FacetFace`, sense from the facet side tags); the implicit complement is the cell of the outer unbounded region (`COMPLEMENT`).
  5. **BVH locate.** Cell AABB BVH (`leaf_size` from `BuildOptions`, default 4, median split on the longest axis, deterministic ties by id). `locate(p, dir, hint, scratch)`: hint cell first (walk to neighbour if p is inside by plane tests), then BVH candidates; containment by the face plane test for convex cells and by a first-hit ray cast along a hash-of-bits direction for non-convex cells (repeat with two more directions and take the majority when `|cos| < 1e-6` or p is within tol of a face; report `on_face`). Outside the model gives the implicit complement. `closest(p, region)` is exact to the planar faces (point-to-triangle distance over the BVH, branch-and-bound).
  6. **Provided methods are not overridden** unless the override is bit-identical to the default and measurably faster; `trace_bundle` is exercised through the default (fixed `block_size` blocks, `content_hash()` equal for 1 and N threads). `bin_map(TallySpec::{Cells, Regions, MeshSets})` correctness is tested through the default.
  7. **Cache.** `export_cache() -> CacheImage` with the contract sections; `load(image)` rebuilds the tracker without the mesh and gives the same `structure_hash`. A `CacheKey` mismatch gives `RtError::CacheStale`, a corrupt hash `RtError::CacheCorrupt`; neither panics. Helpers `pub fn write_smrt(path_or_writer, &CacheImage)` and `read_smrt(bytes) -> Result<CacheImage>`: the `.smrt` file is the `CacheImage` bytes verbatim (magic SMRTCACH, little-endian, 64-byte-aligned sections, per-section hash64), nothing else; loading copies into `Vec`s (no mmap, no unsafe). Private sections use `Private(n)`.
  8. **Diagnostics.** Every `Lost` carries the full `LostInfo` (cause, pos, dir, last cell, last face, nearest surface, retries). `TrackerInfo` reports `cells`, `faces`, `memory_bytes`, `faceted_faces = faces` for facet input and 0 for planar mesh input.
  9. `examples/trace_rays.rs` (record-only benchmark side, used by the sm-rt-capi harness): reads `facets.bin` and `rays.bin` in the formats defined in the sm-rt-capi `bench/README.md` ("smfacets v1": little-endian `u32 nv, u32 nt`, `nv*3 f64`, `nt*3 u32`, `nt*2 i32` forward/reverse region with -1 = complement; "smrays v1": `u32 n`, then `n*6 f64` origin and unit dir, `n i32` start region), builds a Facets tracker, fires every ray to leakage with `fire`-equivalent stepping, prints one JSON object `{build_s, rays, rays_per_s, crossings_per_s, lost, threads, cpu_model}`. Rays are processed in index order; output is independent of thread count.
- **Acceptance (analytic; absolute tolerances; fixtures by name from stage 0):**
  1. Hex block `reference::hex_block(10)` (10^3 unit cells, classified on one volume): for 1e6 seeded IUR lines (isotropic direction, origin uniform on a disk of radius rho normal to it, splitmix64 seeded from the case name) the sum of segment lengths equals the analytic hull chord within 1e-12 per line; 0 `Lost`; for each cell the aggregated chord over lines hitting the hull equals `V_c / (pi rho^2)` per line within 4 sigma.
  2. The same chord-sum and zero-`Lost` check, 1e6 lines each, on testkit `truncated_octahedron`, `hexagonal_prism`, `mixed_transition`, `dual_patch` (hull chords from independent analytic polytope clipping computed in the test).
  3. Non-convex: `nonconvex_poly()` (L-prism cell plus brick neighbour sharing a face): a line through the re-entrant corner region has exactly two disjoint chords in the L cell, with lengths equal to the analytic values within 1e-12; across 1e6 lines the L-cell expected chord is `V_L / (pi rho^2)` within 4 sigma; 0 `Lost`.
  4. Shared-face consistency: for 1e5 lines that cross a face shared by two cells, the exit face from cell A and the entry face seen when tracing the reversed line from cell B are the same `FaceId`; lines passing within 1e-9 of an edge or vertex (constructed so) never end `Lost` and pick the lowest `FaceId` among tied exits.
  5. Cauchy: mean chord of 1e6 IUR lines through the hull of hex_block, `truncated_octahedron`, and an icosphere facet shell (subdivision 4) equals `4V/S` of the polytope within 4 sigma/sqrt(n).
  6. Decks on hex_block(4) with `BoundarySpec`: a mirror deck reflects axis-aligned directions bit-exactly; a translation periodic deck pair wraps the position within 1e-12 of the analytic image and `entered_via` is the mate; a 90 degree rotation wrap (from a model fixture deck) keeps position within 1e-12 and direction within 1e-15 of the analytic image; White/Vacuum/Albedo return `DomainBoundary` with the state on the face; a closed reflective box traced for 1e4 histories of 1e3 steps each stays in the box and loses none.
  7. Facets: a tetrahedron, an icosphere (subdivision 3) and a nested pair of icospheres build; `check_watertight` on the same soup with one triangle removed returns exactly one `facet.open_edge` group whose `location` lies on the missing triangle and whose `gap` equals the triangle's longest edge within 1e-12; a soup with one flipped triangle returns `facet.inconsistent_orientation`; none of these panics. `locate` of 1e5 random points agrees with the exact polyhedron containment (independent winding-number test in the test) except within 1e-9 of a facet; nested shells return the right region.
  8. BVH locate: 1e5 random points in hex_block(10) and in `mixed_transition` return the brute-force cell; points at 1e-3, 1e-9 and 0 from faces, edges and vertices are classified correctly or flagged `on_face`, never `Lost`; `locate(dir=None)` on a face is deterministic (identical across repeated calls and thread counts).
  9. Lost-particle audit: 1e7 seeded histories (mixed: hex_block, nonconvex_poly, mixed_transition, icosphere shells, reflective box) with `RecoveryPolicy::Fail`: 0 `Lost`, 0 `SenseMismatch`; the `#[test]` runs 2e5 histories in debug, the full run is `#[ignore]` and the gate runs `cargo test -p sm-rt --release -- --ignored`. With `Nudge{tries: 3}` the nudge count is reported and must be 0 on this set.
  10. Cache: `build -> export_cache -> write_smrt -> read_smrt -> load` gives equal `structure_hash`, equal `TrackerInfo` (except `from_cache`), and bit-identical outcomes for a fixed 1e5-ray `trace_bundle`; flipping one byte in a section gives `CacheCorrupt`; changing one coordinate (via a different input) gives `CacheStale` for the old image; the `CacheKey` is unchanged by thread count and by mesh cell insertion order within the contract's canonicalization, and changes with any geometry or boundary edit.
