  11. Determinism (CLAUDE.md): `sm_testkit::determinism::check` on the `CacheImage` bytes and on the `trace_bundle` `content_hash()` of a fixed 1e5-ray batch, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` vs default and repeated; build output independent of thread count.
  12. Errors: every `RtError` variant used by sm-rt has a test (`Unsupported` for `Brep` and `World` inputs, `Build` for an open facet shell, `BadDirection`, `NonFinite` for NaN origin, `UnknownId`, `CacheStale`, `CacheCorrupt`); no `unwrap` on input paths; rustdoc on every public item.
  13. Allocation: `step`, `locate`, `closest` with a warmed `Scratch` perform 0 heap allocations at depth <= `LEVEL_INLINE` (counting allocator test).
  14. Throughput is RECORD-ONLY (no gate), release, 1 thread and 4 threads, CPU model recorded in `crates/sm-rt/DESIGN.md`: step rate on hex_block(100) (hex-cell crossings/s), `locate` points/s, `trace_bundle` rays/s on the icosphere, and `build` seconds and bytes per face.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance 1-13, in this order):** (S1) `RtPlugin` read-only queries `rt.info`, `rt.locate`, `rt.fire`, `rt.check` over an in-process `Model`+`MeshRead` (needs sm-op-api/sm-layer-api deps; skip if the orchestrator has not asked for it); (S2) `BuildInput::Brep` with `SurfaceRefine::None` for planar-face B-reps through the same builder; (S3) `Precision::F32Traversal` build option; (S4) exact `closest` for curved faces; (S5) overlap detection in `check`.
- **Deferred to W3b:** ANALYTIC backend (exact quadric/torus/NURBS hits, `FacetFallback`), HIERARCHY (prototype/TLAS, lattice `SlotChange`, `WorldSpec`), PERIODIC cap on non-matching faces, `.smsh` `rt/*` chunk embedding, mmap load, Cartesian lattice DDA, SIMD/packet traversal, GPU, DAGMC `h5m` export.
- **Must NOT:** edit any contract crate (`sm-rt-api`, `sm-mesh-api`, `sm-testkit`); redefine contract types; depend on sm-rt-capi, sm-tess, sm-meshio, sm-engine, or any scheme crate; use `HashMap` iteration order; use time- or thread-based seeds; use `unsafe`; use `unwrap` on input paths; claim a speedup over DAGMC.
- **Registers:** `RtPlugin` with read-only queries `rt.info`, `rt.check`, `rt.locate` (ruling R2, mandatory; `rt.fire` stays stretch). Provides `MeshTrackerBuilder`, `write_smrt`/`read_smrt`, `examples/trace_rays.rs`.

---

### W3-RTCAPI. `sm-rt-capi` (L4): C ABI and DAGMC-shaped shims over any `sm-rt-api` Tracker, plus the DAGMC benchmark harness
- **Binding rulings.** Decision 12: `sm-rt-capi` is layer L4 and the single crate allowed `unsafe` (CLAUDE.md exception; FFI entry points only, every `unsafe fn` has a `# Safety` section, `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn = deny` stays); thread-local last error in the C ABI; `RtError::code()` stable negative codes; catch_unwind at every entry; DAGMC-compat provided functions. Decision 12 dev-dep rule: this task never depends on `sm-rt` (still being built in parallel); it is tested against the contract's stub `AnalyticWorld`. Benchmark is RECORD-ONLY in W3a.
- **Deps:** sm-rt-api only (features `capi` and `stubs` on dev), std `ffi`; no `libc` crate; dev: `cc` (pinned in the stage-0 skeleton), sm-testkit if it hosts the stub (else the stub comes from `sm-rt-api --features stubs`). `crate-type = ["rlib","cdylib","staticlib"]`.
- **Idea.** Wrap `Arc<dyn Tracker>` in an opaque handle; a per-thread context owns `Scratch` and `TrackState`. Everything the transport code needs (Fortran or C++) goes through `include/smrt.h` and `fortran/smrt.f90`. The same ABI works for any backend (the W3a mesh walker, W3b analytic/hierarchy) because it only sees the `Tracker` trait.
- **Scope (mandatory):**
  1. **Handles and construction.** Opaque `SmrtTracker` (shared, read-only, `Arc<dyn Tracker>`) and `SmrtCtx` (per thread: `Scratch` + `TrackState`). Rust-side constructors `pub fn tracker_into_raw(Arc<dyn Tracker>) -> *mut SmrtTracker` (safe fn, returns raw ptr) for embedding and tests. `smrt_open(path, &out)` takes a `.smrt` image path and a loader: loaders are installed by an explicit `pub fn set_loader(...)`-free design, namely a cargo feature `sm-rt` that is OFF in W3a (the orchestrator turns it on at merge once sm-rt is on main, one manifest line); without it `smrt_open` returns `SMRT_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`. `smrt_tracker_free`, `smrt_ctx_new(tracker) / smrt_ctx_free`.
  2. **Functions** (every function returns `int32_t` status: 0 ok, 1 no hit/escaped, negative = `RtError::code()`, -100 null argument, -101 panic caught, -102 bad enum/size): `smrt_init_state(ctx, x,y,z, u,v,w, hint_cell)`; `smrt_step(ctx, max_dist, out *SmrtStep)`; `smrt_peek(ctx, limit, out *SmrtStep)` (`distance_to_boundary`); `smrt_fire(ctx, dist_limit, max_steps, out *SmrtStep)`; `smrt_resolve_boundary(ctx, face, kind, u0, u1, out_dir[3])`; `smrt_locate(ctx, p[3], dir_or_null, hint, out *SmrtLocated)`; `smrt_closest(ctx, p[3], region, out *SmrtClosest)`; `smrt_info(tracker, out *SmrtInfo)` (cells, faces, memory_bytes, faceted_faces, structure_hash lo/hi); `smrt_region_info(tracker, region, out *SmrtRegionInfo)` (original id, color id, measure); `smrt_trace_bundle(tracker, n, rays_soa, block_size, out_buf, cap, out_len)` (SoA `x,y,z,u,v,w,start_cell` arrays; output CSR, index-aligned, bit-identical for any thread count); `smrt_last_error_message(buf, cap) -> int32_t` (thread-local last error, never a global Mutex); `smrt_version()`. `#[repr(C)]` types `SmrtState`, `SmrtStep`, `SmrtLevel[8]` mirror the contract state (levels past 8 are reported via `levels_total` and truncated in the C view, never UB).
  3. **DAGMC-shaped shims** (provided functions in the contract wrapped 1:1): `smrt_dag_ray_fire(tracker, ctx, region, origin[3], dir[3], orientation, dist_limit, out_surface, out_dist)` (orientation 1 exiting, -1 entering, 0 any), `smrt_dag_point_in_volume(tracker, ctx, region, p[3], dir_or_null, out_inside)`, `smrt_dag_next_vol(tracker, surface, old_region, out_new_region)`, `smrt_dag_surface_sense(tracker, surface, region, out_sense)` (+1 forward, -1 reverse, matching DAGMC), `smrt_dag_get_angle(tracker, surface, p[3], out_normal[3])`, `smrt_dag_measure_volume/area`. Implicit complement is region id `SMRT_COMPLEMENT`. Documented differences from DAGMC (no history vector; `entered_via` state replaces `RayHistory`; deterministic hashed direction when `dir` is null).
  4. **Header and Fortran.** Checked-in `include/smrt.h` (C, only `int32_t`, `int64_t`, `uint64_t`, `double`, opaque pointers, `extern "C"` guards) and `fortran/smrt.f90` (a module with `bind(C)` interfaces using `iso_c_binding`, matching types and the status constants). A test parses both files and compares the `smrt_*` names against the crate's exported `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]` list (edition 2024 spelling), and compares struct sizes/offsets with `size_of`/`offset_of` through a generated C `static_assert` file compiled by `cc`.
  5. **Robustness.** Every entry point: null checks, `catch_unwind` (returns -101 and sets the thread-local message), no panic across the boundary, no allocation on `smrt_step/peek/fire` after `smrt_ctx_new` for depth <= 8. Thread safety: a tracker is `Send + Sync`; a ctx is used by one thread at a time (documented; not enforced by a lock).
  6. **Benchmark harness (record-only), in `crates/sm-rt-capi/bench/`:** `README.md` (formats, Docker commands, how to read results), `gen_case.py` (stdlib only: writes `facets.bin` and `rays.bin` for cases C1 icosphere shell (subdivision 5) in a box, C2 five nested icospheres, C3 box-with-spherical-void; rays by splitmix64 seeded from the case name, uniform interior starts, isotropic dirs; formats "smfacets v1"/"smrays v1" defined in the README and parsed identically by `sm-rt/examples/trace_rays.rs`), `dagmc/Dockerfile` (conda-forge `dagmc` + `moab` via micromamba; the base image is multi-arch and installability on linux-aarch64 (arm64) was verified before this task), `dagmc/facets_to_h5m.py` (pymoab: facets -> triangle surfaces with sense tags + volume sets + `make_watertight` check), `dagmc/dagmc_driver.cpp` (loads the h5m, `DagMC::init_OBBTree`, then the standard `ray_fire` + `next_vol` loop to leakage using the same rays, counts lost particles as: ray_fire error, no-hit inside a bounded volume, or `point_in_volume` disagreement on a 1-in-1000 audit; prints JSON `{rays_per_s, crossings_per_s, lost, build_s, threads, cpu_model}`), `run_bench.sh` (builds the image, runs DAGMC (1 thread and N), runs `cargo run --release -p sm-rt --example trace_rays` when sm-rt is available, otherwise skips that side and says so, merges both JSONs into `bench/results/<case>.json` with a unique name per run). A Rust `examples/rt_capi_bench.rs` runs the same rays through the C ABI over the stub `AnalyticWorld` sphere (analytic, so it is a sanity and overhead number, not a competitor number). Method: 3 warm-up and 7 measured repetitions, median with min/max, CPU model recorded.
  7. `crates/sm-rt-capi/DESIGN.md`: ABI table, status codes, ownership/threading rules, DAGMC difference list, the benchmark protocol and the recorded numbers.
- **Acceptance (against the stub `AnalyticWorld` unless noted):**
  1. C test program in `tests/c/` built with `cc` and linked to the cdylib: loads a stub tracker through the Rust constructor test hook (exported behind the `stubs` feature), traces a sphere chord at impact parameters b in {0, 3, 4.9} for R = 5 and gets `2 sqrt(R^2 - b^2)` within 1e-12; nested shells' chords sum to the outer chord within 1e-12; a reflective box reflects bit-exactly; `smrt_resolve_boundary` with White returns a unit vector for 1e5 seeded `u` pairs.
  2. Same C test against the stub's 2-level lattice: the chord sum equals the analytic value within 1e-12 and a `SlotChange` is reported through `smrt_step` and skipped by `smrt_fire`.
  3. DAGMC shims on the stub sphere: `smrt_dag_ray_fire` exiting, entering and any give the analytic distances; `smrt_dag_point_in_volume` is correct for 1e5 random points; `smrt_dag_surface_sense` returns +1/-1 consistent with `smrt_dag_next_vol`; the complement id is returned outside.
  4. Status and errors: null pointers give -100 for every function (table-driven test); a stub tracker configured to panic inside `step` gives -101, the process survives, and `smrt_last_error_message` on that thread returns the message while another thread's message is unaffected; every `RtError` variant maps to a distinct stable negative code and the codes equal `RtError::code()`.
  5. Header and Fortran: `smrt.h` and `smrt.f90` match the exported symbols and struct layouts (test in Scope 4); if `gfortran` is on PATH, compile `fortran/smrt.f90` and a 20-line Fortran program calling `smrt_version`; otherwise that sub-test is skipped and says so.
  6. Bundles: `smrt_trace_bundle` on 1e5 seeded rays gives the same output bytes for `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` and default, and equals the Rust `trace_bundle` `content_hash()`; `sm_testkit::determinism::check` on the output bytes.
  7. No allocation in `smrt_step` after ctx creation (counting allocator test); `smrt_step` overhead over the direct Rust `step` call on the stub is recorded (record-only).
  8. Benchmark harness: `gen_case.py` is deterministic (SHA256 of the output files recorded in `bench/README.md` and asserted by `run_bench.sh --check-gen`); `docker build` of `dagmc/Dockerfile` succeeds on this arm64 machine and `dagmc_driver` runs case C1 end to end and writes JSON with `rays_per_s` and `lost` (the DAGMC numbers for C1-C3 are committed under `bench/results/` with CPU model and date); the Rust side runs on the stub and records its overhead. If Docker is unavailable in the worker's environment, report `status: partial` with the exact failing command; do not fabricate numbers.
  9. Lints and docs: clippy `-D warnings`, rustdoc on every public item including every `extern "C"` fn with a `# Safety` section; every `unsafe` block is inside an FFI entry point or a thin helper with a comment naming the invariant; a grep test asserts no `unsafe` outside `src/ffi/`.
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance):** (S1) wire `smrt_open` to sm-rt through the `sm-rt` feature and run `run_bench.sh` end to end against the real tracker (needs sm-rt on main); (S2) C++ header-only RAII wrapper `smrt.hpp`; (S3) a double-down (Embree) rival image and numbers; (S4) OpenMC CSG rival on a CSG-expressible case; (S5) a `smrt_trace_segments` callback API for MOC codes.
- **Deferred to W3b:** the "beats DAGMC" claim and its numbers on G1/G2/G4-style hierarchy geometries (needs the ANALYTIC/HIERARCHY backends), DAGMC `h5m` export from smart-mesh, Python bindings.
- **Must NOT:** depend on sm-rt or any L3 crate (dev or normal) in W3a; edit `sm-rt-api` (return `blocked_on_api` with an `api_change_request`); use `unsafe` outside the FFI layer; let a panic cross the C boundary; keep error state in a global; use time- or thread-based seeds; commit benchmark numbers that were not measured; claim a speedup over DAGMC.
- **Registers:** C ABI `libsmrt` (cdylib/staticlib) with `include/smrt.h` and `fortran/smrt.f90`; bench harness under `crates/sm-rt-capi/bench/`.

#### orchestrator wiring (not worker work)
After both merge: enable `sm-rt-capi`'s `sm-rt` feature (one manifest line) so `smrt_open` works and run `bench/run_bench.sh`; keep the benchmark record-only.

## 3. Stage 0 (on main before the workers start)

### 3.1 What stage 0 put on main

Every item was implemented by a worker, reviewed by an Opus reviewer and fixed until it passed, then merged. All changes to existing contract crates are additive: no golden or state hash changed.

| Item | Where | Notes for workers |
|---|---|---|
| Skeletons for the 13 new crates | `crates/sm-boolean`, `sm-cadio`, `sm-smooth`, `sm-poly`, `sm-diag`, `sm-scheme-{submap,pave,tri,sweep,tet}`, `sm-exodus`, `sm-rt`, `sm-rt-capi` | Plugin crates expose `pub struct XPlugin;` implementing `sm_op_api::Plugin`; scheme crates expose `pub fn register(&mut SchemeRegistry) -> sm_scheme_api::Result<()>`. Keep those signatures. |
| `TopologyEdit::join_edges(first, second)` and `join_faces(keep, drop)` | `sm-topo-api` (shared planner `src/join.rs`), implemented for `CellComplex` and `TopoStore` | Inverses of `split_edge`/`split_face`. The lower edge id survives `join_edges`. `join_faces` handles one connected chain of shared edges, a whole hole, and the two-edge case (a chord plus the seam of a periodic face: the chord dissolves, the seam stays). With 3 or more shared edges that are not one chain it returns `Unsupported`. On coincidence records, keep wins. Tolerance overrides on dissolved vertices are dropped. |
| Topology fixtures `torus`, `cone`, `brick_with_void`, `nested_spheres` | `sm_topo_api::fixtures`, mirrored in `sm_testkit::topo` | Analytic volume and area in the rustdoc. |
| Geometry fixtures | `sm_testkit::topo` (`planar_face`, `annulus_face`, `sphere_zone`, sweeps `extruded_l`, `twisted_prism`, `annular_sector`, `hollow_cylinder`, `regular_prism`, assemblies `pin_assembly`, `instanced_core`), `sm_testkit::boolean::cases()` (7 cases with expected volumes, areas, V/E/F and Euler numbers), `sm_testkit::w3a` (milestone constants + `STEP` bytes with pinned sha256), `sm_testkit::step` (the 7 OCC-written STEP files with volumes), `mixed_all_shapes` | Every constant was recomputed by the reviewer. |
| Mesh fixtures | `sm_testkit::surf3` (closed triangle surfaces, incl. negative cases), `sm_testkit::lattice` (`hex`, `quad`, `tube_quad`, `tet_kuhn`, `perturb`, `LatticeFixture::perturbed(seed, amplitude_over_h)`, `LatticeError`), `meshes::tet_block` family (returns `Result<_, MeshError>`, classified onto a CAD box), `meshes::nonconvex_poly`, `loops2d::staircase` | Safe jitter amplitudes are in the `perturb` rustdoc. Kuhn tets stay valid up to 0.25h. |
| `MeshLayer::replace_mesh`, `add_mesh_set`, `ModelLayer` constructor | `sm-layer-api` | Atomic on every error path. |
| `GenCtx::loop_uvs(face, loop)`, `GenerateFailure::with_nodes(nodes)` | `sm-scheme-api` | `loop_uvs` is seam aware (the seam reads u=2π on one side, 0 on the other) and pole aware (a pole takes the outgoing coedge's u). |
| `ExportFormat::{Exodus, Openfoam}`, `ExportedFile`, `ExportResult::files` | `sm-protocol` (catalog re-blessed) | sm-server returns `unsupported-format` for both until integration wires the writers. |
| CR-2 completed | `sm-model-api` validate, symmetry, detect | All three use the robust face plane; no hash moved. |
| New frozen contract `sm-rt-api` | `crates/sm-rt-api` | The mesh-native panel winner plus grafts. Read `DESIGN.md` ("Deviations and design-arounds"). Growable types are `#[non_exhaustive]` with `new` constructors. Stub `AnalyticWorld` behind feature `stubs`. `RtError::code()` gives stable negative codes. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`. |
| STEP data | `crates/sm-testkit/data/step/*.step`, `tools/conformance/make_step.py` | Written by OpenCASCADE: milestone part, cylinder, sphere, torus, cone, filleted box, B-spline loft. |


### 3.2 Stage-0b rulings (binding for W3a workers; they answer the open questions left in the task entries)

When a task entry and a ruling below disagree, the ruling wins. When a task entry and the code on main disagree on a name, the code on main wins: adapt and say so in your report.

**General**
- G1. All stage-0 items named in the task entries are on main: `TopologyEdit::join_edges`/`join_faces` (both hosts), the sm-testkit modules (`boolean`, `w3a`, `step`, `surf3`, `lattice`, the sweep/pin/planar fixtures, the `tet_block` family, `nonconvex_poly`, `mixed_all_shapes`), `MeshLayer::replace_mesh`, `add_mesh_set`, the `ModelLayer` constructor, `GenCtx::loop_uvs`, `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`, `ExportFormat::{Exodus, Openfoam}`, `ExportedFile`/`ExportResult::files`, and the new frozen crate `sm-rt-api`. Read their rustdoc before you start. Do not build local copies of them.
- G2. External crates: declare them in your own `crates/<crate>/Cargo.toml` with an explicit version (there are no workspace-level dependencies). Beyond the W2 allow-list (thiserror, smallvec, indexmap, rayon, serde, serde_json, schemars; proptest/criterion as dev-deps), justify each new one in your report. Pure-Rust only, no system libraries. Pre-approved per task: sm-cadio `sha2`, `base64`; sm-rt `crc32fast`; sm-rt-capi `cc` (dev/build only); sm-exodus none (own netCDF-3 encoder).
- G3. Stub schemes: register individual `sm_scheme_api` stub structs (feature `stubs`) through the registry's normal `register` call. Look at `crates/sm-scheme-api/tests/` for the exact call.
- G4. Quality floors, perf gates and score values marked "estimate" or "provisional" are calibrated by the worker on the first run. If a floor cannot be met, report the measured value and the reason; do not quietly relax it. Perf targets that depend on the W3b bulk commit are recorded only, not gated.

**Booleans (sm-boolean)**
- B1. Roles: `positive`/`negative` hold the bodies on each side, `volumes` holds every resulting volume, and `bodies` holds the unsided remainder (empty for plane, surface and cylinder cuts). A role never repeats an id (commit_topology rule).
- B2. After webcut and subtract, every resulting volume is its own body (Cubit webcut behaviour). `unite` of bodies that do not touch returns `Invalid` in W3a; compound bodies come in W3b.
- B3. Regularize uses `join_edges`/`join_faces`. The periodic case (a face split by one chord plus its seam) is handled by `join_faces` on main; check its rustdoc for the exact behaviour.

**STEP (sm-cadio)**
- C1. The filleted block has 7 faces: 6 planar plus 1 rational NURBS fillet. Its volume is 997.853981634.
- C2. `loft_bspline` is mandatory, with volume 54.454271953 (OCC mass properties) to relative 1e-6.
- C3. `sphere_r3` (VERTEX_LOOP poles) and `cone_r2_h3` (apex) are the first stretch item. Real CAD files have poles, so do them right after mandatory acceptance.
- C4. `to_unit` comes from the op argument (default millimetres, no conversion); project.toml wiring is W3b. The inline STEP limit is 8 MiB.

**Schemes**
- S1. Score for a submap corner sum other than 4: 20. Score for triadvance: 20, provisional (only W3b auto selection reads it).
- S2. tri: keep poles (full sphere) mandatory. The 20° bound is a target: when a fixture's boundary makes it impossible, report the minimum angle you reach and why. Do not escalate.
- S3. sweep copies use tolerance `1e-9 × bbox diagonal` in W3a. Solid-cylinder revolve with a pole column is W3b.
- S4. tet: Steiner nodes carry `Param::None` and are owned by the partition cell. If the Driver commit refuses them, return `blocked_on_api` with the exact error, and keep the rest working through a local test harness. Keep interior Steiner points at least 0.3h from boundary vertices and from boundary triangles.

**Mesh post-processing**
- M1. sm-smooth keeps its own private `CarrierNormals` in W3a; a shared FaceNormals is W3b. `mesh.smooth` targets a whole mesh or an explicit cell list; targeting by set is W3b. Deck-pair nodes are pinned in W3a.
- M2. sm-poly: `CellShape` follows the cell's real topology, so a dual cell that is exactly a hex stays `Hex` (exact cells, never re-tagged). `POLYHEDRON` is the block-level element type at the fem/export level and means "any 3D cell". `tet_block` comes with a CAD box topology (classified like `hex_block`), so `TopologyResolver` works on it.
- M3. sm-poly `keep_source: true` leaves two meshes on one volume. That is allowed: the poly mesh entry is marked as derived from its source, and scripts and exports name the mesh explicitly. How `mesh.run` treats a volume that already has a derived mesh is an integration wiring item (sm-mesher), not sm-poly's. Exact tiling is the only boundary behaviour in W3a.

**Exporters (sm-meshio, sm-exodus)**
- E1. OpenFOAM export writes a case directory (`constant/polyMesh/*`, ASCII only). The Rust API returns `PolyMesh::files` plus a `FoamReport`, and the protocol lists the written files in `ExportResult::files` with sha256. No tar stream.
- E2. Loss and quirk reports (`FoamReport`, `LossReport`) are returned from the Rust API and logged in W3a. Surfacing them in the protocol is W3b.
- E3. An OpenFOAM boundary face that no named set covers goes to a patch named `defaultFaces` of type `patch`. Face sets that cannot be patches do not become faceZones automatically.
- E4. Exodus: element blocks and side sets in W3a; node sets wait for sm-fem node-set lowering (W3b). Polyhedra are written as `NFACED` behind `ExodusOptions::polyhedra`, marked experimental until a SEACAS reader confirms them.
- E6. `.smsh` 1.1 is written always (`VERSION_MINOR = 1`), so the bytes of every `.smsh` file change. Golden files and hashes of `.smsh` bytes in other crates (sm-fem, sm-cli, sm-server tests) will move: do not edit them; list every one you know of in W3_REPORT.md, and the orchestrator re-blesses them at merge. State hashes must not change.
- E5. `.smsh` stores `POLYHEDRON`/`POLYGON`, never `NFACED`/`NSIDED` (decision 12).

**Diagnostics (sm-quality, sm-diag)**
- D0. The sm-quality delta is strictly additive: new items only, no renamed or re-signed public items, no changed metric values. sm-smooth depends on sm-quality as it is on main.
- D1. Op availability for suggestions comes from an `OpNames` service (a sorted list of op names) that sm-server injects during integration. Tests inject their own. No contract change.
- D2. Thresholds: start the `transport` and `generic` profiles from the values in the task entry, and treat them as provisional. They are data in one table, so they are easy to retune.

**Ray tracing (sm-rt, sm-rt-capi)**
- R1. Names: the frozen `sm-rt-api` on main wins over the synthesis names in the task entries.
- R2. sm-rt: the minimal `RtPlugin` is MANDATORY in W3a (promoted from stretch S1), because ray tracing must be reachable from MCP. It provides read-only queries `rt.info`, `rt.check` (watertightness and build issues) and `rt.locate`, built from a `meshes` entry plus the model, with sm-op-api/sm-layer-api deps. `rt.fire` stays stretch. sm-server installs it during integration.
- R3. sm-rt-capi never depends on sm-rt in W3a. `smrt_open` returns a "no backend" error code until integration adds an off-by-default `sm-rt` feature (stretch S1, done by the orchestrator after both crates are merged). Everything else is tested against the stub `AnalyticWorld` (`sm-rt-api` feature `stubs`). The repr(C) types live in sm-rt-capi, not in the contract.
- R4. DAGMC numbers are measured and recorded only: no speedup claim in W3a. The benchmark harness uses `condaforge/miniforge3` plus `mamba install -c conda-forge dagmc moab` (verified on aarch64). If the image does not build, fail loudly.

**Integration**
- I1. STEP staging: front ends add an explicit staging command (`session.stage`, MCP `session_stage`, `smesh stage <path>`) that stores the bytes and returns `{sha256}`. `model.import_step` takes only `{sha256}` or `{inline}`. sm-lang does no I/O.
- I2. `tools/conformance.sh` (Docker OpenFOAM `checkMesh`, Gmsh comparison, DAGMC) is approved. It stays outside `tools/ci.sh`.
- I3. The V2|V3 interface: V2 is a prism sweep, so its triangle faces are not trimeshed by the user script; the planner meshes them as sweep source and target.


## 4. Integration and milestone

### W3-INT. Integration, wiring and milestone (integration agent after the merges; D1-D6 are owner micro tasks)
- **Deps:** stage 0 on main (decision 12 list, named in "Stage-0 items relied on" below). D2, D4, D5, D6 and D1(a,b) need only stage 0 plus testkit fixtures and can run in parallel with the crate workers. D1(c) (calls into sm-exodus and `sm_meshio::foam`) and each plugin line of D3 land when that crate merges (decision 12: a worker never depends on a W3a crate still being built). The milestone phase needs every mandatory W3a crate merged: sm-boolean (webcut_plane, imprint, merge, unite with regularize), sm-cadio (`model.import_step`), sm-scheme-{pave,tri,sweep,tet}, sm-smooth, sm-poly, sm-exodus, sm-meshio (OpenFOAM, `.smsh` 1.1), sm-quality FV delta + sm-diag, sm-rt (VOLUME_MESH backend over `sm-rt-api`).
- **Names this entry is written against (the drafts' real names; if the merged catalog differs, only script templates and wiring change):**
  - Ops (all take `deny_unknown_fields` params):
    - `model.webcut_plane {targets: [Body|Volume], plane: {origin:[f64;3], normal:[f64;3]} | {axis, offset} | {vertices}, keep?: "both"|"positive"|"negative", merge?: bool=true, imprint?: bool=false}`. Roles: `bodies`, `volumes`, `cut_faces`, plus (decision 12) `positive` and `negative` = the piece bodies on the +normal / -normal side.
    - `model.unite {bodies: [Body], keep?, regularize?: bool=true, tol?}` (roles `bodies`, `volumes`, `new_faces`, `faces`).
    - `model.imprint {bodies?: [Body], faces?: [Face], curves?: [Edge], merge?: bool=false}` (roles `faces`, `edges`).
    - `model.merge {entities?: [topology cells], kinds?, tol?, check_only?}` (default scope: all bodies; interface faces from the `Model` boundary tables are skipped by the op).
    - `model.delete {bodies}`.
    - `model.import_step {source: {sha256} | {inline: base64}, name?, to_unit?, assume_unit?, weld_tol?, solids?, sheets?, check?}`, never a path. Roles `bodies`, `volumes`, `faces`, `edges`, `vertices`, in STEP-id order. Bytes come from the injected `StepInputs` service.
    - `mesh.scheme {targets, scheme, params?}` with schemes `submap`, `pave` (surface), `trimesh` (default), `tridelaunay`, `triadvance` (surface, TRI), `sweep` (volume; params `source:[Face]`, `target:[Face]` both or neither), `tetmesh` (volume, needs TRI on every piece face). There is no `tri` alias (that is the stub's name).
    - `mesh.smooth {mesh, entities?, method: {kind:"laplacian"|"optimize"|..., ...}, iterations?, tolerance?, schedule?, fix_boundary?: bool, guard_floor?}`.
    - `mesh.to_poly {mesh, keep_source?: bool = true}` (decision 12: converts a whole all-tet mesh entry into a new Mesh entry, role `mesh`; the draft's `targets` and `keep_source=false` default are superseded). Also query `mesh.poly_info {mesh}`.
    - `mesh.flag_cells {mesh?, name, profile?, level?, grow?}`.
  - Queries:
    - `mesh.validate {mesh?, geometry?: bool=true, planarity?, min_volume?, limit?}` -> `{valid, geometry_checked, total, by_code, by_owner, violations[{code, severity, entity, detail, owner, near, location}], truncated}`. A violation has no suggestion field.
    - `mesh.quality {mesh?, profile?: generic|openfoam|fluent|transport, metrics?, thresholds?, worst_n?, bins?}` -> `{verdict, validity, metrics[{metric, summary, n_warn, n_fail, histogram, worst[], clusters[]}], skipped, suggestions[{..., op, available}]}`.
    - `topo.related`, `model.measure {entity}` -> `{area, volume, centroid, bbox{lo,hi}}` (existing).
  - Library names:
    - sm-exodus: `exodus_bytes(&dyn MeshRead, &ExodusOptions) -> Result<Vec<u8>, ExodusError>`, `ExodusOptions{blocks: BlockSelection, faces_as_sidesets, provenance, title, ..}`, test decoder `sm_exodus::read::read_exodus(&[u8]) -> ExodusData`.
    - sm-meshio: `foam::build_polymesh(&dyn MeshRead, &FoamOptions) -> FoamBuild{mesh: PolyMesh, report: FoamReport{shape_counts, patches, loss, ..}}`, `PolyMesh::files() -> Vec<FoamFile{name, bytes}>`, `foam::parse_polymesh`, `foam::check_polymesh(&PolyMesh, tol) -> Vec<FoamIssue>`, `SmshWriter::element_type(set, name)`, `SmshFile::decode`.
    - sm-rt (through `sm-rt-api`): `TrackerBuilder` for the VOLUME_MESH backend, `Tracker::{trace_bundle, locate}`, `TrackBundle::content_hash()`, `CacheImage` / `.smrt` sidecar. Exact names are those of the merged `sm-rt-api`; the row-13 test adapts to them.
  - Element types: internal and `.smsh` 1.1 use `POLYHEDRON` / `POLYGON`. Exporters map them (Exodus `NFACED` / `NSIDED`, VTK `VTK_POLYHEDRON`, OpenFOAM faces).
- **Stage-0 items relied on (decision 12; referenced by name, none is an ACR of this task):**
  - `TopologyEdit::join_edges` / `join_faces` (needed by `model.unite` regularize).
  - Topology fixtures torus, cone, brick_with_void, nested_spheres.
  - `MeshLayer::replace_mesh` + `add_mesh_set` (needed by `mesh.flag_cells`).
  - `GenCtx::loop_uvs`, `GenerateFailure::with_nodes`.
  - `ExportFormat::{Exodus, Openfoam}`, `ExportedFile {name, bytes: u64, sha256}` and `ExportResult.files` in sm-protocol, with `catalog.json` already re-blessed. No `tracking` field exists anywhere in the protocol or in `SessionCommand`.
  - `ModelLayer` constructible from `(TopoHost, Model)`.
  - sm-testkit fixtures: `w3a` part constants and STEP (`crates/sm-testkit/data/step/w3a_part.step`, OCC-written, three solids, sha256 pinned by a testkit test), `tet_block` family, `lattice`, `nonconvex_poly`, boolean cases.
  - The `sm-rt-api` contract crate and the skeletons of the new crates.
  - The CLAUDE.md `unsafe` exception for sm-rt-capi (not used here).
  - If any of these is missing when the worker starts, the worker stops with `blocked_on_api`; it never works around one.
#### 0. Orchestrator rulings at integration (2026-10-30; they win over the text below)
- INT-1. Row 11: the poly cell count is `pairs + wedges - split_cells` (the reflex-edge repair in sm-poly 6ec5ca6 splits non-star-shaped dual cells).
- INT-2. Ruling R2 wins over D3's "No sm-rt plugin is installed": D3 installs `sm_rt::RtPlugin` (read-only queries `rt.info`, `rt.check`, `rt.locate`) in `distribution_with_core` and `reference_standard()`. There is still no tracking op, no protocol field and no `.smsh` section; row 13 still builds the tracker in process.
- INT-3. Ruling D1 wiring: D3 injects `sm_diag::OpNames` (the sorted names of every registered op) into the workspace services, built from the final registry, so `mesh.quality` suggestions report `available` correctly.
- INT-4. Ruling M3 wiring (sm-mesher owner micro task D7): `mesh.run` on a selection where any target volume already has more than one mesh entry (e.g. a source plus its derived poly mesh) fails atomically with `OpError::Invalid` naming the volume and the mesh ids, and suggesting `mesh.delete`. A volume with exactly one entry keeps the W2 behaviour bit-identically (W2 hashes unchanged). No contract change (the `MeshEntry.derived_from` ACR stays in the post-W3a batch).
- INT-5. Ruling I3 holds: the V2|V3 triangle is never trimeshed by the script; it comes from V2's sweep copy.
- INT-6. The sm-scheme-tet S4/performance follow-up runs in parallel and only touches sm-scheme-tet; the milestone uses whatever is on main when it runs.

#### 1. Who registers what (W3a)
| Op / query / format | Registered by | Writes | Reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| `model.unite/subtract/intersect`, `imprint`, `merge`, `webcut_plane/_surface/_cylinder` (+ stretch `_body/_sheet`), query `model.merge_check` | sm-boolean `BooleanPlugin` (id "boolean"; sm-model keeps `create_*`, `delete`, transforms) | `model` | `meshes` (refuses a meshed volume: 'mesh.delete first') |
| `model.import_step` | sm-cadio `CadioPlugin` | `model` | service `StepInputs` |
| new schemes | each crate's `register`, called from `sm_server::standard_schemes()`: sm-scheme-submap (`submap`), -pave (`pave`), -tri (`trimesh`, `tridelaunay`, `triadvance`), -sweep (`sweep`), -tet (`tetmesh`). Auto lists stay `curve [equal], surface [map], volume [map]` (W2 hashes unchanged); the provisional surface score table (map 100, submap 80, pave 40, trimesh 30) is metadata only; no fallbacks are set in W3a. Stub names are never registered by `standard_schemes()`, so there is no duplicate-name clash | | |
| `mesh.run` / `mesh.scheme` / `mesh.size` | unchanged (sm-mesher) | `intent`, `meshes` | |
| `mesh.smooth` | sm-smooth `SmoothPlugin` | `meshes` (new geometry, same topology) | `model` |
| `mesh.to_poly`, query `mesh.poly_info` | sm-poly `PolyPlugin` | `meshes` (adds a Mesh; keeps the source by default) | `meshes`, `model` |
| queries `mesh.validate`, `mesh.quality`; op `mesh.flag_cells` | sm-diag `DiagPlugin` (id "diag"; sm-quality is a plain library at L2 and has no plugin) | `meshes` (flag_cells only) | `meshes`, `model` |
| `fem.*` ops | sm-fem `FemPlugin` (adds element types `POLYHEDRON`, `POLYGON`) | `model`, `fem` | |
| export Exodus / OpenFOAM / `.smsh` | not ops: `session.export` -> `sm_fem::export` (one stream) or `sm_fem::export_files` (directory) | none | `model`, `meshes`, `fem` |
| tracking | not registered, no op, no protocol field, no `.smsh` section: the test builds the sm-rt tracker in process; the cache is a `.smrt` sidecar owned by sm-rt | none | `model`, `meshes` |
#### 2. Wiring deltas (owner micro tasks; each keeps all existing tests green)
- **D1 sm-fem:**
  - (a) `ExportFormat` gains `Exodus`, `OpenFoam` (+ `name()`, `is_multi_file()`). `ElementType` gains `Polyhedron` ('POLYHEDRON', shape Polyhedron) and `Polygon` ('POLYGON'), at the next free codes, additive.
  - (b) `pub struct ExportFile {pub name: String, pub bytes: Vec<u8>}`, `pub struct ExportBundle {pub mesh: EntityId, pub files: Vec<ExportFile>, pub sets: Vec<SetSummary>, pub notes: Vec<String>}`, `pub fn export_files(state: &State, req: &ExportRequest) -> Result<ExportBundle, FemError>` (files sorted by name). `ExportReport` gains `notes: Vec<String>` (one line per loss item, `code: detail xcount`). `export(..)` keeps one stream and returns `FemError::MultiFile` (code `multi-file-format`) for OpenFoam. `ExportRequest` gains nothing (no tracking).
  - (c, after sm-exodus / sm-meshio merge) Exodus: `sm_exodus::exodus_bytes(lowered_mesh, &ExodusOptions::default().provenance(..))`. Lowered volume colors are labelled cell sets, so `BlockSelection::Labelled` writes them as blocks, `Sides` sets become sidesets, deck `Faces` sets become sidesets (`faces_as_sidesets`). Element types come from the cell shapes. OpenFOAM: `foam::build_polymesh(lowered_mesh, &FoamOptions{decks: lowered.decks, ..default})`, then `PolyMesh::files()`, names prefixed `constant/polyMesh/` (`points`, `faces`, `owner`, `neighbour`, `boundary`, and `cellZones` / `faceZones` only when non-empty). `.smsh`: `SmshWriter::element_type(set, name)` for every declared block type (`Lowered.block_types: BTreeMap<String, ElementType>`).
  - A declared type that mismatches the cell shape stays `ElementMismatch`. A block with no declared type may mix shapes; the Exodus writer splits it (`<set>_<ELEMTYPE>`), which is sm-exodus business.
  - Tests, using `sm_mesh_api::reference::{mixed_transition, dual_patch}` (no new fixture):
    - bundle bytes identical over runs and 1 vs N threads;
    - `read_exodus` of `mixed_transition` gives HEX8, WEDGE6, PYRAMID5, TET4 blocks of one element each;
    - `dual_patch` gives NFACED blocks;
    - `foam::parse_polymesh(files)` equals the built `PolyMesh`;
    - `POLYHEDRON` declared on a hex block gives `ElementMismatch`;
    - `export` with OpenFoam gives `multi-file-format`;
    - the `.smsh` `element_types` header lists the declared names.
- **D2 sm-lang:**
  - `session.export` accepts `format` in {'smsh','vtu','exodus','openfoam'} (did-you-mean lists four).
  - New value command `session.stage(path: string)` -> `{sha256, bytes}` (`SessionCommand::Stage {path}`, statement-or-expression like `hash()`; the host resolves the path and stages the bytes, the language does no I/O).
  - complete.rs table and LANGUAGE.md updated; golden diagnostics for each new error.
  - This replaces the stage-0 proposal of a `tracking` flag (dropped).
- **D3 sm-server:**
  - `standard_schemes()` registers the five new scheme crates (strict, errors never swallowed).
  - `distribution_with_core` adds `BooleanPlugin`, `CadioPlugin`, `SmoothPlugin`, `PolyPlugin`, `DiagPlugin` and keeps the `mesher` service. `reference_standard()` (CellComplex core) installs the same plugins.
  - Format mapping: protocol `Exodus` / `Openfoam`; extension inference `.exo .e .g .exodus` -> exodus; openfoam needs an explicit format and `path` is the CASE directory (files go to `<path>/constant/polyMesh/`).
  - Directory export via `export_files`, each file written with a unique temp name (pid + counter, which closes the `.part` backlog item) then renamed.
  - `ExportResult.files: Vec<ExportedFile>` is filled, and `sha256` is the SHA-256 of the sorted manifest lines `name\tsha256hex\n`. `inline: true` with a multi-file format is `bad-request`.
  - `tests/registry.rs` (OUTPUTS and x-entity-kind on every op) passes on `try_standard()`.
  - No sm-rt plugin is installed (tracking is in process in W3a).
- **D4 sm-mcp:**
  - `session_export` schema: format enum of four, description 'openfoam: path is the case directory'.
  - New tool `session_stage {path}` -> `{sha256, bytes}` (calls `Server::stage_input`, D6).
  - Result text leads with a `summary` string when a query result has one (so `mesh.validate` / `mesh.quality` read well).
  - Every new tool name is <= 64 bytes. The golden tool list is regenerated.
- **D5 sm-cli:**
  - `--format exodus|openfoam` on export.
  - `smesh stage FILE` prints `{sha256, bytes}`.
  - `smesh validate [--mesh ID]`: exit 1 when `valid` is false; prints `code`, `severity`, `entity`, `owner`, `near` and `location` per violation (there is no suggestion on a violation).
  - `smesh quality [--mesh ID] [--profile generic|openfoam|fluent|transport] [--worst N]`: prints the verdict, per metric min / max / mean / n_warn / n_fail, the worst entries, and the suggestions with their `available` flag.
  - `--json` on both, thin over `Request::Query`. Help text. Tests with the fake server.
- **D6 sm-server (content-addressed STEP wiring):**
  - `sm_server::standard()` injects `StepInputs(Arc<DirInputStore>)` rooted at `<project>/inputs/`. `.memory()` and `reference_standard()` inject `MemoryInputStore`. `Workspace::state_at`, cold replay and checkout use the same service, so replay on another machine needs only the project directory.
  - `Server::stage_input(&self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<InputRef{sha256, bytes}, ServerError>` (in-process API; uses cadio's `stage_file` / `put`). `SessionHost` handles `SessionCommand::Stage {path}` by reading the file and calling it. The front ends (D2 `session.stage`, D4 `session_stage`, D5 `smesh stage`) are thin over it. The recorded op always carries `{sha256}`, never a path.
  - Tests: a missing blob gives a typed error with the op state hash unchanged; inline and sha256 forms give the same state hash; the project directory alone replays a script that imported a STEP file (copy the project to a new temp dir, `smesh hash -p`).
  - There is no `ApplyCtx::blob` change.
#### 3. tools/conformance.sh (NOT in tools/ci.sh)
- Gate: without `SM_CONFORMANCE=1` print 'conformance skipped' and exit 0. With it, a missing prerequisite exits 3, unless `SM_CONFORMANCE_ALLOW_SKIP=1`. `--only openfoam|meshio|exodus` (stretch: `gmsh|polydual`), `--keep`. Work dir `target/conformance/<utc>-<pid>/` (unique logs per run), summary in `report.json`; exit 0 only when every mandatory check passes.
- Prerequisites: docker with `opencfd/openfoam-default:2506` (add `--platform linux/amd64` on arm64; commands run as `docker run --rm --user $(id -u):$(id -g) -v $CASE:/case -w /case IMG bash -lc '. $(ls -d /usr/lib/openfoam/openfoam*/etc/bashrc | head -1) && checkMesh -allGeometry -allTopology'`); a venv at `target/conformance/venv` from `tools/conformance/requirements.txt` with pinned `meshio`, `netCDF4`, `numpy`, `vtk` (pin exact versions on first green run; `gmsh==4.15.2` only for the stretch check).
- Order per fixture: (0) build `smesh --release`, run `tools/conformance/fixtures/<name>.smesh --memory`, then `smesh validate` must pass. If our validator fails, report 'OUR BUG' and skip the external tools. (1) The external tool judges the exporter only. Our cells are never changed to satisfy an external tool.
- Mandatory fixtures: `hexbox` (10^3 map), `wedgeprism` (sweep of a tri source), `tetbox` (10x10x6, tetmesh, size 1), `polydual` (tetbox + `mesh.to_poly`), `w3a_hybrid` and `w3a_tet` (the two milestone variants of path B; the milestone test emits their scripts, with resolved ids, to `$CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR/milestone_w3a/` with `MILESTONE_W3A_EMIT=<dir>`, and the script copies them).
- OpenFOAM (`openfoam`): checkMesh must print `Mesh OK.`; its cell census (hexahedra, prisms, tetrahedra, polyhedra) equals `FoamReport.shape_counts` exactly (polyMesh stores no shapes, so a true 6-node prism is 2 tri + 3 quad faces and checkMesh must recognise it as a prism); points, faces, internal faces and patch count equal; `Max non-orthogonality` within 1e-3 degree and `Max skewness` within 1e-4 of our `mesh.quality` values (hexbox: both 0 and < 1e-6).
- meshio/VTK (`meshio`): `.exo` and `.vtu` read by meshio (cell counts per type, points within 1e-12 of the in-process mesh coordinates dumped by the test); `vtkExodusIIReader` and `vtkXMLUnstructuredGridReader` load them (VTK_POLYHEDRON count equals our poly cells); `netCDF4` structure check of the Exodus file (num_nodes, num_elem, num_el_blk, num_side_sets, `connect<i>`, side set element / side lists reconstruct the same face node sets as our sidesets under the Exodus side numbering).
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance):**
  - Gmsh comparison on tetbox (Delaunay, size 1). Gamma = 3 r_in / R_circ, which is 1/RadiusRatio in sm-quality when its DESIGN.md states the Verdict range >= 1. Pass when ours min gamma >= 0.8 x Gmsh min, mean gamma >= 0.95 x Gmsh mean, tet count ratio in [0.5, 2]. Thresholds are initial.
  - `polyDualMesh 180 -overwrite` on tetbox: total volume of both duals equals 600 within 1e-9; cell counts only reported (sm-poly keys dual cells by (vertex, region), polyDualMesh by point).
  - `SM_PVPYTHON` loads the same files in ParaView.
#### 4. Milestone (home: crates/sm-cli/tests/milestone_w3a.rs, written by the integration agent; same collect-all-rows style as milestone.rs)
**Part** (analytic, shared as `sm_testkit::w3a` constants): V1 L-slab (L polygon (0,0),(20,0),(20,10),(10,10),(10,20),(0,20), z in [0,4]) volume 1200; V2 right-triangle prism (10,10),(20,10),(10,20), z in [0,4], volume 200; V3 box [10,20]x[10,20]x[4,10], volume 600; total 2000. After imprint + merge: 3 volumes, 17 faces (8+5+7-3), 3 shared faces (V1|V2 two of area 40, V2|V3 the triangle of area 50), outer area 920+236.568542+440-260 = 1336.568542 (V2 hypotenuse face is 10*sqrt(2) x 4). V2's z=4 triangle is the sweep target and the V3 interface. V1's z=0 loop has 80 intervals at size 1 (even, so pave is legal); at size 2 it has 40.
- **4.1 Heads** (the test substitutes `@STEP@` with the absolute path of a temp file holding `sm_testkit::w3a::STEP`):
  - **Path A** `w3a_a.smesh`:
    ```
    let s = session.stage("@STEP@")
    let p = model.import_step(source: { sha256: s.sha256 })      // 3 solids: V1, V2, V3 in STEP-id order
    model.imprint(bodies: p.bodies)
    model.merge(entities: p.bodies)
    let v1 = p.volumes[0]   let v2 = p.volumes[1]   let v3 = p.volumes[2]
    ```
  - **Path B** `w3a_b.smesh` (bottom-up, mandatory; it exercises unite + regularize and a unite input that shares a merged face with a volume outside the op):
    ```
    let b  = model.create_brick(x: 20.0, y: 20.0, z: 4.0, center: [10.0, 10.0, 2.0])
    let c1 = model.webcut_plane(targets: b.body,       plane: { origin: [10.0, 0.0, 0.0],  normal: [1.0, 0.0, 0.0] })
    let c2 = model.webcut_plane(targets: c1.positive,  plane: { origin: [0.0, 10.0, 0.0],  normal: [0.0, 1.0, 0.0] })
    let c3 = model.webcut_plane(targets: c2.positive,  plane: { origin: [10.0, 20.0, 0.0], normal: [1.0, 1.0, 0.0] })
    model.delete(bodies: c3.positive)                              // the far triangle x+y>30
    let u  = model.unite(bodies: c1.negative | c2.negative)        // L [0,10]x[0,20] + [10,20]x[0,10]
    let b3 = model.create_brick(x: 10.0, y: 10.0, z: 6.0, center: [15.0, 15.0, 7.0])
    let all = u.bodies | c3.negative | b3.body
    model.imprint(bodies: all)
    model.merge(entities: all)
    let v1 = u.volumes   let v2 = topo.related(kind: "TopoVolume", within: c3.negative)   let v3 = b3.volume
    ```
  - Both heads end with assertions inside the script: `abs(model.measure(entity: v1).volume - 1200.0) < 1e-9`, likewise V2 200 and V3 600, so a STEP order surprise fails loudly.
- **4.1 Hybrid tail** (shared text; the test splices head + tail). Faces are chosen by geometry in the script itself:
  ```
  // source = the z=0 face, target = the z=4 face, found with model.measure(..).bbox (|z - c| < 1e-9 on lo and hi)
  for f in topo.related(kind: "TopoFace", within: v1) { ... set s1 = f ... set t1 = f ... }   // same for v2 -> s2, t2
  mesh.size(targets: v1 | v2 | v3, size: 1.0)
  mesh.scheme(targets: s1, scheme: "pave")
  mesh.scheme(targets: s2, scheme: "trimesh")
  mesh.scheme(targets: v1, scheme: "sweep", params: { source: [s1], target: [t1] })
  mesh.scheme(targets: v2, scheme: "sweep", params: { source: [s2], target: [t2] })   // t2 is the V2|V3 triangle, a copy of s2
  // every face of v3 except t2 gets trimesh; t2 takes its triangles from V2's sweep copy
  mesh.scheme(targets: f3, scheme: "trimesh")            // f3 = faces of v3 minus t2, built with a loop
  mesh.scheme(targets: v3, scheme: "tetmesh")
  let m = mesh.run(targets: v1 | v2 | v3)
  mesh.smooth(mesh: m.mesh[0], method: { kind: "laplacian" }, iterations: 5, fix_boundary: false)
  fem.block_add(block: 1, volumes: v1)   fem.block_element_type(block: 1, element_type: "HEX8")
  fem.block_add(block: 2, volumes: v2)   fem.block_element_type(block: 2, element_type: "WEDGE6")
  fem.block_add(block: 3, volumes: v3)   fem.block_element_type(block: 3, element_type: "TET4")
  fem.sideset_add(sideset: k, faces: topo.related(kind: "TopoFace", within: vk), wrt: vk)    // k = 1..3
  let hm = session.hash()
  session.export("out.exo")    session.export("out.smsh", provenance: true)    session.export("out.vtu")
  session.export("case", format: "openfoam")
  ```
  - The explicit `trimesh` on V3's faces follows decision 12; the planner is not changed. The interface triangle t2 is excluded because V2's sweep copy produces it. If the planner turns out to accept `trimesh` on t2 as well, the exclusion is dropped; otherwise the exclusion stays and is documented.
  - The `...` in the loops is the script text the agent writes against LANGUAGE.md (for / if / `set`). It must not need any op that does not exist.
- **4.2 Fork and tet variant** (`w3a_fork.smesh` and the tet branch of each path):
  - `let h0 = timeline.head().node` right after the head. After the hybrid tail, `hm` is recorded and `timeline.branch('alt', at: h0)` re-applies the tail and asserts equal hash. Branch 'coarse' (size 2.0) asserts a different hash. `timeline.checkout('main')` asserts equality.
  - **Branch 'tet'** (at `h0`, the part tet-meshed, which is the supported poly input):
    ```
    mesh.size(targets: v1 | v2 | v3, size: 1.0)
    mesh.scheme(targets: <all faces of v1, v2, v3>, scheme: "trimesh")
    mesh.scheme(targets: v1 | v2 | v3, scheme: "tetmesh")
    let mt = mesh.run(targets: v1 | v2 | v3)
    fem.block_add(block: 1, volumes: v1)   ... blocks 2, 3 ...     // no declared element types
    fem.sideset_add(sideset: k, faces: ..., wrt: vk)               // k = 1..3
    let pm = mesh.to_poly(mesh: mt.mesh[0], keep_source: true)
    let ht = session.hash()
    session.export("tet.exo", mesh: mt.mesh[0])   session.export("tetcase", format: "openfoam", mesh: mt.mesh[0])
    session.export("poly.exo", mesh: pm.mesh[0])   session.export("poly.vtu", mesh: pm.mesh[0])
    session.export("poly.smsh", mesh: pm.mesh[0])  session.export("polycase", format: "openfoam", mesh: pm.mesh[0])
    ```
  - The source mesh is kept, so both meshes are exported from one state (the export names the mesh, otherwise `ambiguous-mesh`). `timeline.branch('tet2', at: h0)` re-applies the branch and asserts the same hash.
  - Block element types are left undeclared in this branch: the exported meshes differ in shape. A second sub-branch declares `POLYHEDRON` on each block and asserts that exporting `pm` succeeds and exporting `mt` fails with the `element-mismatch` error.
- **4.3 CLI** (same pattern as W2 4.3):
  - `smesh new`; `smesh run w3a_{a,b}.smesh -p $T/{a,b} --author it --fixed-time ... --json`; `smesh hash -p` (cold replay from the project directory only); `smesh export $T/a/re.exo`, `smesh export $T/a/recase --format openfoam`; `smesh validate -p $T/a` (exit 0) and `smesh quality --profile transport --json`; `smesh stage` prints the sha256 equal to the pinned testkit constant.
  - `--memory` runs in their own cwd with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` and default; unpinned run; fork run; `smesh checkout alt && smesh hash`.
- **4.4 MCP** (`smesh mcp --memory`, pinned): the same ops as `tools/call`: `session_stage`, `model__import_step`, `model__webcut_plane`, `model__unite`, `model__imprint`, `model__merge`, `model__delete`, `topo__related`, `mesh__scheme`, `mesh__run`, `mesh__smooth`, `mesh__to_poly`, `fem__block_add`, `fem__block_element_type`, `fem__sideset_add`, `session_export` with `format:'exodus'|'openfoam'`, `mesh__validate`, `mesh__quality`, `session_hash`; `run_script` of w3a_fork in a fresh process.
- **4.5 In-process:**
  - `sm_server::standard().memory()` with the STEP bytes staged by `Server::stage_input`, then `apply_json` in tail order, gives `H_api`; replay via `Workspace::state_at` from the root; `reference_standard()` (CellComplex core) gives the same hash.
  - The same handle gives the final `State`, from which the test reads the meshes (`MeshLayer`, `as_read()`) for rows 6 to 13. Nothing is decoded back from `.smsh` into a `MeshRead`.
- **4.6 Rows** (each path A and B separately unless noted; the hybrid state and the tet branch are both covered):
  1. `H_cli == H_mem == H_1t == H_mcp == H_api` for the hybrid state and for the tet branch, plus equal per-layer hashes (`model`, `intent`, `meshes`, `fem`; no W3a layer is expected, and a new one is reported as a deviation).
  2. Fork: alt == main, coarse != main, checkout back bit-identical, tet2 == tet (script and Rust).
  3. Cold replay (`smesh hash -p` in a copied project directory) reproduces `H_cli`, including the staged STEP blob (path A); CLI and MCP node lists equal when pinned; unpinned: state hashes equal. The recorded `model.import_step` op carries `{sha256}` and no path.
  4. Export bytes: `out.exo`, `out.vtu`, `tet.exo`, `poly.exo`, `poly.vtu`, `poly.smsh` and every OpenFOAM file are byte-identical across CLI, MCP, re-export after replay and 1 vs N threads; `out.smsh` identical when pinned, else only the provenance differs. The `ExportResult.files` manifest sha256 equals the recomputed one.
  5. Part facts: 3 volumes, 17 faces, 3 shared faces; volumes 1200/200/600 within 1e-9; shared areas 40, 40, 50 within 1e-9. Paths A and B give the same counts (advisory note, not a failure, if mesh counts differ).
  6. Validity: `mesh.validate` returns `valid:true` for `m`, `mt` and `pm`, and the independent `sm_mesh_api::validate` + `validate_geometry` (default tolerances) on the in-process meshes is empty.
  7. Conformity and analytic relations (on `m`):
     - hex cells = 4 x quads on V1's source face; wedges = 4 x tris on V2's source face;
     - every tri face of the V2|V3 interface has one wedge and one tet;
     - no face has a single side except the outer surface;
     - sum of boundary face areas = 1336.568542 (1e-9); sum of cell volumes = 2000 (1e-9); per-block volumes 1200/200/600.
     - On `mt`: conformal across V1|V2|V3 (no `NonConformal` faces), same volumes.
  8. Quality and smoothing:
     - min scaled Jacobian >= 0.2 on hexes and >= 0.3 on wedges and tets (thresholds are initial, calibrated on the first run; tets are calibrated against tri's 20 degree surface guarantee).
     - Smoothing keeps validity, keeps min scaled Jacobian >= min(initial, guard_floor), leaves vertex-classified nodes bit-identical, keeps curve nodes on their lines and surface nodes on their planes within 1e-12 (`fix_boundary:false`), and keeps total volume 2000 within 1e-9.
     - `mesh.quality` (profiles generic and transport) returns a verdict, histograms whose counts add up, and a `smooth` suggestion with `available:true`. If sm-diag ships `mesh.flag_cells`: on a branch after smoothing, `mesh.flag_cells(name:'q_warn', level:'warn')` makes a set whose size equals `n_warn`, the state hash differs from main, and checkout restores it.
  9. Sidesets: the 3 side sets have areas 920, 236.568542, 440 (1e-9), each side on the correct block's cell.
  10. Exodus (via `read_exodus`):
      - `out.exo` has blocks HEX8, WEDGE6, TET4 with the mesh counts; nodes equal the mesh vertices; coordinates bit-equal to the in-process mesh; each side set entry reconstructs, under the Exodus side numbering, exactly the face node set of our sideset.
      - `poly.exo` has NFACED blocks whose counts equal the poly cells per block.
  11. OpenFOAM (in-test topology conformance, i.e. checkMesh's topological checks in Rust): `foam::check_polymesh` is empty for `case`, `tetcase` and `polycase`; the parsed back `PolyMesh` equals the built one; an independent re-check in the test (owner ascending, neighbour > owner, neighbours ascending per owner, each cell closed, patches cover [nInternal, nFaces) contiguously, face normals point owner -> neighbour) agrees. `polycase` cell count = pairs + wedges - split_cells, where pairs = sum over V1, V2, V3 of the number of tet-mesh vertices touching that volume (sm-poly keys dual cells by (vertex, region), so interface vertices count once per volume), and wedges / split_cells come from `mesh.poly_info` (a dual cell at a reflex interface edge that is not star-shaped is split into wedges; see the sm-poly W3_REPORT). Ruling INT-1.
  12. Poly (tet fork):
      - Sum of dual volumes = 2000 (1e-9) and per block 1200/200/600 (no dual cell mixes volumes).
      - `pm` carries `POLYHEDRON` cells and passes `validate` + `validate_geometry` with default tolerances.
      - `mesh.poly_info` matches `poly_stats`; the source `mt` stays valid and exportable (`keep_source:true`).
      - `poly.smsh` keeps polyhedra and its `SmshFile::decode` header lists `POLYHEDRON` element types and `mesh.stats` counts equal the census; `out.smsh` lists HEX8, WEDGE6, TET4.
  13. Tracking (in process, sm-rt VOLUME_MESH backend; no protocol field, no `.smsh` section):
      - Build the tracker from the final `TopoHost`, `Model` and the in-process meshes `m` and `pm`; trace 10,000 rays per mesh (seed 0x57A3, fixed LCG in the test) with `trace_bundle`.
      - Compare region sequence and chord lengths against a brute-force ray / face test over all faces classified on model faces (not interior mesh faces) within 1e-9.
      - Axis rays along z through V3 over x,y in [10,20] but outside V2's footprint give chord 6, through V1 give 4.
      - `TrackBundle::content_hash()` is identical for 1 vs N threads.
      - The `.smrt` sidecar round trip (`export_cache` -> `CacheImage::decode` -> restore) gives the same `structure_hash` and the same bundle hash; a cache with a changed key is `CacheStale`.
  14. Reference vs production host hash equality (`TopoStore` vs `CellComplex`), including the boolean ops and `join_faces` regularize.
  15. External conformance (section 3, mandatory part) is run once by hand on the final tree and its report is quoted in the wave report; it is not a cargo test.
- **Acceptance (the task):**
  1. D1-D6 merged with their own tests green and clippy clean.
  2. Stage-0 constants and the pinned STEP make rows 5 and 9 numeric.
  3. `cargo test -p sm-cli --test milestone_w3a` passes for both paths, with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` and default, rows 1-14.
  4. `SM_CONFORMANCE=1 tools/conformance.sh` exits 0 on the final tree (mandatory checks), and exits 0 with a skip message when the variable is unset.
  5. The W2 milestone and p0_rows still pass with unchanged hashes (auto lists unchanged), and the sm-protocol `catalog.json` golden is not touched by this task.
  6. `tools/ci.sh` unchanged and green.
  7. Every failure becomes a fix task routed to the owning crate, never a cross-crate patch.
  8. Wall time of the milestone and of each `mesh.run` is recorded in the wave report (record only, no gate: the bulk-commit performance items are W3b).
- **Stretch (after mandatory acceptance):**
  - Gmsh gamma comparison and `polyDualMesh` volume comparison in `tools/conformance.sh`; ParaView load.
  - `model.webcut_body` / `webcut_sheet` / `imprint curves` variants of path B if sm-boolean ships M3.
  - A STEP re-export round trip if sm-cadio ships its writer.
  - A `triadvance` run of the tet branch.
- **Deferred to W3b (stated so nothing is silently dropped):**
  - Auto scheme selection, `mesh.plan` query, surface-only `mesh.run`, script record.
  - PyO3 bindings, the web viewer.
  - DAGMC h5m and Gmsh msh4 export, Fluent export and its verification checklist.
  - The remaining PARITY P1 primitives, the STEP writer if it slips.
  - Remote staging of input files through the protocol (a `Request::Stage`); W3a stages in process only.
  - The backlog contract items (bulk mesh commit, plan once, firmness cascade, scheme-derived counts, overlay `MeshRead`), together with the performance gates that depend on them.
  - Partial dualization of a mixed mesh, ANALYTIC / HIERARCHY tracker backends, embedding the tracker cache in `.smsh`.
  - The sm-rt-capi crate and the DAGMC benchmark harness belong to the rt tasks, not to integration.
- **Must NOT:**
  - edit a crate outside D1-D6 (including their stated sm-lang / sm-mcp / sm-cli parts), the sm-cli tests, and `tools/conformance*`;
  - edit the frozen contract crates or sm-testkit;
  - add the conformance script to `tools/ci.sh`;
  - weaken a row to pass (loosen a threshold only with a note in the report);
  - tune an internal cell, the validator or a mesher to satisfy an external tool;
  - require docker or Python in `cargo test`;
  - add a path-taking op, an `ApplyCtx::blob` change, or a `tracking` field or section anywhere;
  - install an sm-rt plugin or touch sm-rt-capi;
  - add Fluent export;
  - register stub schemes together with the real ones.

model.import_step (sm-cadio CadioPlugin, StepInputs service injected by sm-server)
mesh.smooth (sm-smooth SmoothPlugin)
mesh.to_poly and mesh.poly_info (sm-poly PolyPlugin)
queries mesh.validate and mesh.quality, op mesh.flag_cells (sm-diag DiagPlugin)
schemes submap, pave, trimesh/tridelaunay/triadvance, sweep, tetmesh registered by sm_server::standard_schemes(); auto lists unchanged
fem element types POLYHEDRON and POLYGON (sm-fem)
session.stage (sm-lang), session_stage (sm-mcp), smesh stage/validate/quality (sm-cli)
export formats exodus and openfoam through sm_fem::export / export_files and the protocol ExportFormat; no tracking option

#### ACRs
[]

#### fixtures
sm_testkit::w3a (stage 0): VOLUMES 1200/200/600, TOTAL_VOLUME 2000, FACES 17, SHARED_FACE_AREAS 40/40/50, SIDESET_AREAS 920/236.568542494923802/440, OUTER_AREA 1336.568542494923802, STEP bytes of crates/sm-testkit/data/step/w3a_part.step (OCC-written, three solids V1, V2, V3), its sha256 pinned in a testkit test. No new testkit item is requested by this task.
Exporter and D1 tests use sm_mesh_api::reference::{mixed_transition, dual_patch} (no mixed_all_shapes).
tools/conformance/fixtures/*.smesh (hexbox, wedgeprism, tetbox, polydual): scripts owned by the integration agent; w3a_hybrid and w3a_tet are emitted by the milestone test.

#### depends
['sm-boolean: webcut_plane, imprint, merge, unite with regularize (stage-0 join_edges/join_faces)', 'sm-cadio: model.import_step + StepInputs', 'sm-scheme-pave, sm-scheme-tri, sm-scheme-sweep, sm-scheme-tet', 'sm-smooth: mesh.smooth', 'sm-poly: mesh.to_poly', 'sm-exodus: exodus_bytes + read_exodus', 'sm-meshio: foam::build_polymesh/PolyMesh::files/check_polymesh, .smsh 1.1 element_types', 'sm-quality FV delta + sm-diag: mesh.validate, mesh.quality, mesh.flag_cells', 'sm-rt over sm-rt-api: VOLUME_MESH tracker']

#### baseline
Conceptually similar to the W2 integration plan (docs/design/w2/plan.md sections 1.3 and 4) and to the cross-tool checks a Cubit-to-Exodus workflow gets by loading output in ParaView; it differs in that an exporter is judged by an external tool (checkMesh, meshio, VTK) while mesh validity is judged only by our own sm_mesh_api::validate and mesh.validate. Every failure has an owner: our validator failing is a mesher or model bug, an external tool failing after our validator passed is an exporter bug. The same analytic part is built two ways (content-addressed STEP import and bottom-up webcut + unite) and checked with identical hash and byte-equality rows across CLI, MCP, in-process, fork and 1 vs N threads, and the whole thing replays from the project directory alone (the STEP blob lives in inputs/), which neither Cubit nor Gmsh offers.

#### risks
Sibling names are now taken from the drafts, but ops and roles are still drafts until merged: the webcut positive/negative roles (decision 12 adds them to the boolean draft), the sm-rt-api tracker names and the exact `mesh.to_poly` signature. Only script templates and test adapters change.
Conformity of hex, wedge and tet: V2's sweep target is a copy of its tri source and V3 takes that triangle. The planner may reject `trimesh` on the copy-target face (hence the exclusion) or the sweep copy constraint may clash with tetmesh's TRI requirement; the fix then belongs to the mesher or sweep owner. The part has no hex-tet face (no pyramids): V3 touches only V2's triangular top.
Path B depends on `model.unite` with regularize (`join_faces`) and on a unite whose input shares a merged face with a volume outside the op; boolean must keep that case mandatory. Contingency if unite slips: path B drops to webcut + imprint + merge with V1 as two volumes and the part facts adjusted, reported as a deviation; path A is unaffected.
Pave source count is not analytic; the plan asserts only relations (cells = 4 x source facets) and areas. Thresholds in rows 8 and 13 are initial and must be calibrated on the first green run (tet quality against tri's 20 degree boundary guarantee).
sm-poly returns `UnsupportedFeature` for a vertex with exactly one split edge; the part's model vertices and curves may hit it. If so the fix belongs to sm-poly, not to the milestone.
Dual faces are split into planar pieces by sm-poly (`Planar` mode), so the face count of `pm` is not analytic; only volumes, validity and the cell count relation in row 11 are asserted.
The Exodus side tables and NFACED variable names come from memory in the exodus draft; netCDF4 and VTK in conformance are the external judge.
OpenFOAM image path to the bashrc, amd64-only image on Apple silicon, and checkMesh output formats may differ from the plan; the script discovers the bashrc and parses with tolerant regexes.
The sm-meshio draft still spells polyhedra `NFACED`/`NSIDED` in `.smsh` element_types; decision 12 says `POLYHEDRON`/`POLYGON`. Row 12 fails until meshio follows the decision.

#### open (answered by the rulings in §3.2)
1. Staging: decision 12 says CLI/MCP/lang 'stage a path and rewrite it to {sha256}'. This entry realizes it as an explicit step (`session.stage`, `session_stage`, `smesh stage`) that returns the sha256 for `source: {sha256}`, because the language does no I/O and the op schema has no `path`. If a literal `path` -> `{sha256}` rewrite sugar is wanted, it is added in D6's `SessionHost`; ratify.
2. Does the planner accept `mesh.scheme trimesh` on a face that is also a sweep copy target (the V2|V3 triangle)? The entry excludes it; confirm or drop the exclusion.
3. Is the `positive` / `negative` role content the piece bodies (assumed, so they feed `unite`, `delete`, `imprint`) rather than volumes? The boolean draft must state it.
4. Does the merged sm-poly dualize a whole conformal multi-volume all-tet mesh (region keyed) as the draft says, and which `keep_source` default ships (the entry always passes `keep_source: true`)?
5. Which `sm-rt-api` method names ship for tracing (`trace_bundle`, `TrackBundle::content_hash`, `export_cache`/`restore`)? Row 13 is written against the synthesis and adapts.
6. The sm-diag `smooth` suggestion must read the op registry (decision 12) so that `available:true` once `DiagPlugin` and `SmoothPlugin` are installed together; row 8 relies on it.
7. tools/conformance.sh is CI-adjacent: confirm the integration agent may add it (outside tools/ci.sh) despite the rule that CI belongs to the orchestrator.
8. Should an sm-rt `RtPlugin` (rt.* queries) be installed by the rt task owner in W3a, or is it W3b? D3 installs none.
9. Do deck face sets become OpenFOAM `symmetryPlane` patches (meshio assumes yes only when coplanar) and does the part's milestone need any deck? It has none; open only for later parts.

## 5. Order and risks

- **Order.** Stage 0 (done) → 14 cloud workers in parallel → Opus review per crate (fix loop) → merge in dependency-free order → D1-D6 wiring micro tasks → integration agent writes `crates/sm-cli/tests/milestone_w3a.rs` and `tools/conformance.sh` → parity critic → polish.
- **Critical path.** sm-boolean (regularize on quadrics) and sm-scheme-tet (boundary recovery without Steiner points on the boundary) are the two hardest tasks; the milestone needs both. Fallback for the milestone if booleans slip: the STEP-imported part (sm-cadio) gives the same three volumes.
- **Risks.** (1) tri's 20° bound versus tet's need to accept any valid surface: tet must not assume tri quality. (2) Exodus NFACED layout is unverified without SEACAS; it ships behind an experimental flag. (3) Perf numbers that depend on the W3b bulk commit are recorded only. (4) DAGMC numbers are recorded, never claimed. (5) Contract gaps found by workers come back as api_change_requests and are batched by the orchestrator after the wave; workers implement around nothing.
